- [x] **Orphan-scan false-positive noise** — meeting notes often phrase action items differently from their TODO.md counterparts (e.g. full paths vs basename, bold markers shifting word positions), so the 4-word key match fails and already-done items are flagged as orphans every meeting. Root cause: key4 is extracted after `**` stripping but before backtick/path simplification. Options: (a) strip backtick content before key extraction; (b) extract only the first non-punctuation token as a label (e.g. `N9b-1.` or `Sc3.`) and match that against TODO.md; (c) only scan meeting notes newer than the last `[x]`-closure date. **Closed 2026-05-14** — superseded by `docs/meeting-notes/2026-05-14-1803-orphan-scan-accumulation.md`. Root fix: union-read `TODO.md + TODO.archive.md` (D1) + SKILL.md Step 5b process discipline (D2). Options (a)/(b)/(c)/anchor-marker all withdrawn.

- [x] **Workflow profiler for the /relay loop — real numbers, no observation bias** (done 2026-06-15: `relay/scripts/profile-run.sh <runId|wf-id|wf-dir>` + `tests/test_profile_run.sh`; emits concurrency-over-time vs cap, a blocked-vs-`queued-behind-cap` round-boundary verdict, and per-phase/per-model aggregates) — user 2026-06-15: recurring "I think I saw a single Haiku being waited for before another discovery round" suspicion that can't be settled by eyeballing `/workflows`. Build a profiler that parses the run's on-disk journal (`…/subagents/workflows/wf_*/journal.jsonl` has per-agent `started`/`result` keyed by `agentId`) + each `agent-*.jsonl` (usage/timing) + `*.meta.json` into per-agent records: {label, model/tier, start, end, duration_ms, tokens_in/out, phase}. Emit: (a) a **concurrency-over-time** curve (how many agents live at each instant) against the harness cap `min(16, cores-2)`; (b) a **round-boundary analysis** — the gap between round N's last integration and round N+1's first `discover-prelude`, and exactly which agents (and models) occupy it; (c) per-label / per-phase aggregates (count, total/avg duration, total tokens). Tool: `relay/scripts/profile-run.sh <runId|wf-dir>`. **Settles the Haiku question with data**: post-cb50 there is no *logical* await of status writes between rounds (scheduleStatusWrite is fire-and-forget; `await statusTail` only at run END) — BUT a queued Haiku status-write can still occupy 1 of the ~6 cap slots and *delay the prelude's start under cap contention*, which LOOKS like "discovery waiting for a Haiku" without being a logic block. The profiler distinguishes "blocked-on" from "queued-behind-under-cap". Token usage falls out for free (cost-per-round, cost-per-verdict-class). <!-- id:a59e -->

- [x] **Cross-session lease LEAKS when a child dies without integrating (release is coupled to the integration path); + no heartbeat means long children can lose their lease mid-work** (done 2026-06-16, strong-execute) — fixes (a)+(b) landed: `runUnit` now calls `releaseLease(unit)` in a per-unit FINALLY path after the child settles with ANY outcome (merged/handback/null/error), run-scoped + idempotent vs. the integrator's retained step-0 release (defense-in-depth), and guarded against the review→execute re-chain steal window (`rechainedSameRepo`); claim.sh gained worktree-anchored liveness (a stale-mtime claim whose recorded `--worktree` still has commits beyond main is LIVE — the converse of id:3ac8 — so a >TTL long child isn't stolen mid-work) plus a `heartbeat` subcommand and a `worktree` shard field, and the work child now passes `--worktree` to acquire. (c) TTL shortening deferred — leave at 1800s now that liveness no longer depends solely on it. Tests: `tests/test_relay_claim_liveness.sh` (heartbeat + worktree-anchored staleness, hermetic git repos) and new id:7570 static checks in `tests/test_relay_loop_structure.sh`. Suite 59/0. — observed live 2026-06-16 (run relay-20260616-112222-29307, round 9): another session was blocked from writing mathematical-writing's TODO/ROADMAP because "a live relay run holds mathematical-writing (mode hard, since 13:18)" — but that hard child was DEAD: worktree `…/mathematical-writing/relay-…-29307-hard` had HEAD==main (0 commits) and zero file activity for 16 min, and there was no integrate/handback event for it. The lease (id:ebfb) stayed held by the ephemeral dead pid 1202111 until its 30-min TTL, needlessly deferring the other session. **Manually released** via `claim.sh release mathematical-writing --run relay-20260616-112222-29307`. (Likely cause of the child death itself: math-writing is Lean/Mathlib — memory-heavy — so an OOM-killed hard child is plausible; cf. [[oom-local-model-session-kills]] — but the LEAK is the bug here regardless of why the child died.) **Root cause (relay-loop.js):** (1) lease release lives INSIDE the integrate agent (L940 step 0); a child that returns null/errors/dies SKIPS integration (L1041/1045 catch → recoverable-handback path), so the release never runs → lease leaks for the full TTL. (2) The claim `pid` is the ephemeral shell that ran `claim.sh acquire`, NOT the agent — so it's useless as a liveness signal (always "dead" within seconds). (3) NO heartbeat: claim mtime is fixed at acquire time and TTL=1800s, so a LEGITIMATELY-long child (>30 min) would have its lease EXPIRE mid-work → another run grabs the repo → double-work/collision (the converse failure, worse than a leak). **Fixes:** (a) immediate — release the lease in a per-unit FINALLY path (after the child settles with ANY outcome: integrate, handback, null, error), not only inside integration; (b) tie claim liveness to the HELD WORKTREE (reuse the id:3ac8 worktree-staleness signal: a claim whose worktree HEAD==main with no recent mtime is stale) and/or HEARTBEAT the claim mtime while the child is alive, so both the leak AND the long-child-expiry directions become correct; (c) optionally shorten TTL once (a)+(b) land. Relates to id:ebfb (lease registry), id:0902 (interactive claim-aware), id:3ac8 (worktree-staleness reap — the analog liveness signal claims should share), id:0547 (same-repo race), id:719e (reliability/waste). <!-- id:7570 -->

The script read a non-existent file and exited 0 with NO output — the worst failure mode: `/relay human` looked like "no human backlog" when there were **52 open items** (10 REVIEW_ME + 42 gated-HARD). Ran fine once pointed at the real path via `RELAY_TOML=...` override. **Root cause = partial/stalled id:10c0 migration:** `~/.config/relay/` already holds `claims`, `claims.done`, `inject.d`, `inject.done`, `relay-events.jsonl`, `RELAY_STATUS.md`, `fable-probe.json` (so claim.sh/inject.sh/probe-fable.sh/events all use the NEW path), but `relay.toml` was left at the OLD path, and BOTH dirs are REAL directories (neither is a symlink) — `RELAY_STATUS.md` exists in both with different mtimes (live runs split: run 130157 wrote `fables-turn`, run 132749 wrote `relay`). This is precisely the inconsistency id:10c0's design (symlink-old→new + idle guard, `relay/scripts/migrate-state-dirs.sh`) was meant to avoid — it appears a pool hard-execute child began the move (state dirs + script path-defaults flipped to `~/.config/relay/`) without (a) moving `relay.toml` and (b) leaving the back-compat symlink, so the two dirs diverged. **Fix (sequenced):** (1) IMMEDIATE unblock — either move/symlink `relay.toml` to `~/.config/relay/relay.toml` OR have every relay.toml reader fall back old↔new when one path is absent (back-compat read, the safe minimal change); (2) DURABLE — run id:10c0's `migrate-state-dirs.sh` to completion under its idle precondition: reconcile the two divergent dirs (newest-wins per file, esp. the two `RELAY_STATUS.md`), move `relay.toml`, and replace the OLD dir with a permanent symlink→new (the design's safety net), so no reader can split-brain again. **DO NOT hand-mutate state dirs while a pool is live.** Until fixed, any new-path-default relay.toml reader (gather-human-backlog.sh at minimum; audit discover-repos.sh and others) is silently broken. Subsumes/blocks the clean completion of [[id:10c0]]; relates to id:c8b6 (events path), id:15bd (statusline RELAY_STATUS path). **RESOLVED 2026-06-17 (TDD):** the existing `relay/scripts/migrate-state-dirs.sh` was buggy (skipped name-collisions then bailed without symlinking — the half-done cause) AND was never symlinked into `~/.claude` nor run. Rewrote it to RECONCILE collisions (merge `*.jsonl` as a deduped union — no log lost; union dirs; newest-mtime wins for snapshots; drop `*.lock`), honor env overrides for hermetic tests, and exit 3 on the idle-guard refusal. Drove it with `tests/test_migrate_state_dirs.sh` (9 cases, `# roadmap:bbd2`), ran it for real (pool idle): both `~/.{config,cache}/fables-turn` are now symlinks→`relay`, `relay.toml` at the new path, `relay-events.jsonl` merged 347+12→358 (no loss), `gather-human-backlog.sh` returns 48 items on its DEFAULT path (was 0). `make install-relay` linked the script. Full suite 66/66 green. id:10c0 now genuinely complete. <!-- id:bbd2 -->

- [x] **Integrator can DESTROY uncommitted edits in a repo's main checkout (data loss)** — fixed by relay 2026-06-18 (clean-tree-gate.sh + integrate-step-1 wiring + git-lock-push autostash refusal; test_integrator_foreign_dirty.sh green). observed 3× on 2026-06-18: while the pool integrated dotclaude-skills, a concurrent uncommitted edit to `relay/scripts/relay-loop.js` vanished (reflog `reset: moving to HEAD`, empty stash list = real loss). - [x] **Discovery shards flailing on the filesystem — no-hunting guard** — fixed 2026-06-18. - [x] **Handbacks must DIRECTLY create durable follow-up TODOs (auto-split `[HARD]` seams OR `[MEETING/HUMAN]`) + gate the parent against re-dispatch** — user 2026-06-17. Today a size-out / gated / blocked handback returns a rich REPORT (the zkWhale id:f14f handback even enumerated a clean 3-seam split: signPsbt+OP_RETURN PSBT builder / amount-nonce poll+localStorage / funnel UI state-machine; the dotclaude-skills id:dba3 handback identified the OS-user+meeting gate id:d0c0) — but that judgment **evaporates** into RELAY_STATUS for one run and nothing durable is created. WANTED: on a handback, the integrator (or a cheap follow-up agent) DURABLY records adequate follow-up items in the repo's TODO/ROADMAP under the **same id-ecosystem** (single-id-two-views), choosing per the handback's own classification: **(i) too-large-but-decomposable** → mint child `[HARD — strong model]` seam items from the child's recommended split (each executor/strong-sized) + leave the parent OPEN; **(ii) decision-gated / needs-design** → mint or re-tag a `[HARD — decision gate]` / `[MEETING]` / `[HUMAN]` item so a `/meeting` or `/relay human` picks it up; **(iii)** in BOTH cases ADD THE GATE so the pool stops re-dispatching the parent every round. **Gate partially exists already** (id:2d20: the classifier's EXECUTABLE-HARD test excludes `[HARD — decision gate]` / Gated-section items and surfaces them; id:2d20 option (c) — durable auto-re-tag of a freshly-handed-back HARD to `[HARD — decision gate]` — is the OPEN durable half). This item GENERALIZES id:2d20(c): not just re-tag-to-gate, but **create the right follow-up work** (split-into-seams vs route-to-meeting/human) so the backlog moves forward instead of stalling. **Design forks (likely a `/meeting`, heavy overlap with id:719e/id:2d20/id:194e):** (a) who authors the split — trust the handing-back child's one-shot proposed-seams (add a `proposed_split: [...]` / `route: hard-split|meeting|human` field to the REPORT schema) vs a dedicated splitter agent re-reading the item; (b) where the items are written + dedup so a re-run doesn't mint duplicate seams (idempotency key on parent-id + seam-title; reuse existing ids per single-id-two-views, never mint a duplicate for already-tracked work); (c) routing heuristic hard-split vs meeting/human (size + decision-gated + dependency-blocked signals); (d) how the parent's gate is invalidated when the seams are done / the meeting clears it; (e) trusting a one-shot "too-large" verdict enough to spawn durable items (anti-gaming: the next review re-checks). Builds on id:2d20 (drain + classifier exclusion), feeds id:719e (DMAIC: handback rework→durable forward progress), uses id:194e (failure-reason category as the routing signal) + id:c8b6 (event log). Concrete first targets to validate against: zkWhale id:f14f (3-seam split), dotclaude-skills id:dba3 (gate on id:d0c0); leAIrn2learn id:d61a (4th identical handback 2026-06-19 — manually re-gated `[ROUTINE]`→`[HARD — decision gate]` by hand with the a/b decision spelled out, exactly the case-(ii) auto-gate this item should perform so the pool stops re-dispatching it; the by-hand fix is the proof the automation is overdue). **SHIPPED 2026-06-19** — `relay/scripts/handback-followup.py` (durable auto-gate / auto-split of a handed-back item: decision-gate/human → re-tag parent to `[HARD — decision gate]`; hard-split → gate parent + mint the child's `proposed_split` seams as pickable units; idempotent by id + title-dedup; flock'd `md-merge.py` write + `--ff-only` commit/push) + REPORT-schema fields `route`/`handback_item`/`gate_reason`/`proposed_split` (relay-loop.js + handoff.md/review.md contracts) + fire-and-forget integrator wiring (`durableHandbackFollowup`, non-fatal, Haiku agent — the sandbox can't shell). Covered by `tests/test_handback_followup.sh` (7 cases incl. both idempotency paths + manual-gate respect); suite 76/0, `node --check` clean. Live end-to-end validates on the next pool handback. Deferred refinements (low-pri follow-ups): (d) auto-close the parent when all seams tick; richer `human` route → mint a REVIEW_ME box rather than gating. <!-- id:3801 -->

- [x] **Auto-handle uv.lock drift in the relay pool** (user request 2026-06-12; RESOLVED 2026-06-22) — pilot run relay-20260612-2304: a parent-zkm version bump (0.14.0) left ` M uv.lock` in plugin checkouts, blocking 2 integrations (zkm-calendar, zkm-eml) + 1 dispatch (zkm-scan); resolution was mechanical (`git add uv.lock && commit "chore: refresh uv.lock"`). Levers, combine as needed: (a) integrator prompt: if porcelain shows ONLY `uv.lock` modified, auto-commit `chore: refresh uv.lock — dependency re-resolution` before the merge instead of aborting; (b) discovery: same uv.lock-only exemption so the repo still dispatches (child regenerates in its worktree); (c) prevention: executor conventions.md mandates `uv run --frozen` / `uv sync --locked` so test runs can't silently mutate the lock — drift then only enters via deliberate dep changes; (d) zkm release flow: bump plugin lockfiles as part of the parent version-bump commit. Guard: the exemption must match uv.lock ONLY — any other dirty path still aborts. **RESOLVED 2026-06-22 (user "fix this once and for all"):** root cause was the *parent zkm's own* uv.lock self-pin going stale on bump (0.15.0 while pyproject=0.16.0) cascading into 15 plugin locks scattered across 0.14/0.15/0.16 (some committed-stale, latent landmines). Fixes shipped: **(d) generator** — `zkm/scripts/relock-plugins.sh` (relock every plugin + `--check`/`--push`/`--install-hook`) + `zkm/scripts/hooks/pre-push` guard that REFUSES a version-bump push while the parent or any plugin lock is stale/uncommitted (tested: blocks stale-bump, allows non-bump + in-sync); all 15 plugins + parent relocked/committed/pushed. **(a)+(b) relay tolerance** — `gather-repo-state.sh` now emits `lock_only_unaudited` + `dirty_lock_only` booleans (conservative: only the unambiguous root `uv.lock`; any other path defeats them; `test_gather_repo_state.sh` cases 9a–9d), and `relay-loop.js` discovery exempts them: a uv.lock-only unaudited commit is audit-exempt (no review), a uv.lock-only dirty tree is committed-in-place then classified (no block, no worktree-merge hazard, no thrash). **(c) `uv ... --frozen` executor mandate was NOT taken** (user did not select it) — optional future hardening; drift can still enter via a deliberate dep change, which is correctly reviewable. <!-- id:bae5 -->
- [x] **`/meeting` deferred ledger write-back: breadcrumb + replay-on-next-invocation + log** — when step 2a's relay-pool claim is refused, the meeting currently DEFERS the `<root>` ledger write-back to a manual `/todo-update` re-apply (decisions stay durable in the note). Meeting 2026-06-22 (af04) ruled this is step 2a *working as designed* (no data loss) — the only gap is that the deferral never auto-completes. Fix: on a refused claim, persist a **generic** `{target_file, helper, payload}` JSON to a gitignored drop path (`<root>/.meeting-deferred-writeback.json`) AND append an event to `~/.claude/logs/meeting-deferred-writeback.log`; add a setup-phase replay check (in `/meeting` setup + `/todo-update`) that applies any pending payload via the named helper (`md-merge.py`/`append.sh`) under a fresh `claim.sh acquire`, then clears the drop file. Payload format generic so it extends if another defer site appears, but WIRED IN only at step 2a (the sole site that defers today — `~/.claude` shared files are flock-safe, no pool there). Test contract: a deferred write-back leaves a replayable payload; the next invocation applies it under a fresh claim and removes the drop file; nothing is applied while the pool still holds the claim. Add `.meeting-deferred-writeback.json` to `.gitignore`. **Also:** add the af04 motivation as a cross-link note on id:3558 and record that the literal worktree-per-meeting option was REJECTED by this meeting (inherits id:ca87's non-unionable-checkbox merge problem; contradicts id:ca87 `/meeting`=same-dir→flock + worktrees-are-code-only). See `docs/meeting-notes/2026-06-22-2139-meeting-worktree-writeback.md`. <!-- id:2c42 -->
- [x] **Margin-aware quota-stop staleness** (incident 2026-06-23 run relay-20260623-070136) — the `--afk` pool false-stopped (`stopReason=quota-stale-cache`) at 7%/38%/25% util vs a 90% cap because `quota-stop.sh`'s staleness fail-safe does an unconditional `exit 2` when the cache is stale + the self-refresh 429s, ignoring the (healthy) last-known reading. Same false-stop family as the `RELAY_QUOTA_DECAY_7D` warning. Optional follow-up: respect `/tmp/claude-usage-backoff` + reuse the statusline cache. <!-- id:1d64 -->
- [x] **`--afk` pool reports an INTENSIVE-only repo as "idle — no open work" instead of dispatching the INTENSIVE unit** (observed 2026-06-23 `/relay --afk`, run relay-20260623-112409) — ai-codebench has an open `[INTENSIVE — local-llm]` item (id:244b, ~15 model runs pending) and the run set `allowIntensive=true`, yet the discovery shard classified the repo `idle — in sync, no open work` and never emitted an intensive-tagged unit, so `relay-loop.js`'s INTENSIVE partition (L877, `ALLOW_INTENSIVE ? intensiveUnits : intensiveDeferred`) never got a unit to dispatch. **Not a gather bug:** `gather-repo-state.sh --repo ai-codebench` correctly emits `is_finished=false`, the open `[INTENSIVE]` box, and id:244b (verified this session). **Root cause = shard JUDGMENT:** the shard never sees the `--afk`/`allowIntensive` flag (it's a JS-level decision), and the repo's own ROADMAP convention prose says *"a session finding only `[INTENSIVE]` items open reports 'no executor-eligible work' and stops"* — written for generic/remote executors with no GPU. The shard reads that prose literally and reports the repo idle, short-circuiting the intended design (always emit an intensive unit; let the loop decide dispatch by `ALLOW_INTENSIVE`). **Fix direction:** make the discover-shard prompt treat an open `[INTENSIVE — <res>]` item as WORK unconditionally — emit a unit with `intensive` set and NEVER report idle for it — leaving the auto-run-vs-defer decision to `relay-loop.js` (it already surfaces deferred intensive units as skipped when `!ALLOW_INTENSIVE`). The repo-local "reports no executor-eligible work" convention must not override the shard's emit. Add a shard-canary case: INTENSIVE-only open repo → classified as an intensive unit, not idle. Distinct from id:000d (false-dispatch on FINISHED repos — the inverse) and a natural check to fold into the relay-health report id:0907. Directly blocked the user's overnight ai-codebench drain (id:244b/8bea). **Promoted to ROADMAP [ROUTINE] (review Run 47, single-id-two-views) with acceptance + red spec `tests/test_relay_loop_intensive_emit.sh`.** <!-- id:ad74 -->
- [x] **Recurring `[HARD — pool]` audit (id:401c) spins forever when it's the only open item — vacuous self-audit loop burns Opus** — DONE 2026-06-23 (both mechanisms shipped, suite 91/0/0): gather `substantive_unaudited`+`work_sig` primitive (ignores the pool's own `relay: checkpoint` commits) + mechanism 1 shard recurring-audit gate (id:401c marked `relay:recurring-audit`, idled when nothing new to audit) + mechanism 2 deterministic `redispatchGuard` circuit breaker (suppress any non-injected (repo,verdict) dispatched >3× with unchanged work_sig; injected exempt). (observed 2026-06-23 `/relay --afk`, run relay-20260623-172446) — once every genuine `[ROUTINE]`/`[HARD — pool]` item in dotclaude-skills had drained, **id:401c (the RECURRING strong-model audit, which never closes by design) became the only open pool-dispatchable item**, so the `hard` verdict (id:da26) re-selected it EVERY round. Each round audited "commits since last checkpoint" — but the only commit since the prior round WAS that round's own `relay: checkpoint …` commit, so every audit reported *"LEDGER-ONLY clean by vacuity — no findings"* and wrote a fresh checkpoint, which the next round then audited. **~13 consecutive vacuous rounds (audit Run 55→67, ~20:01–21:47, ~9 min each) drove the 5h quota bucket 98%→41% for ZERO output**, and each checkpoint commit changed HEAD → invalidated the discover-sig cache (id:c3a6) → forced a re-classify, so the cache couldn't damp it. **Root cause:** a never-closing recurring HARD item + no guard for "the audit window contains only the pool's own checkpoint commits." **Fix directions:** (a) the `hard` scheduler (or the 401c audit step) must treat an audit window of ONLY relay-checkpoint commits (no real/new code-or-ledger commits) as **nothing-to-audit → idle/skip**, not a dispatch (mirrors the id:000d is_finished demote-guard, generalized to "no NEW auditable work"); (b) a per-item **cooldown / min-interval** on recurring HARD items (e.g. don't re-fire id:401c more than once per N hours or until ≥1 non-ckpt commit lands); (c) make a recurring audit's "open" state CONDITIONAL — it's only dispatchable when there are unaudited *substantive* commits since `last_review`/`last_strong_ckpt`, else it sits dormant. Without this, ANY repo that drains to "only the recurring audit open" will silently spin and burn the apex tier. Caught only because the user asked "stuck or catching up?"; a natural check to fold into the relay-health report (id:0907) AND a strong argument for the exit-reason/idle-detection work (id:8602). Relates id:401c (the recurring audit), id:da26 (`hard` verdict steady-state), id:000d (finished-repo demote guard — the analogue), id:c3a6 (sig cache, defeated by the ckpt-commit churn). <!-- id:365b -->
- [x] **DONE 2026-06-23 (shipped + tested) — First-class per-run priority + exclusion args for the autonomous pool — `--priority <repo[s]>` / `--exclude <repo[s]>` without mutating relay.toml** — `relay-loop.js` reads `args.priorityRepos`/`args.excludeRepos`: EXCLUDE drops repos from the own list before sharding (skipped `excluded for this run (--exclude)`, no relay.toml write); PRIORITY is a per-run within-class ordering bump (above income, below injected + D3 verdict class), reorders the repo's natural unit only (no double-dispatch — the finding). Unknown names LOUD-rejected/surfaced. Logic in pure helper `relay/scripts/pool-args.mjs` (byte-equiv inline copies pinned by `tests/test_relay_pool_args.sh`); SKILL.md front door documents both run-scoped flags. ROADMAP id:d530. — observed 2026-06-23 (`/relay --afk priority on zkm-stt, exclude codebench-ai`): the front door has NO arg for either, so both had to be hand-worked: priority via `inject.sh add zkm-stt` (the raw script — abuses the user-injection mechanism id:baf1, which is really "do this NEXT/once", not "this repo ranks higher all run"), and exclusion via a **temporary `classification = "own"->"excluded"` edit to relay.toml** that must be manually reverted after the run (a destructive mutation of the sticky registry — survives a session-kill mid-run = silent permanent exclusion). Neither is a clean per-invocation knob. **Goal:** `/relay [--afk] --priority <repo|repo,repo> --exclude <repo|repo,repo>` (also accept the natural-language forms the user typed) parsed by the front door and passed as `args.priorityRepos` / `args.excludeRepos` into `relay-loop.js`, scoped to THIS run only, registry untouched. Semantics to design: **priority** = bump those repos to the front of slot contention WITHIN their verdict class (a per-run tier above `income`, but still under the D3 verdict-class order + injected-unit precedence — NOT a verdict override), distinct from `inject` (which forces a one-shot unit); **exclude** = the discovery shards simply never emit a unit for those repos (surface them in `RELAY_STATUS.md` Skipped as "excluded for this run (--exclude)"), no relay.toml write. Reject unknown/unconfirmed repo names LOUDLY (id:415b grammar). **Observed 2026-06-23 (same session):** approximating priority via `inject.sh add zkm-stt` caused a REDUNDANT DOUBLE-DISPATCH — the injected unit ran ahead AND normal discovery still emitted its own zkm-stt execute unit the same round (events log: two `dispatch repo=zkm-stt mode=execute round=1`; the 2nd integrate logged "already pushed (integration completed by previous relay run); worktree already cleaned"). Concrete proof that priority must be an ORDERING bump within the verdict class, NOT a separate injected unit (which `inject` is) — `--priority` must dedupe against / merge with the repo's naturally-discovered unit, never add a second one for the same repo. Relates id:354f (`/relay inject` — the one-shot cousin), id:1ffc (richer income ranking — priority could read the same weight), id:a02c (uniform scope-arg grammar — `--exclude` is the inverse of a repo-list scope), id:11c6 (front-door arg parsing). Natural fold into the scope-arg-grammar work. <!-- id:d530 -->

- [x] **`orphan-scan.sh --cross-ledger` false-positive: reports drift when the `<!-- id:XXXX -->` token lines AGREE** — found 2026-06-24 by dogfooding `relay-doctor.sh --all`. CONFIRMED repro (isochrone): `orphan-scan.sh --cross-ledger` reports `id:f4a7 — TODO:[x] ROADMAP:[ ]`, but the actual `<!-- id:f4a7 -->` token line is `- [ ]` in BOTH files and NO `[x]` line anywhere contains the string `f4a7` (the id is also mentioned in prose on 3 sibling child lines `f4a7-A/B/C`, all `[ ]`). Control: `id:601e` in the same repo is reported correctly (genuinely `[ ]` TODO / `[x]` ROADMAP). So the check mis-attributes a checkbox NOT belonging to the id's own token line — likely a substring / multi-mention / adjacent-id confusion in how it pairs the id to a checkbox (it must correlate STRICTLY on the single line bearing `<!-- id:XXXX -->`, exact-token, not a prose/substring match). **Why it matters:** this is the interim TODO↔ROADMAP drift backstop that id:2840 leans on AND a relay-doctor input (id:9bec) — a noisy mix of real + phantom drift erodes trust in both. **Acceptance:** a hermetic test with (a) an id whose token lines agree but whose id is mentioned in prose on other lines → reported CLEAN; (b) a genuine token-line disagreement → reported DRIFT; orphan-scan correlates only the `<!-- id:XXXX -->`-bearing line's checkbox. [ROUTINE] — done 2026-06-24 (fix shipped d2a9d98 + hermetic test; TODO twin ticked by review) <!-- id:9221 -->

- [x] **Un-promoted TODO backlog is invisible to relay routing → repos falsely read as "drained"** [ROUTINE] — DONE 2026-06-25 (ROADMAP id:2dea): `relay/scripts/unpromoted-scan.sh` (lane-tag-agnostic; `promote`/`surface`/`untracked` dispositions, the last catching id-less open checkboxes like truncocraft's favicon) wired into `relay-doctor.sh` (escalates to "needs HANDOFF" only when the ROADMAP is genuinely DRAINED — otherwise informational, so a design-ledger repo isn't flagged) + the `/relay next` route-2a pre-check + handoff.md C2 ("ROADMAP closed ≠ nothing to hand off"); `tests/test_unpromoted_scan.sh` + full suite green. — LIVE evidence 2026-06-25 (truncocraft): `/relay next`/`review`/handoff gauge "is there work?" from OPEN ROADMAP items + unaudited commits ONLY, never from open `TODO.md` items lacking a ROADMAP twin. truncocraft's ROADMAP was fully `[x]`-closed while TODO held 5 open executable items (`b1e4`/`a7d2`/`c3f7`/`a03c` + favicon) — so prior `/relay` runs (and an apex turn) reported the repo DRAINED and the un-promoted backlog sat idle for days, even though promoting TODO→ROADMAP is exactly handoff C2's job. **Second instance** (first: id:78ff, TODO-only HARD-pool the pool couldn't see). The existing d9b0 promotion-tracking note (ROADMAP ~142.1) only catches TODO items *already lane-tagged* `[ROUTINE]`/`[HARD]` — truncocraft's were UNTAGGED raw backlog, so they slip past it; d9b0 was ticked `[x]` but this never shipped as a routing-visible signal. **Build (ROADMAP id:2dea, single-id-two-views):** a mechanical scan listing open TODO ids with no ROADMAP twin (lane-tag-agnostic), wired into `/relay next`/`review`/`relay-doctor.sh` so a closed-ROADMAP/open-TODO repo surfaces "N un-promoted — needs handoff" and `/relay next` routes to handoff, never "drained." Ambiguous-lane items SURFACE for strong-turn triage, never auto-promote. Relates id:2840 (durable home), id:3947 (cross-repo dead-letter sibling), id:9bec (relay-doctor). **Filed as a ROADMAP item, not TODO-only — that IS the lesson.** <!-- id:2dea -->
- [x] **META-lesson (this session): an apex turn judged "handoff = no-op" from ROADMAP closure alone** — DONE 2026-06-25: encoded in `relay/references/handoff.md` C2 (first check is the open TODO backlog via `unpromoted-scan.sh`, not the ROADMAP open-count) and the `/relay next` route-2a ladder. — 2026-06-25, before id:2dea was filed: asked to run handoff+review on truncocraft, the strong turn read the fully-`[x]` ROADMAP and concluded "both modes find an empty repo," missing the 5 open TODO items handoff exists to promote. - [x] **Flaky test: `tests/test_relay_claim_liveness.sh` (roadmap:7570) intermittently red under suite load** — surfaced by the id:401c Run 14 audit 2026-06-17: a full `tests/run-tests.sh` showed `65 passed, 1 failed` on this file, then two reruns were clean 66/66; it passes in isolation. Timing/mtime-sensitive (worktree-mtime heartbeat liveness, id:7570) — non-deterministic under load. NOT a feature defect; the test itself is racy. Fix: widen the mtime/TTL margins or stub the clock so the suite is deterministically green. Until then a lone red here is likely a flake — rerun before trusting. Unrelated to id:bbd2/id:10c0. **DONE 2026-06-29**: replaced `$((now-mt)) -ge 2` clock-comparison with before/after mtime snapshot (`mt_before` == `mt_after`); added explicit git-repo verification assertions at setup. Timing dependency structurally removed. 20× consecutive + full-suite green. <!-- id:16e9 -->
- [x] **Flaky test: `tests/test_git_lock_push_slash_branch.sh` intermittently red under suite load** — surfaced by the id:401c Run 15 audit 2026-06-19: a full `tests/run-tests.sh` showed `75 passed, 1 failed` on this file, then it passed in isolation AND on an immediate full-suite rerun (76/0). Same flake CLASS as id:16e9 (real-git fetch/push timing under parallel suite load), NOT new `/tmp` contention from the id:0d31 quota sampler — both `test_quota_sample.sh` and this test isolate their state via `mktemp -d` (+ `QUOTA_CACHE` override). NOT a feature defect. Fix: make the slash-branch test's git fetch/push deterministic (local-only remote / serialize) so the suite is green regardless of run order. Until then a lone red here is likely a flake — rerun before trusting. **DONE 2026-06-29**: root cause was `git-lock-push.sh`'s `ssh-add -l` guard — if no SSH key loaded in agent (CI, expired agent), the script exits 0 without pushing, causing the push assertion to fail. Fixed by injecting a fake `ssh-add` (always exits 0) into the test's PATH; the local file:// remote needs no SSH. Timing dependency structurally removed. 20× consecutive + full-suite (123/0) green. <!-- id:05e8 -->
- [x] **Gate `claim.sh` `is_live` worktree-anchor on the run heartbeat (dead-but-LIVE claim fix)** — meeting 2026-06-29 (`docs/meeting-notes/2026-06-29-1750-dead-but-live-claim-heartbeat-gate.md`, the id:9000 remainder after id:672b). `is_live`'s id:7570 `worktree_working` clause keeps a stale-mtime claim alive whenever its worktree has commits beyond main — but that never detects death, so a dead-but-committed run holds its claim forever. Fix (D1): gate the worktree clause on `heartbeat.sh status <runId>` (id:e149, the pure-staleness run-liveness oracle that exists for exactly this); dead/stale-heartbeat run → reclaimable. (D2) absent-heartbeat → ordinary mtime-TTL fallback (reuse heartbeat's TTL, no new knob). (D3) reclaim frees only the reservation — the orphan worktree is disposed by the existing reconcile (id:a4e9/7809), never deleted (reversible). Test: extend `tests/test_relay_claim_liveness.sh` with stubbed-ts cases (fresh-hb+worktree→live; stale-hb+worktree→reclaimable; absent-hb→mtime-TTL; mtime-fresh→live). Was gated on id:16e9 (now landed). See memory [[relay-claim-liveness-heartbeat-gate]]. <!-- id:33d3 -->

Closes the one-sided-lease asymmetry ([[meeting-pool-claim-asymmetry-incident]]). On an EXISTING pool claim at setup, /meeting **informs** the user and **offers a non-conflicting session**: the decide phase needs no claim, so design proceeds; only the ledger **write-back** is **deferred + replayed** (id:2c42) — explicitly NOT worktree-per-meeting (rejected, af04: non-unionable checkboxes). - [x] **`ckpt-tag.sh` hard-fails (exit 1) when a repo can't track `.gitattributes`** — observed 2026-06-30 reviewing `kienzler-homepage`, whose `.gitignore` had a `.*` dotfile catch-all that swallowed `.gitattributes`, so ckpt-tag's `git add -- RELAY_LOG.md .gitattributes` aborted the WHOLE checkpoint (no commit, no tag) and left `RELAY_LOG.md` staged. The `RELAY_LOG.md merge=union` attribute is a nicety (matters only for parallel relay merges), not essential — a repo that can't add it should still get its checkpoint. **Fix:** ckpt-tag.sh should degrade gracefully — try to stage `.gitattributes`, but on failure WARN + proceed with `RELAY_LOG.md` + tag only (don't abort). Workaround used 2026-06-30: manual `git commit RELAY_LOG.md` + `git tag -a`, and fixed kienzler's `.gitignore` (negated `!.gitattributes`/`!.dns/`). <!-- id:a7a3 -->

The relay's own pattern is already correct (`relay-loop.js` has the CHILD create an explicit `~/.cache/relay/worktrees/<repo>/<runId>-<verdict>` worktree naming the target); the gap was a manual-dispatch footgun. **Fix shipped:** `relay/SKILL.md` orchestrator-invariant step 4 now explicitly forbids `isolation: worktree` for relay children and points at the explicit-relay-worktree / main-checkout-under-lease patterns. ## 2026-07
- [x] **Relay pool quota gate false-stops on round 1 (82e3)** — FIXED + VERIFIED 2026-07-01 (commits f6a2994, aa1828c; suite 146 green; gate reproduction wf_59dd6986 returned exitCode=0, ran=true, denied=false). Three causes, all resolved: (A) `quota-stop.sh` self-refresh read the OAuth token + curled the usage endpoint on a stale cache — inside the auto-mode pool that credential-egress shape is DENIED, the quota-check agent dies, and `relay-loop.js:1381` labels agent-death `quota-cache-unreadable`. Removed ALL credential logic; the foreground statusline is the sole token owner; a stale cache resolves via the stale-but-safe margin check → burn-rate extrapolation (no network/credential). (B) `--once`/`--after` round cap (`relay-loop.js:1871`) unconditionally set `stopReason='user-stop'`, masking a real quota stop from the same round → now `if (!stopReason)`. (C) the usage API stopped exposing per-model weekly sub-limits on 2026-06-30 (`seven_day_sonnet` et al. null; only `weekly_all`/`seven_day` remains — evidence: quota sampler series ~17h/34 samples), so `check_key seven_day_sonnet` mislabeled the absence as an unreadable cache → sonnet tier now falls back to `seven_day`+`five_hour` (fail-safe only if `seven_day` itself is absent). CORRECTED PREMISE (verified prior session): `/tmp` is SHARED, not a private sandbox — the cache is visible+fresh in the pool; the cause was credential denial + the null sub-bucket, never namespace invisibility. FOLLOW-ON: the classifier-flip gate needs a full UNATTENDED autonomous pool run to accrue (bare `/relay`) — the round-1 gate is now verified passing, so that run is unblocked. (from zkm-whatsapp via relay, routed:82e3) <!-- id:0175 -->

(meeting 2026-06-30-1523) **SCOPING FINDING 2026-06-30 (read `relay-loop.js:818-891` shardPrompt closely):** the flip is BIGGER than "swap the verdict source" — the discovery shard does THREE things, only one of which classify-verdict covers: (1) the core VERDICT (review/execute/hard/handoff/idle) ← classify-verdict does this; (2) VERDICT-AFFECTING GUARDS classify-verdict does NOT yet handle — dirty-tree → surfaced (not dispatched), diverged-from-origin → surfaced, `dirty_lock_only`/`lock_only_unaudited` exemptions, behind-origin → ff-merge-then-reclassify; (3) RECONCILIATION ACTIONS (ff-merge, stale-worktree reap, orphan-park, in-place uv.lock commit) + the ~12 per-unit FIELDS (lastCkpt/income/openHard/standin/intensive/work_sig/strongRecheckPending) the loop consumes. So a SAFE flip needs, in order: **(a)** extend `classify-verdict` (+ `classify-repo` to pass the fields through: `dirty`,`dirty_lock_only`,`lock_only_unaudited`,`upstream_ahead_behind`,`has_upstream`,`worktrees`) to reach VERDICT PARITY incl. a disposition for dirty/diverged repos — note this needs a verdict-VOCABULARY decision (a `blocked`/`surfaced` value vs reusing `human`/AMBIGUOUS; touches the DP1 enum, so it's a small contract call, not a blind edit); **(b)** then the relay-loop.js swap — the shard runs `classify-repo.sh` for the verdict, keeps the side-effecting reconciliation guards + emits the per-unit fields from gather, LLM only on AMBIGUOUS. The reconciliation ACTIONS can NEVER move into the side-effect-free classifier — they stay shard-side. **This is careful Workflow-engine work the meeting explicitly gated (the engine crashed the pool 3×, template-literal-lint hazard); do it as a focused supervised step, NOT a tail-of-session rush. Next concrete unit = the verdict-parity extension (a), which is RED-testable with fixtures and touches NO engine code.** **STEP (a) COMPLETE 2026-07-01 (verified, commit pending):** verdict parity is CODE-COMPLETE — every verdict-affecting shard guard is in classify-verdict or subsumed: dirty→`blocked` + diverged→`blocked` + `dirty_lock_only` exemption + behind-only-not-blocked (all id:e424, tested in test_classify_verdict_parity.sh); `lock_only_unaudited`→not-review is SUBSUMED by `substantive_unaudited` (gather sets it False for a pure relock — verified end-to-end: lock-only unaudited ⟹ substantive_unaudited=False ⟹ classify-verdict `idle`, NOT review), now locked in by new assertions in test_gather_repo_state.sh 9a/9b; `worktrees` is a reconciliation input (step b, stays shard-side), NOT verdict parity. classify-repo already passes the full gather JSON through. So NOTHING remains for step (a). **The flip's ONLY remaining work is step (b): the relay-loop.js engine swap** (shard runs classify-repo for the verdict, keeps side-effecting reconciliation guards shard-side, LLM on AMBIGUOUS only) — explicitly GATED as careful supervised engine work (the engine crashed the pool 3×), NOT a tail-of-session edit. With C3 + C4 satisfied (id:9d2b) and step (a) done, step (b) is the sole gate to the flip. **STEP (b) DESIGNED 2026-07-01 (meeting `docs/meeting-notes/2026-07-01-1904-a0b6-step-b-engine-swap.md`):** the Workflow-sandbox constraint (relay-loop.js runs no bash — every git/script goes through an `agent()`) reframes "delete the shard" as "replace the JUDGMENT shard with a MECHANICAL RUNNER agent" that runs scripts + relays JSON. Decomposed into ROADMAP: **(1) id:5987 [ROUTINE]** `reconcile-repo.sh` — the bounded side-effecting git ops (ff-merge/reap/orphan-park/uv.lock-commit) split into a hermetically-tested script (executor-actionable, RED-testable, NO engine code); **(2) a0b6 remainder [HARD — pool]** — the confined `relay-loop.js:820-900` swap (runner = `reconcile-repo.sh`→`classify-repo.sh` per repo, LLM on AMBIGUOUS only), HARD swap / old shardPrompt DELETED / merge+backstop code untouched, **gated on id:5987 green**; **(3) id:b50e [HARD — decision gate]** — delete the 000d/9973/ad74 backstops once the AMBIGUOUS path is proven constrained (365b stays). All four backstops KEPT this session (none fully subsumed while the LLM/AMBIGUOUS path exists). <!-- id:a0b6 -->

- [x] **Investigate the 2026-07-01 flip discovery-precision regressions + model the relay as a STATE MACHINE ("what touches what when" / invalid-state detection)** [HARD — meeting] (user 2026-07-01, from the a0b6-flip `/relay --once` smoke). **Two precision bugs the smoke surfaced** — both are the mechanical classifier (now authoritative post-flip) being LESS precise than the old LLM shard on real ledger shapes; caught by the executor's own size-out → clean handbacks (no damage), but wasteful (executor sessions spawned for nothing): **(1) `classify-verdict.sh` gates `execute` on `has_routine`** (ANY `[ROUTINE]` substring) instead of an actionable-routine signal (open, tag-anchored `[ROUTINE]`, `@manual`/human-gated EXCLUDED). `classify-repo.sh` computes `roadmap_actionable_open` but it MIXES routine+pool and isn't used for the execute gate → `yinyang-puzzle` (all `[ROUTINE]` @manual) mis-fired `execute`→handback. Need a distinct `actionable_routine_open` and gate `execute` on it — restores the id:e107 executor-actionable precision the flip dropped. **(2) `[ROUTINE]` detected by substring `"[ROUTINE]" in ln`, not tag-anchored** to the item's own bracket → `leAIrn2learn` matched `[ROUTINE]` inside id:c3f5's INLINE-HISTORY prose (a `[HARD — pool]` item) → phantom `execute` (same class as the id:1bbd HARD-lane prose-match fix; apply the backtick/prose-strip anchoring to the ROUTINE/execute path). **Fix both as executor-actionable RED-spec items** (fixtures: @manual-only repo → NOT execute; prose-`[ROUTINE]` → NOT execute). **BROADER (the reason to link — user-directed):** build a STATE-MACHINE / state-diagram model of the relay to fully grasp **what touches what when** — the artifact × actor × transition matrix (ROADMAP / TODO / REVIEW_ME / relay.toml / worktrees / leases / decision-queue / **main-checkout**, written/read by prelude / discover-run / executor / reviewer / integrator / id:3801 gate-detection / `/meeting`) — and **define validity invariants so 'invalid states' become detectable**. Two invalid states already observed: (i) an **uncommitted main-checkout edit** (loderite id:3801 gate-detection residue stranded dirty — the deeper fix is to make id:3801's main write COMMIT ATOMICALLY, id:148b flock+commit pattern, so it can't strand); (ii) an **`execute` verdict on a repo with no executor-actionable work** (the two bugs above). A formal state model turns both classes into a deterministic invalid-state DETECTOR (a relay-doctor id:9bec extension) instead of ad-hoc per-round discovery. Relate: id:4d8e (the flip umbrella), a0b6 (shipped), id:3801, id:e107, id:1bbd, id:148b, relay-doctor id:9bec. **PART-1 SHIPPED 2026-07-01 (relay-ckpt-20260701-2110):** the precision fixes landed — `classify-repo.sh` derives `actionable_routine_open` (open [ROUTINE], **primary-lane** = first lane-tag beats a fragile backtick-strip on 3.5k-char history lines, NOT @manual/human-gated, NOT dep-blocked "🚧/BLOCKED on"); `classify-verdict.sh` gates `execute` on it (−1 back-compat fallback). All 3 real /relay --once mis-fires verified fixed (yinyang-puzzle @manual→human, leAIrn2learn prose-[ROUTINE]-on-[HARD-pool]→hard, zkm-threema BLOCKED-on-dep→human); suite 151/0; locked at unit + integration tiers. **REMAINING (this item stays open):** the STATE-MACHINE model of "what touches what when" + the invalid-state DETECTOR (loderite id:3801 uncommitted-main residue + execute-on-no-work are the two seed invalid states) — the durable design that turns these ad-hoc precision patches into a systematic invariant check. **PART-2 DESIGNED 2026-07-01 (meeting `docs/meeting-notes/2026-07-01-2142-relay-state-machine-invalid-state-detector.md`):** the state-machine model (artifact×actor×transition matrix + invariants I1–I9, file:line-anchored) is delivered IN that note; the invalid-state detector is decomposed into `relay-doctor.sh` checks + the atomic-write fix, filed as RED-spec ROADMAP items **C1 id:8018 (check 9 main-residue), C2 id:188c (check 10 verdict-invariant replay), C3 id:333c (check 11 last_ckpt-tag), C4 id:e5e9 (handback-followup atomic-commit fix)**. C5 ratified report-only (surface loud, never auto-block; `--strict` opt-in). This item now tracks only those children shipping. **CLOSED 2026-07-01 (fable catch-up review):** all four children shipped in e407d42 and verified — C1 id:8018 / C2 id:188c / C3 id:333c (relay-doctor checks 9/10/11), C4 id:e5e9 (handback-followup atomic write+commit; death-sim test confirms a dying push leaves a clean tree); C5 was a ratified decision (report-only floor), no build. Suite 155/0; ROADMAP children all `[x]`, TODO mirrors archived. Follow-on state-diagram drift-guard stays open as id:a17a; the not-yet-wired invariants I5/I9 stay named in relay-doctor's coverage-gap block (gated on id:e149 / id:b444). done on 2026-07-01 <!-- id:4da4 -->
- [x] **Integrator mislabels a ZERO-COMMIT review branch as "duplicate dispatch" → handback, NO checkpoint → the audit window never closes and the pool re-dispatches strong reviews forever** [HARD — pool] — **done 2026-07-01** (same-night Fable bugfix child, merged + ckpt): relay-loop.js integrate distinguishes clean-empty-review (⇒ `ckpt-tag.sh -c <reviewed-tip>`, new flag) from genuine duplicate; destructive cleanup scoped to own-runId artifacts (id:6e02 hazard); worked_ids string-coercion fixed (`ids:["[","]"]` telemetry bug). Tests test_ckpt_tag_commit_target.sh + test_gather_stale_strong_watermark.sh; suite 158/0/0. (found by the 2026-07-01 fable catch-up review, from the run `relay-20260701-202806-14640` 20:36 handback). **Mechanism (verified against git timestamps + relay-events.jsonl):** the review child branched at the TRUE then-HEAD `33169ee` (20:20:36; next main commit `65ce4ea` landed 20:34:53 — the child did NOT see a stale main), made **zero commits** (a clean review window with no ledger delta is legitimate: commit-ledger.sh no-ops), so its branch tip stayed == its base == an ancestor of the (meanwhile advanced) main. The integrator's `git merge --no-ff` said "Already up to date" and it concluded "**Duplicate dispatch** — no integration action performed": no ckpt tag, no relay.toml update, handback. Consequences: (a) the misleading label masks the real state ("clean review, nothing to integrate"); (b) the reviewed window is never checkpointed, so the NEXT discovery re-classifies the same commits as unaudited and re-spends a strong review child — an unattended pool can loop this indefinitely; (c) tonight it also compounded the relay.toml staleness (id:0a3b). **Fix (the design nuance that makes this pool-lane, not ROUTINE):** the integrator must distinguish `tip == base ∧ zero commits` (clean empty review → **checkpoint at the reviewed tip**, i.e. tag the child's base/tip commit — NOT current main HEAD, which may contain newer commits the review never saw and must not be marked audited) from a genuine duplicate (branch already merged by an earlier integrator in the SAME run). Surface in relay-loop.js integrate() prompt (:1424 area) + a fixture test (zero-commit branch + advanced main → ckpt at branch tip, no handback). Relates: invariant I8/id:333c (ckpt bookkeeping), id:9d2b backtest (re-review waste), the 2026-07-01 events-log evidence. <!-- id:8e3e -->
- [x] **Supervised/manual checkpoints never update relay.toml (`last_ckpt`/`last_strong_ckpt` written ONLY by the pool integrator) → registry lags the tag truth** [ROUTINE] (found by the 2026-07-01 fable catch-up review; observed live: `[repos.dotclaude-skills]` still reads `last_ckpt = relay-ckpt-20260701-1635` while tags 1948/2019/2110 exist — all three were minted by interactive Opus sessions via `ckpt-tag.sh`, which tags + writes RELAY_LOG but never touches relay.toml; relay-loop.js:1426 is the only writer, via `relay-state-write.sh toml-set`). **Impact (verified, honestly bounded):** discovery verdicts are NOT mis-driven — `gather-repo-state.sh:156` derives `latest_ckpt` from `git tag -l` (tags are the source of truth). What drifts: the id:e030 Fable-recheck queue (`last_strong_ckpt`/`fable_rechecked` miss strong checkpoints made outside the pool) and relay-doctor check 11 semantics (it verifies the RECORDED tag exists, silently passing a stale-but-existing value). **Fix:** `ckpt-tag.sh` updates `[repos.<name>]` `last_ckpt` (and `last_strong_ckpt`+`strong_model` when the annotation records a strong model) through the flock'd `relay-state-write.sh toml-set` single-writer, iff the repo has a `[repos.<name>]` block (silently skip unmanaged repos; honor the existing `FABLES_CONFIG` env override for hermetic tests). Red spec: `tests/test_ckpt_tag_toml.sh`. **done 2026-07-01** (Fable bugfix child): flock'd toml-set sync at the ckpt-tag.sh choke-point, spec GREEN; PLUS the upstream consumer hardened — gather-repo-state.sh audit window anchors on the newest strong-labeled tag when newer than the toml watermark (test_gather_stale_strong_watermark.sh), killing the duplicate-strong-dispatch spin at both ends. <!-- id:0a3b -->
- [x] **`git-lock-push.sh` autostash-rebase guard (id:aa93) false-positives on `~/.claude` every push — perpetual runtime churn reads as "concurrent edit"** [ROUTINE] done 2026-07-02 (executor, ROADMAP twin id:dff8): shipped (b)+(c) — porcelain classifies untracked-only churn (proceed with the autostash-rebase) vs any tracked entry (still refuse); refusal wording drops the "(a concurrent edit?)" guess, states facts instead. Option (a) tracked-runtime-churn gitignore stays a separate claude-diary decision, out of scope here. (recurring, observed multiple times incl. 2026-06-30) — pushing to `~/.claude` via `git-lock-push.sh` repeatedly refuses with *"WARNING: working tree is dirty (a concurrent edit?); refusing to autostash-rebase to avoid data loss (id:aa93). Commit or move the changes, then run 'git push' manually."* **Root cause:** `~/.claude` is a worktree of the claude-diary repo whose tree is **perpetually dirty with harness RUNTIME churn** — tracked `.last-cleanup` + `history.jsonl` (rewritten constantly) and untracked `plans/`, `session-env/`, `sessions/`, `tasks/` (NOT gitignored). The dirtiness guard sees this and refuses, even though NONE of it is a concurrent session's WIP (user confirmed 2026-06-30: "there's no concurrent .claude session"). The error's own parenthetical "(a concurrent edit?)" is misleading — it's structural runtime state, not contention. **Effect:** every `~/.claude` push needs the manual plain-push fallback; the guard's "concurrent edit" framing also actively MISLEADS the operator into a wrong diagnosis (filed as a chidiAI anti-bluff datapoint, cf. cf01). **Fix options to weigh:** (a) **gitignore the runtime churn** in `~/.claude` (`.last-cleanup`, `history.jsonl`, `plans/`, `session-env/`, `sessions/`, `tasks/`) so the tree is clean except real edits — but check the `snapshot` commit job + the sessions-worktree archival (claude-diary TODO) don't rely on tracking them; (b) **scope the dirtiness check to the manifest** — in manifest mode `git-lock-push.sh` stages specific files, so the guard should only block when THOSE files (or the rebase) would conflict, ignoring unrelated untracked/runtime churn; (c) reword the warning to drop the false "concurrent edit?" causal guess (state "tree dirty; not autostashing" without inventing a cause — per the mechanize-first / no-confident-bluff rule). Prefer (b)+(c): the guard should reason about the actual commit, not the whole tree, and never assert an unverified cause. Relates id:aa93 (the guard), id:3558 (worktree-per-session), claude-diary sessions-worktree archival. <!-- id:dff8 -->

(found 2026-07-02 by the Fable recheck of relay-ckpt-20260702-0119 — itself the bogus dispatch: empty diff window, `strong_model = "claude-fable-5"`, yet the verdict reason claimed "Opus stood in for Fable"): the id:e030 consume gate `isFableRecheck = SESSION_IS_FABLE && unit.verdict === 'review'` (relay-loop.js:1393) sends a Fable **handoff**/hard strong checkpoint down the else branch → `fable_rechecked = false` → a Fable-rechecks-Fable review next round; and the elevation reason (~:1046) hardcodes the Opus-standin phrasing regardless of the recorded `strong_model`. Write-side sibling of id:a42e (read-side standin substring) and id:25aa (tag anchor) — third mechanism in the wasted-re-dispatch family. Red spec `tests/test_fable_recheck_write_side.sh`. - [x] **"Effectively-drained" ROADMAP (only `@manual`/human-lane items open) doesn't trigger the handoff-promote / unpromoted-scan route → large TODO backlog reported as "needs human / idle"** [HARD — pool] **DONE 2026-07-02 (handoff C5, ROADMAP twin id:9014):** loop half had already shipped via id:4da4/id:5eb3 (classify-repo.sh actionable counts + classify-verdict case-b split, test-locked); the remaining `/relay next` route-1/2a doc predicate re-keyed on executor-ACTIONABLE with classify-repo.sh cited as authority; drift-guard tests/test_next_actionable_predicate.sh green. (user 2026-06-30, "something's fishy… was there no work left in truncocraft?") — the autonomous pool classifier AND `/relay next` route 2a (id:2dea) only fire the TODO→ROADMAP promote / unpromoted-scan path when ROADMAP has **ZERO** open `- [ ]` items. A ROADMAP whose ONLY open item is `@manual`/human-lane is *not* zero-open, so the route never fires — yet that item can't be executed either, so the repo falls into the "needs a human, ROUTINE all @manual, not dispatched (id:e107)" bucket. **Observed:** truncocraft ROADMAP = 1 open item (id:ab02, `@manual`); TODO = **35 open items**. The `--afk` run reported truncocraft as `ROUTINE backlog is all @manual/human-only — needs a human` and moved on — describing only the single @manual ROADMAP box while **silently hiding the 35-item TODO backlog**. `unpromoted-scan.sh ~/src/truncocraft` shows all 35 as `surface` (untagged design backlog — correctly NOT auto-promotable, but several, e.g. id:556f box-select-start ghost, are concrete and would become `[ROUTINE]` under a handoff-C2 lane triage; the rest are `[HARD — meeting]` design epics). Same class as the 2026-06-25 truncocraft miss ([[unpromoted-scan-and-inbox-layering-2dea]]), one rung up: there a fully-`[x]` ROADMAP hid the backlog; here a lone `@manual` box does. **Fix:** the "drained" predicate for the promote/surface route must mean *no executor-ACTIONABLE open ROADMAP item* (treat `@manual`/human-lane-only as effectively-drained), so it routes to handoff-C2 (or at minimum **SURFACES** "N unpromoted TODO items need triage" in RELAY_STATUS) instead of filing the repo as plain "needs human / idle". Applies to BOTH the relay-loop classifier and `/relay next` route 2a. Keep the anti-guess discipline (id:78ff): still NEVER auto-promote an untagged item with a guessed lane — the fix is SURFACING + triggering a handoff pass, not auto-executing. <!-- id:9014 -->

- [x] **Discovery step inherits the session model (Opus) — pin it cheap** — CLOSED 2026-07-02 (handoff C2, overtaken-by-events): the preferred "remove the model from the hot path" line SHIPPED — the LLM discovery shard was DELETED by the id:a0b6 mechanical-runner swap (ROADMAP a0b6 [x] 2026-07-01, discovery now deterministic reconcile+classify+route), and the residual transport agents are pinned cheap (relay-loop.js:733 `discover-prelude` model:'haiku', :849 `discover-run` model:'haiku' — verified 2026-07-02); the id:3ac8 reap decision moved into deterministic `reconcile-repo.sh`, dissolving the "don't put reaping on haiku" blocker. Original text: relay-loop.js the per-round discovery `agent()` (classifier) has NO `model:` override, so it runs on the session model (Opus on a `--strong-tier opus` run), scanning 30+ repos every round; write-status/quota/integrate are already pinned haiku/sonnet. Classification is purely mechanical (4 deterministic rules over `git tag/log`, ROADMAP `[ROUTINE]` grep, `status --porcelain`, standin grep) with only the "acceptable-dirt" relay.toml-comment check needing any nuance. Cheap interim: add `model: 'sonnet'` (haiku if the acceptable-dirt judgment proves robust) to the discover opts — cuts per-round cost immediately. Full fix = the hybrid mechanical+model discover (id:23fe) / deterministic `discover-repos.sh` emitting DISCOVER_SCHEMA JSON. Do NOT cache verdicts in relay.toml (staleness — git is the source of truth; re-running cheap deterministic discovery each round is both correct and fast). Surfaced 2026-06-15. **DEPRIORITIZED 2026-06-15:** id:3ac8 added a *consequential* `worktree remove --force` decision to the discover agent, so a blind cheap-model pin is now risky (don't put reaping on haiku). A genuine conflict aborts the merge and exits non-zero (branch/commit untouched, nothing pushed) instead of silently last-writer-wins, matching D5.2's plumbing-CAS rejection. - [x] **[HARD — pool] Discovery-queue verdict/state coherence — content-address the CASE A copy (per-repo `discover-sig` stamped in the queue; copy only on queue-sig == live-sig) + sig-echo as mangle canary** (Fable review 2026-07-08) — three verified gaps in the id:7402 consumption path: (1) `relay-loop.js:914-923` trusts `latest.json` MTIME alone — a repo whose live sig just flagged CHANGED still gets the ≤20-min-old snapshot verdict copied (executor commits at T; snapshot from T−10min says execute/idle → the required `review` never fires), and `:969` then caches that stale verdict under the NEW live sig so it outlives the freshness window; (2) a repo that goes dirty AFTER the snapshot bypasses the classify `blocked` guard (live reconcile surfaces nothing → stale non-blocked verdict copied) — bounded only by the executor-side clean-tree gate; (3) the Haiku bridge-copy is shape-validated (SHARD_SCHEMA) but never content-checked against the queue bytes it just cat'd — a mangled copy that drops `is_finished`/`open_hard_pool`/`top_intensive`/`work_sig` silently DISARMS the id:000d/9973/ad74/365b backstops (missing fields default 0/''/false = never fire). ONE mechanism fixes all three: the producer (id:9d97) stamps each queue entry with that repo's `discover-sig.sh` value; CASE A copies a repo's verdict ONLY when its queue sig equals the live sig from the prelude (else that repo falls to the live exec path); the JS then asserts `u.sig === sigByRepo[u.repo]` on every queue-sourced unit — content-addressing dissolves (1)+(2) structurally and the sig echo is a mechanical mangle canary for (3) (a fabricated-but-correct sig is implausible; mismatch → surface LOUDLY, never dispatch). **done 2026-07-08 (directed opus):** producer (`discover-repos-mechanical.sh`) runs `discover-sig.sh` per repo and stamps `queue_sig` on every units/surfaced/skipped entry (fail-open empty stamped as-is); CASE A runner prompt copies a verdict ONLY when the queue entry's `queue_sig` byte-matches the repo's live sig (carried into the chunk JSON), else falls to the live `discover-repo.sh` path for that repo; JS mangle canary (`u.queue_sig !== sigByRepo[u.repo]` → drop+surface loudly) runs BEFORE the `discoverCache` write (kills stale-cache poisoning). Schema declares `unit.queue_sig`; manifest documents the field. Tests extended; full suite 197 pass / 2 expected-red. <!-- id:4860 -->

- [x] **[MEETING] Sandboxing: run Claude / `/relay` as a separate OS user** — so auto-mode can't break anything beyond that user's permissions (group setup on `~/src`, write-isolation, polkit interaction). Motivated by the probe's invocation design (see `docs/meeting-notes/2026-06-17-0905-model-probe-tos-and-band.md` D1); broader scope warrants its own design meeting. **Child use-case 2026-07-07 (id:ec23):** run the UNATTENDED mechanical discovery producer (id:9d97 `.timer`) as a write-restricted OS user — write only its output dirs (`~/.config/relay/discovery-queue/`, heartbeat, logs), NO write to repos or `~/.cache/relay/worktrees` → a future code regression that reaches a destructive git op becomes an OS-denied LOUD failure, not a silent `rm`. Defense-in-depth outer layer above the fail-closed reconcile fix (id:e0f8); folds into this meeting's scope. **Two more use-cases 2026-07-08 (Fable review):** (i) the mechanical-run daemon (id:b3d0) `bash -c`'s relay-authored recipe cmds unattended, and its drop-dir trust boundary ("only a relay reviewer session writes pending/") is documented, NOT enforced — `~/.config/relay/recipes/pending` is plain user-writable and the `.path` unit auto-fires on any write; running daemon + producer under the same write-restricted OS user turns a forged/compromised recipe into an OS-denied loud failure (see id:1cb8 for the daemon's mechanical hardening). (ii) The id:af30 launch-wall: a dedicated sandboxed OS user (the model-probe id:2d01 ToS-reviewed precedent — subscription `claude -p` as its own user with empty `~/.claude`) is the structural answer to "headless pool relaunch needs `--dangerously-skip-permissions`" — inside a write-scoped user that flag stops being a blanket bypass; resolving it also unblocks eliminating the D3 residual queue-read (id:7402, per-round relaunch becomes possible). This meeting should therefore rank the sandbox as the LEAD permission strategy, with the broker-instance path (b444 transport / long-lived interactive session) as the human-INPUT channel, not the permission solution. Keep the [[anthropic-agent-sdk-billing-deferred]] hedge: headless `claude -p` volume economics may flip on the billing cutover — advance notice promised. **AGENDA ADD 2026-07-08 (user found `permissionMode: auto` default in `/config`):** constraint-archaeology question — does the af30 launch-wall still bind under a default-auto permissionMode? CAUTION (Fable): auto mode is NOT skip-permissions dissolving the wall — the af30 governor was denied BY the auto-mode classifier, so auto mode is the thing that said no to the *persistent permission-bypassing loop*; but it plausibly changes the `claude -p` calculus for ordinary relay operations (headless under auto may auto-approve the allowlisted/read surface without `--dangerously-skip-permissions` at all, classifier as guard). Meeting should TEST empirically what a headless `claude -p "/relay --afk"` under permissionMode=auto actually gets approved/denied, per operation class, before designing the sandbox around the skip-permissions assumption — the cheapest outcome is that the sandbox only needs to cover the classifier's deny-tail, not a blanket bypass. **HELD 2026-07-08** — see `docs/meeting-notes/2026-07-08-1214-sandboxing-relay-os-users.md`; decisions D1 (two users now: `relay-ro`/`relay-svc`, `relay-pool` scoped-deferred), D2 (per-dir ACLs on `~/.config/relay`; `~/src` reads free via DAC), D3 (hybrid `--user`+root-owned-units-in-`/etc/systemd/user`+hardening-directives for A/B, `--system` for C; file-based creds, polkit default-deny), D4 (measure-first: auto-mode deny-tail probe GATES skip-perms; sandbox=containment≠authorization), D5 (ssh-push boundary is preferred C repo-write). Build children: id:13ae/02c7/8e7a/5937/38bf/e2b1/d916. <!-- id:d0c0 --> <!-- id:ec23 -->
- [x] **[HARD — meeting] Formally PROVE component contracts (read-only / pure / idempotent) with Lean4, not only test them** — ✅ DECIDED 2026-07-08 (see docs/meeting-notes/2026-07-08-1337-lean4-relay-mechanization-substrate.md): Lean pilot, substrate REVISITABLE at island-2; follow-ups id:50c4 (relay-core repo) / id:82c4 (classify-verdict pilot) / id:ba31 (reconcile island 2, gated) / id:7746 (executor-lane doc) + routed:b8e5 (aae4 triad toolchain pin). — user 2026-07-07: tests sample; a proof covers all inputs. Explore machine-checked contracts for the safety-critical relay primitives (reconcile reap, the producer's read-only promise, claim/lease invariants) via Lean4. Big design meeting (scope, what's provable vs testable, the spec↔code gap, effort). Ties to the user's Lean interest and the ChidiAI **Grand Truth Triad** (.mw / toesnail / ChidiAI) — see routed relation to chidiai. Relates [[feedback-enforce-not-document-contracts]], [[inflownistration-term]], id:e0f8/758e (the test-based layers this would formalize). **Fable pre-meeting take 2026-07-08 (user asked how this relates to the sandbox d0c0/ec23):** COMPLEMENTS, does not replace — they guard DIFFERENT layers. The OS sandbox enforces the write BOUNDARY over all inputs immediately (it would have stopped the e0f8 near-miss: the read-only producer had no business writing worktrees at all), but it CANNOT stop an authorized-but-wrong action — the dispatch-side reconcile legitimately reaps worktrees, so a wrong-TARGET reap is invisible to any boundary enforcement; that logic layer is exactly where proofs add unique value. The crux is the spec↔code gap (bash is not directly provable); the pragmatic pilot shape: formalize the reap/claim/lease STATE MACHINE already half-specified in the id:4da4 matrix + invariants I1–I9 (`docs/meeting-notes/2026-07-01-2142-relay-state-machine-invalid-state-detector.md`), prove ~2 invariants in Lean4 ("reap-set ∩ live-claims = ∅ in every reachable state"; "absent/unknown claim context ⇒ reap-set = ∅" — the e0f8 fail-closed rule), then GENERATE conformance test vectors from the model and run them against the bash (verified-reference-model pattern: the proof is exhaustive on the model, the vectors carry it to the code; the residual model↔code gap is pinned by the vectors and stated honestly, never claimed as proved). NB the mechanization work already built the precondition: classify-verdict.sh / the reconcile decision islands are pure JSON-in/JSON-out functions — the exact shape a Lean model can mirror 1:1. Sequencing: sandbox meeting first (days of effort, broad coverage, catches the observed incident class); this pilot second; neither gates the other. The meeting should resist scope creep beyond the one state machine — "prove the relay" is not the pilot. **RESCOPED 2026-07-08 (user): NOT Lean4-proofing bash — write the mechanization ITSELF in Lean4, bash only where unavoidable.** This supersedes the verified-reference-model/conformance-vector pattern above: the model↔code gap dissolves because the proven function IS the shipped code (Lean 4 compiles to native; `IO.Process` runs git; systemd runs the binary). Assessment (Fable): ~80% of the win is types-not-theorems — total JSON parsers (unknown/missing field = LOUD error, kills the backstop-disarm class id:4860-3), exhaustive verdict matches (kills the 0d58 substring class), one shared vocabulary type (kills the intensity/probe mismatch id:1cb8-6), no swallowed subshell exits (kills id:0fa0-a) — all by construction, zero theorems; the last 20% is proofs where they matter (reap invariants, fail-closed lemmas, 5ac6 "INTENSIVE never operative on human lanes" as a theorem). RELAY SYNERGY: a required named theorem is an UNGAMEABLE acceptance test — `lake build` + mandatory theorems cannot be faked green, strengthening the anti-gaming apparatus for delegated work. Architecture: plan/apply split — pure proven PLANNER (state → actions) + dumb effectful interpreter; the unproven rind (flock, tmp+mv, git exec) stays thin and typed. Strangler migration behind the EXISTING JSON contracts, reusing the a0b6 flip machinery (shadow → backtest parity → gated flip, bash fallback until N clean runs) — do NOT big-bang the week-old mechanical layer; land id:4860/0fa0/1cb8 in bash first. Pilot = port `classify-verdict` (pure JSON→JSON, fixture corpus + backtest replay = ready parity gate); island 2 = reconcile PLANNER w/ reap-invariant theorems (kills the havoc-reaper class at the root). MEETING must decide: toolchain/install story (public zero-dep repo + elan/lake — in-repo lake pkg vs sibling repo shipping a binary; the biggest real cost), island order + flip gates, executor-lane economics (Lean-w/-proofs = HARD-lane initially; theorem-gated delegation may flip that), what gets theorems vs types-only (`sorry`-free but theorem-light is a valid first rung), Lean-vs-Rust diligence question stated honestly (types-only would favor Rust's ecosystem; theorems + user's Lean investment justify Lean). **chidiAI / Grand-Truth correlation (user asked 2026-07-08):** the Lean relay core is the strongest rung of the triad's certainty-gating mesh (measure certainty → gate the action → degrade gracefully): (1) relay anti-gaming IS chidiAI's anti-bluff invariant in another substrate — an executor's "done, tests green" is a high-confidence/weak-verification claim (the exact case-law: `chidiai/docs/cases/2026-06-30-done-claim-tests-green-untested-clause.md`, our id:369c); a required named theorem is verification that CANNOT be bluffed, so theorem-backed modules can be gate-ASSERTed with cheaper review — i.e. tier the relay's own review depth by verification class, the mesh applied to the relay's review economics; (2) chidiAI's `VerificationBackend` protocol (chidiai id:79ee) already lists formal/Lean4 via the SHARED tier owned by mathematical-writing id:aae4 as a future concrete backend — the relay core is its first concrete target (routed:e470 to chidiai, gated on this pilot); the 23ab meeting must COORDINATE the toolchain/install choices with aae4 so elan/lake/binary decisions are made ONCE for the triad, not per-repo; (3) case-law symmetry: chidiAI's `docs/cases/` bluff library and the relay's RED-fixture corpus are the same epistemic pattern (each observed failure becomes machine-checked); a by-construction Lean fix retires whole case FAMILIES — record such retirements on both sides. **Distribution (user 2026-07-08):** GH releases work mechanically but clash with this repo's stated no-build/no-release/live-install-IS-published ethos → favor a SIBLING repo (`relay-core`) shipping per-arch binaries via its own GH releases with proper vX.Y.Z bump-and-tag (the versioning this repo deliberately lacks); the user-required opt-in ("`/relay` must work without it") falls out of the strangler FOR FREE — capability-detect the binary (PATH / `~/.local/bin`), else the bash path, the exact CASE A/CASE B fallback shape the discovery queue (id:7402) just shipped. Guard the house anti-pattern (derived-artifact drift): opt-in dual-path must NOT become permanent dual maintenance — once the flip gate passes, the bash twin FREEZES (bugfix-only), new behavior is Lean-only, and relay-doctor loudly reports "mechanical core absent — legacy bash path" so the fallback never silently ossifies. Full /relay repo split = separate trigger-gated question, id:a72c. <!-- id:23ab -->

- [x] **Reap a relay run's heartbeat on OBSERVED failure, not just TTL expiry — kill the redundant late watchdog popup** [ROUTINE] (observed 2026-07-07) — the outage watchdog (id:98f0) correctly notified that the `-c`-crash run (relay-...-174626) died, but ~1h LATE and REDUNDANTLY: the session had already detected the Workflow failure immediately (task-notification), relaunched, and reconciled the orphans within minutes — yet the dead heartbeat marker lingered because at relaunch it was only ~25min stale (under the ~3600s TTL), so auto-reconcile-on-restart (id:7809) didn't reap it; it aged past the TTL ~1h later and tripped the watchdog for a crash already resolved. Fix: when a failure is OBSERVED synchronously — the front door sees the `relay-loop.js` Workflow reject, OR the relaunch's auto-reconcile just parked a run's orphans — reap/mark that run's heartbeat IMMEDIATELY (it is known-dead; don't wait for the conservative TTL). Keep the TTL path as the backstop for the true out-of-session death (session killed, no observer) the watchdog exists for. Net: an in-session crash that's handled promptly stops re-alarming; only genuinely-unobserved deaths wait out the TTL. Small change to the front-door failure path + possibly `relay-reconcile --auto` (reap markers whose orphans it just parked). — covered by tests (tests/test_heartbeat_reap_run.sh) on 2026-07-08 <!-- id:7725 -->

- [x] **[HARD — meeting] Move the relay DISCOVERY loop off the Workflow engine so it can run mechanically (cron/systemd, free to curl/exec) — architecture call** (user 2026-07-01, from the id:2ec4 "can the Workflow JS just curl a local RPC?" question). The Workflow sandbox has **no network/shell/fs/subprocess** (confirmed `relay-loop.js:1529/1551` — `process.env` crashed the pool; a Haiku agent runs ONLY because "the sandbox has no shell/fs"), so `agent()` (a billed LLM) is the ONLY way to shell out from inside the loop. That is the *sole* reason discovery still spends an LLM round-trip after the a0b6 flip made it pure-transport (id:2ec4). **Alternative architecture:** run discovery as an ordinary cron/systemd script OUTSIDE the Workflow — free to `curl` a local RPC / `exec` `discover-repo.sh` directly, zero LLM — and only use a Workflow for the executor **dispatch** (which genuinely needs the managed `agent()` pool, [[relay-autonomous-pool-design]]). **The catch to resolve in the meeting:** the Workflow can't read a file/queue either (no fs), so handing per-round discovery results *into* the executor Workflow still needs one `agent()` read per round (via the existing prelude/inject path) UNLESS the whole loop leaves the Workflow engine — which means reimplementing executor parallelism (worktrees, leases, integrator, quota-stop) outside the pool. So it trades the discovery LLM shim for either (a) one residual `agent()` bridge-read per round (hybrid: cron discovery → queue → Workflow executor), or (b) abandoning the Workflow engine wholesale (lose the pool; own the parallelism). Design Qs: is the hybrid's single bridge-read cheaper than today's haiku prelude+run? does leaving the Workflow engine forfeit too much (the pool is WHY it's a Workflow)? does a cron discovery reintroduce the outage-resilience problem the Workflow-managed loop solved ([[relay-outage-resilience-design]], [[babysitter-durable-cron-no-op]])? Relate: id:2ec4 (the mechanical-tier forward-flag this splits from), [[relay-autonomous-pool-design]], id:98f0/7809 (watchdog/reconcile that already assume a local loop), id:a17a (state-diagram — the loop's engine boundary is exactly what a state model must draw). Gate: only worth it if the residual haiku discovery cost (measure per id:2ec4) proves material. **DECIDED 2026-07-07** (`docs/meeting-notes/2026-07-07-1228-relay-discovery-off-workflow.md`): reframed — the gate is NOT token cost but **reliability** (Haiku mangles even "exec+echo JSON"; rule = "no LLM if mechanical can do as good or better"). SPLIT: build the mechanical discovery producer now (id:9d97); residual queue-read stays deferred+labeled gated on the launch-wall (id:7402); two-liveness-domain heartbeat (id:54fc); wholesale-off-Workflow = trigger-gated contingency (id:882a). This umbrella stays open until the children land. <!-- children:9d97,7402,54fc --> <!-- id:65f9 -->
- [x] **[ROUTINE] `append.sh inbox-done XXXX` deletes EVERY checkbox line containing `routed:XXXX` — a cross-reference in another item's prose is collateral** (found 2026-07-10 while routing two chidiAI cases). `meeting/append.sh`'s inbox-done predicate is `needle in l and l.lstrip().startswith("- [")` with `needle = f"routed:{token}"` — a substring test, not an anchor. So an inbox item whose *prose* legitimately cites a sibling's token (e.g. "the contrast with `routed:4fa9` is the signal") is a checkbox line containing that needle, and resolving the sibling **silently deletes the citing item too**. inbox-done is destructive by design (vanish-on-resolve), and the inbox is local-only and never committed, so a wrongly-deleted item is **unrecoverable** — no git twin to restore from. Hit live this session: a praise-case item citing the offender case's token would have been destroyed by `inbox-done 4fa9`; worked around by writing the sibling token bare (`4fa9`, no `routed:` prefix), which is fragile convention, not a guard. **Fix:** anchor on the trailing marker comment `<!-- routed:XXXX -->` (the anchoring already mandated for the twin check in the global CLAUDE.md, and for typed edges id:46f6), not a bare substring; and refuse loudly (non-zero, write nothing) when the token matches ≠1 line. Same fail-open-substring class as id:1b1a (`md-merge.py update-ids` appends on an unmatched id) and id:0d58 (`[MECHANICAL]` substring false-positive) — a third instance, which argues the anchoring rule belongs in ONE shared helper rather than being re-derived per reader. Contract a test would verify: an item whose prose cites another's `routed:` token survives that other's `inbox-done`; a token matching two marker comments exits non-zero and deletes nothing. <!-- id:411d -->
- [x] **[ROUTINE] Typed-edge detector in `meeting/orphan-scan.sh --shipped`** (CLOSED-2026-07-10, review-verified: shipped c9645d1, `tests/test_orphan_scan_edges.sh` green — always-counts) — parse the `children:` / `gated-on:` sibling HTML comments placed BEFORE the id comment (amendment A1) and emit six advisory classes: UMBRELLA-READY / UMBRELLA-OPEN / UMBRELLA-CROSS-REPO / UMBRELLA-UNRESOLVED (LOUD, non-zero exit) / GATE-READY / UNMARKED-GATE. A marker-bearing item bypasses `wait_re` entirely (A2 — dissolve, don't clause-parse); unmarked items keep today's path byte-for-byte. Closure = child edges only, over `TODO.md` + `TODO.archive.md` by CHECKBOX, not archive membership (`TODO.archive.md:311` holds an OPEN sub-item). Never reads ROADMAP.md; never auto-ticks. Cross-repo evidence via `relay/scripts/lib-own-repos.sh` `own_repos()`, NEVER a `~/src` glob (`zkm-ner` lives at `~/src/zkm/plugins/zkm-ner`). Contract: hermetic `tests/test_orphan_scan_edges.sh`, one fixture per class + a regression fixture where the only `gated` sits inside a quoted child clause. `docs/meeting-notes/2026-07-10-1430-typed-ledger-edges-umbrella-closure.md` <!-- id:46f6 -->
