- [x] [ROUTINE] **[HIGH PRIORITY] `md-merge.py update-ids` silently APPENDS an unmatched id — a typo'd token creates a duplicate ledger line instead of failing** (found 2026-07-10 while designing the typed-edge marker). `md-merge.py:143-152`: ids not present in the file are appended (anchored before the first archive heading, id:14d0). That is correct for *new* items but fail-OPEN for *updates*: a caller intending to edit `id:abcd` who mistypes it gets a silent second line, not an error — and the whole point of routing writes through this helper is that it is the SAFE path. Concretely, the near-miss this session: an inline `id:XXXX children:...` marker would not have matched `md-merge.py:135`'s `<!--\s*id:([0-9a-f]{4})\s*-->` regex, so every one of 18 intended in-place updates would have been appended as a duplicate. **Fix:** make intent explicit — updates fail LOUDLY on a miss (non-zero + named tokens on stderr); appends require an opt-in flag (`--allow-new` or a separate `add-items` subcommand). Then audit the ~10 readers that assume the id comment terminates immediately after the 4 hex digits (`orphan-scan.sh` ×6, `md-merge.py:135`, `handback-followup.py` ×3, `unpromoted-scan.sh` ×2, `backtest-historical.py`, `relay-doctor.sh`, `classify.sh:36`, `tests/run-tests.sh:33`). **Already broken today:** `handback-followup.py:71`'s `$`-anchored regex does not match `id:78ff`, whose id comment is followed by an `xledger-ok:` annotation — a pre-existing silent no-op, not introduced by the marker work. Contract a test would verify: `update-ids` with an unknown id exits non-zero and writes nothing; with `--allow-new` it appends as today; `handback-followup` appends its note to a line whose id comment is non-terminal. Relates id:46f6 (the typed-edge marker whose syntax this constrained), id:ee62 (visible-vs-comment annotations). `docs/meeting-notes/2026-07-10-1430-typed-ledger-edges-umbrella-closure.md` <!-- id:1b1a -->
- [x] [ROUTINE] ** `orphan-scan.sh --shipped` never sees INDENTED sub-items — 14 of 241 open id-bearing TODO items (6%) are structurally invisible to every `--shipped` class** (found 2026-07-10 by the id:46f6 executor, confirmed against the live ledger). The shipped-mode driver greps `^- \[ \] ` anchored at column 0, so any `  - [ ] … <!-- id:XXXX -->` nested under a parent is never classified — not for TICK-READY, not for GATE-STALE, and now not for the typed-edge umbrella/gate classes either. Concretely inert today: the `gated-on:` markers written onto `id:3c4c` (TODO.md:36) and `id:eb92` (TODO.md:37), and `id:7df1` (TODO.md:56) which was expected to surface as UNMARKED-GATE. This is PRE-EXISTING (not introduced by id:46f6) and was deliberately left alone by the executor as an unrequested blast-radius change — correctly. **Decide before fixing:** widening the anchor to `^\s*- \[ \] ` pulls 14 previously-unclassified items into all shipped classes at once, which may produce a burst of advisory output; that is a report-only burst (nothing auto-ticks) but it should be measured, not sprung. Contract a test would verify: an indented `  - [ ]` item with a `children:` marker whose children are all `[x]` is reported UMBRELLA-READY; and the previously-suppressed col-0 behaviour is unchanged. Relates id:46f6 (typed edges), id:1b1a (md-merge fail-open), id:b3ee (the original --shipped detector). `docs/meeting-notes/2026-07-10-1430-typed-ledger-edges-umbrella-closure.md` <!-- id:431f -->

- [x] [HARD — pool] ** Rehaul the cross-project inbox: an UNVERSIONED store with a DESTRUCTIVE resolve has no recovery path** (user 2026-07-10, after id:411d surfaced a live near-miss) — `~/.claude/todo-inbox.md` is **gitignored and untracked** (`~/.claude/.gitignore:42`), deliberately local-only because it names private repos. `append.sh inbox-done` is **vanish-on-resolve** (deletes the line, decided 2026-06-30) and matches on a **bare substring** (id:411d). Compose those three and a single mistyped or over-matching token **permanently destroys a pending routing item with no git twin, no archive, and no audit trail** — nothing to diff, nothing to restore. The stated safety premise ("the durable record is the committed `routed:XXXX` breadcrumb in the *target* repo") holds only when the delete is CORRECT; a WRONG delete removes the one copy of an item that was never written anywhere else. Today: 8 open items, and **3 non-conforming prose lines that `inbox-done` can never resolve** and no scanner can route — they will sit there forever. **CORRECTION 2026-07-10, same day, observed live:** direction (a) is **ALREADY SHIPPED for the AUTOMATIC path**. `relay/scripts/scan-routed.sh --apply` (id:678e slice 2) writes the `- [ ] [INBOUND routed:XXXX from <repo>]` stub into the target TODO via flock'd `md-merge.py`, commits it with `commit-ledger.sh`, and only THEN marks `inbox-done` — write-before-delete, idempotent. Witnessed this session: a parallel run drained all 9 open items into their targets (this repo ×2, chidiai ×3, loderite ×3, toesnail ×1) while I worked; my three chidiAI cases landed as INBOUND stubs and the inbox went to zero. I briefly read that as data loss — it was the mechanism working. **So the REAL residual is narrower than first written:** the risk lives on the **MANUAL `inbox-done XXXX`** path, which deletes with (i) no check that a twin exists in the target, and (ii) a bare-substring match (id:411d). That is the path a human/LLM invokes by hand — I used it twice this session. **Directions to weigh (decide the ordering):** (a) **make manual `inbox-done` refuse unless the target twin exists** (the auto path already proves the twin first) — or delete the manual verb entirely and route every resolve through `scan-routed.sh --apply`, which would dissolve this item. Evaluate FIRST. (b) back the inbox with an existing PRIVATE git repo — `~/.claude/projects` is itself a git repo with hourly `sessions(<host>)` auto-commits and is gitignored from `~/.claude`, so relocating the file there buys free history + recovery while staying unpublished (the same trick that made the memory-index before-state free, id:2e6d). (c) non-destructive resolve — move the line to a local `todo-inbox.archive.md` instead of deleting. (d) anchored, unique matching (**id:411d**, do this regardless — it is the cheap interim guard). (e) a conformance rule forbidding one item's prose from citing another's `routed:` token — weakest, guards a symptom. **Constraints:** must never be committed to a PUBLIC repo ([[feedback-no-leak-specifics-in-public-files]]); public-repo scripts must keep taking the path by injection, never hardcode it; keep the flock. Cousins: id:3947 (routed dead letters — same layer), id:678e (auto-reconcile, report-only), id:1b1a + id:0d58 (the same fail-open-substring class, which argues for ONE shared anchoring helper). Prefer *dissolving structurally over guarding* — see the design heuristics. — **DECIDED 2026-07-11 (meeting 1239, D4):** (b) relocate the inbox store into git-tracked `~/.claude/projects` FIRST (free history/recovery, stays private — dissolves "unversioned/no-recovery"), THEN (a-guard) make manual `inbox-done` refuse unless the target twin exists. Drop (c) archive-move (git subsumes it) + (e) conformance; (d)/411d shipped. Injection contract + flock unchanged; never hardcode the path in a public script. — 🔁 RE-LANED `[INPUT — meeting]`→`[HARD — pool]` 2026-07-14 (relay human): the design meeting already happened (mtg 1239 D4, 2026-07-11) and settled (b)+(a-guard) — the `[INPUT — meeting]` tag was stale and kept re-surfacing this as needing a session it already had. Now pool-executable: relocate the store into git-tracked `~/.claude/projects` (idempotent migration on first use) + add the twin-check to manual `inbox-done`; no secrets/hardware, verifiable by tests. — ✅ DONE 2026-07-14 (/meeting session): (b) inbox store relocated to git-tracked `~/.claude/projects/todo-inbox.md` (idempotent first-use migration under flock, `RELAY_INBOX`-injectable; committed hourly to the private `zomni/sessions` branch — versioned but unpublished) + (a-guard) manual `inbox-done` now REFUSES (exit 3) unless the target `routed:TOKEN` twin exists in its TODO/ROADMAP. Touched `meeting/append.sh` (resolve_inbox + resolve_target + twin-check; also fixed the `-t inbox` append path's latent RELAY_INBOX gap), `relay/scripts/scan-routed.sh` (mirrored resolver), docs (SKILL.md 7b, projects/SKILL.md, human.md, global CLAUDE.md), tests `test_inbox_done_twin_guard.sh` + `test_inbox_relocation_migrate.sh` (+ adapted `test_inbox_done_anchor.sh`); full suite green. <!-- children:411d --> <!-- id:9fdb -->

- [x] **`REVIEW_ME.md` accumulates closed `[x]` items with NO archiver — extend the archive treatment to it** (user 2026-07-14). **DONE 2026-07-15** (relay handoff→exec→review, ROADMAP twin [x]): `archive-closed.sh` now archives REVIEW_ME.md → REVIEW_ME.archive.md (3rd ledger, no twin-safety, header protected); tests green + apex-reviewed. Periodic-trigger WIRING remains id:046a. Confirmed: `REVIEW_ME.md` (the human review queue) holds 15 closed `[x]` items / ~20 KB and is covered by NO archiver — `todo-update/archive-done.sh` is TODO-only, `relay/scripts/roadmap-archive.sh` (id:6b67) is ROADMAP-only, and the twin-safe `relay/scripts/archive-closed.sh` (id:046a) archives TODO+ROADMAP but not REVIEW_ME. So resolved review items just pile up, growing the file every reviewer reads. **Fix:** teach `archive-closed.sh` to also drain closed REVIEW_ME items into a `REVIEW_ME.archive.md` sibling under the same less-conservative gate id:046a defines, then wire via the same id:046a trigger. **Twin-safety nuance:** REVIEW_ME items aren't cross-ledger checkbox twins the way TODO↔ROADMAP are — a REVIEW_ME entry often *references* an `id:` whose real ledger home is TODO/ROADMAP (e.g. "id:de4e ticked in ROADMAP"), so the twin-safe rule doesn't map 1:1; a closed REVIEW_ME line is a settled human-review record, safe to archive on its own `[x]`. Do NOT prune section headings (`# Human review queue`, budget marker). **Other files audited (2026-07-14):** TODO.md ✓ (archive-done.sh), ROADMAP.md ✓ (roadmap-archive.sh / archive-closed.sh), REVIEW_ME.md ✗ (this item). `RELAY_LOG.md` is DELIBERATELY exempt — its header declares `merge=union; append-only — never edit or reorder past entries`; it is a log, not a checkbox ledger, and is meant to grow unbounded (its `[x]` matches are inside prose, not archivable items). No other git-tracked `.md` accumulates checkbox items except meeting-notes (immutable records by design). Sibling of / gated on id:046a. <!-- gated-on:046a --> <!-- id:85d3 -->
- [x] **ROADMAP archiver orphans column-0 prose/blockquote bodies + leaves then-empty headers behind** (another project session, reported 2026-07-14). **DONE 2026-07-15** (relay handoff→exec→review, ROADMAP twin [x]): both `roadmap-archive.sh` + `archive-closed.sh` now capture column-0 prose/blockquote (boundary-model) and MOVE emptied transient headers into the archive adjacent to their items (user-decided; H1 + {Items,Current,Done,Backlog} protected); tests green + apex-reviewed + real-world reproduction confirmed. Two defects, both reproduced 2026-07-14 with a fixture (`scratchpad/rmtest`): **(1) prose/blockquote orphaning — `relay/scripts/roadmap-archive.sh` only.** Its `is_continuation()` treats a body line as part of the `[x]` item block *only if the line is blank or indented*; a **column-0 prose paragraph or `>` blockquote line** (common in ROADMAP item bodies — Session-note / Done-check / Context prose is frequently un-indented) is NOT recognised, so when the `- [x]` header moves to `ROADMAP.archive.md` its own body text is **left stranded in `ROADMAP.md`** under the now-item-less heading. Verified: item's header archived, its "This prose…" + "> blockquote" lines stayed behind — the record is split across two files, and the orphan prose then looks like dangling garbage. **`archive-closed.sh` (id:046a) does NOT have this bug** — its block boundary is "next top-level bullet OR heading", so it captures column-0 prose/blockquote correctly; the fix for roadmap-archive.sh is to adopt the same boundary rule (or consolidate the two archivers — see id:046a's "subsume/retire roadmap-archive.sh" open question). **(2) then-empty headers left behind — BOTH archivers.** After every item under a `##`/`###` grouping heading is archived, the emptied heading is deliberately left in place ("ROADMAP headers are structural — no section pruning"). The reporting session hit ROADMAP files littered with empty headers. This is a **design-judgment fork, not a clear bug:** some ROADMAP headings ARE persistent structure (e.g. a standing `## Current work`) and must NOT be pruned, but transient grouping headers (e.g. `## Review batch 2026-07-06`) should go with their items. Resolution options: (a) port `archive-done.sh`'s empty-non-protected-section pruning (name-protect a small set, prune the rest); (b) move the emptied heading INTO the archive alongside its block ("archived together with their items" — the reporter's phrasing) so the archive keeps the grouping context; (c) leave as-is and treat empty headers as acceptable. Decide (a)/(b)/(c) before coding — pairs with id:046a's consolidation decision and the [[todo-prune]] name-protection design. **Scope:** fix (1) mechanically (RED test: a `[x]` item with a column-0 prose line + a `>` blockquote → both move to archive, nothing stranded); (2) is gated on the a/b/c judgment. Relates to id:6b67 (built roadmap-archive.sh), id:046a (archive-closed consolidation), `tests/test_roadmap_archive.sh`. <!-- id:546b -->
- [x] [ROUTINE] **Relay pre-integrate isolation gate — a spawned child can write to the target's MAIN checkout instead of its own worktree** (observed 2026-07-14, loderite R2 consumer handoff). DISTINCT from the c6c8 `isolation:worktree`-param hazard: the child correctly RAN `git worktree add …` but then wrote every edit to `~/src/loderite` (repo-root-relative paths / never `cd`-ing into the worktree). Its worktree stayed empty (0 commits ahead of base), so its "commit in worktree" was a no-op and its self-report was wrong; the agent then wrongly concluded its new tests "weren't collected" (it had run `npm test` in the empty worktree). Cost: the whole handoff's RED specs landed loose in the main checkout, mixed with an unrelated in-flight edit, and had to be reconciled by hand. **RECOMMENDED FIX (3 parts):** (1) **PROMPT** — the child instruction must force the worktree as its working dir (absolute worktree path in every Read/Write/Edit, or make it cwd) and explicitly forbid touching the main checkout; fold this into the invariant-4 child-spawn boilerplate. (2) **INTEGRATOR GATE (mechanize — the load-bearing fix)** — a shared `scripts/verify-isolation.sh <worktree>` the integrator (invariant 5) runs BEFORE merging: assert `git -C <worktree> log --oneline origin/main..HEAD` is non-empty AND `git -C <worktree> status` is clean; if the worktree is empty while the target's main checkout is dirty with the child's work, isolation FAILED → fail loud, do not merge an empty branch. (3) **RECOVERY doctrine** — when it happens the work is usually sound but mislocated: finish/commit it in the MAIN checkout under the held lease (the id:15d5 pattern), salvage beats discard+re-run. Relates c6c8 (sibling isolation hazard), 15d5 (main-checkout-under-lease), d2cd (lock/hazard umbrella), the invariant-4 worktree spawn step + invariant-5 integrate step. **PROMOTED to ROADMAP 2026-07-15** (handoff run relay-20260715-121544-12169, single-id-two-views): [ROUTINE] with a functional RED spec `tests/test_verify_isolation.sh`; load-bearing deliverable = `relay/scripts/verify-isolation.sh` (fix part 2), parts 1+3 fold in as doc edits. <!-- id:f682 -->
- [x] **Make relay worktree/branch cleanup FORCE-FREE (respect a strict no-force policy)** (user 2026-07-15) — the relay pool's serialized integrator + cleanup runs **`git worktree remove --force`** (and relies on `git branch -D`), which BREAKS under a strict destructive-op guardrail (a user policy blocking `--force` / `rm -f` / `git reset --hard` / force-delete — see `docs/destructive-op-guardrail.md`): the force op is denied, the worktree isn't removed, and inert debris (orphaned worktrees + branches) accumulates **every run**. Worse, a `TaskStop`'d pool parks orphan worktrees that ALSO need `--force`/`rm -rf` to dispose. **FIX:** make cleanup force-free — before `git worktree remove` (NO `--force`), bring the worktree to a clean state (executors already commit per contract; for stray untracked/build files `git stash` or a scoped clean of KNOWN-throwaway paths), then remove without `--force`; on a genuine can't-remove, **surface it, never force**. Prefer `git worktree remove` (no flag) + fallback-to-stash over the defensive `--force`. Also audit ALL relay scripts (integrator recipe in SKILL.md §5, `relay-reconcile.sh`, `ckpt-tag.sh`, `git-lock-push.sh`) + `git-diary-workflow` for `--force`/`-f`/`reset --hard`/`rm -rf`/`branch -D`, and provide force-free paths or gate them behind explicit human confirmation (never auto — the `force-push.sh` confirm-gate pattern is the model). **Goal: a relay run under a strict no-force policy leaves NO debris and never ATTEMPTS a forbidden op.** Relates `docs/destructive-op-guardrail.md`, id:0902 (repo lease), the D1 orphan-park (id:689c), `relay-reconcile.sh`. **DONE 2026-07-15** (meeting `docs/meeting-notes/2026-07-15-2227-relay-force-free-cleanup.md`, D1–D7): new force-free `relay/scripts/worktree-retire.sh` (plain `worktree remove` → `branch -d`/park; dirty ⇒ surface+leave, never force) called from reconcile reap/park + the relay-loop integrator prompt; `relay-reconcile.sh` integrate now `branch -d`, its human `--discard` gated behind `RELAY_DISCARD_CONFIRM=1` (force-push.sh model); executor contract v7→v8 clean-worktree exit gate (rule 5b) + review.md anti-gaming check; `git stash drop`/`clear` added to the guardrail; single-file `rm -f` de-cargo-culted repo-wide + `tools/check-no-bare-rm-f.sh` regression lint; `make test` 246/0. <!-- id:373e -->
- [x] **Exempt contract-pointer-refresh (meta) commits from `substantive_unaudited`** (user 2026-07-15) — a version bump (contract v7→v8) makes every managed repo's `## Relay contract vN` pointer stale; refreshing it lands a CLAUDE.md-pointer-only commit that `gather-repo-state.sh`'s substantive filter (only `uv.lock`-exempt) counted as substantive → each repo re-classified "needs review" and burned a strong review turn for pure meta (observed zkm-pdf). FIX: content-based exemption — a commit whose only changed lines are the `relay-executor contract vN` pointer is meta-only (mirrors the id:bae5 uv.lock exemption). `tests/test_gather_substantive_pointer_exempt.sh`. DONE 2026-07-15. <!-- id:fbbf -->
- [x] **Make the executor refactor step a LOUD claim (TDD rigor)** (user 2026-07-15, meeting `docs/meeting-notes/2026-07-15-2348-tdd-rigor-refactor-step.md`) — executor contract rule 2/4 now requires a `refactor:` self-report line (what was cleaned up, or `none needed — <reason>`; blank fails, like empty `# swallow-ok:`); `review.md` §2b check 6 flags it only when the diff visibly contradicts "none needed"; `handoff.md` C3 gains a triangulation sentence; contract bumped v8→v9. Refactoring is unverifiable *by construction* (behaviour-preserving) so it's a forcing-function, not a proof. DONE 2026-07-15. <!-- id:108e -->
- [x] **git-annex worktrees can NEVER be retired — `worktree-retire.sh` refuses forever and misreports it as "dirty"** — FIXED 2026-07-16 (same session). Root cause proven empirically: the `.git` symlink appears **immediately at `git worktree add`**, before any annex command — it is `filter.annex.process` (the smudge filter), NOT the annex `post-checkout` hook (hypothesis tested and DISPROVED: hooks-disabled still produced the symlink; zkWhale sets `core.hooksPath=tools/git-hooks`, so the annex hook was never active). Confirmed by construction: `worktree add` with the annex filters disabled yields a proper `gitdir:` file that removes cleanly. **Prevention at add-time REJECTED** — disabling the smudge filter checks annexed files out as raw pointer files; zkWhale annexes 4 snapshot JSONs and its relay items touch `snapshot:predeploy`, so a child could commit pointers as content. The symlink is deliberate annex design (annexed relative symlinks `../.git/annex/objects/...` must resolve inside the worktree), so fighting it at creation is wrong. **Fix shipped at the RETIREMENT side instead:** `worktree-retire.sh` now normalizes a symlinked `.git` back to git's own standard `gitdir:` pointer before removal — guarded (only when it resolves to THIS repo's own admin dir for THIS worktree; anything else surfaces untouched), non-destructive (rewrites only the pointer; admin dir/index/tree untouched), and force-free (plain `rm --`, no `-f`). The dirty check still runs after and still wins, so it cannot become a backdoor. Also fixed the reporting bug: `git worktree remove`'s stderr was swallowed (`2>&1` → `/dev/null`), which is what made a structural failure masquerade as "dirty" on a provably clean tree for ~5 weeks; the real git error is now surfaced verbatim. Also fixed the malformed double-`-C` in the deferral message. Covered by 2 new hermetic tests in `tests/test_worktree_retire.sh` (annex layout → retires; annex layout + dirty → still surfaced+left, work intact) incl. a vacuity guard asserting stock git really refuses. Reclaimed 1.2G (3 zkWhale worktrees retired, all clean+merged). Suite green 247/0. Relates id:373e (force-free retirement), id:6e02 (single-target scope), [[no-swallow-stderr]]. <!-- id:de4a -->
- [x] **`verify-isolation.sh` is documented as invariant 5's gate but is NEVER wired into the pool — and its documented path is repo-relative** — **DONE 2026-07-16** (handoff C2/C3 + Sonnet executor + apex review, merged `main`): wired as integrator step 1a in `relay-loop.js` (absolute `~/.claude/skills/...` path + explicit ABORT-on-non-zero, mirroring step 1's clean-tree gate), and both docs corrected to the absolute form. Blocker found and solved during C3: wiring the gate **as built** would have regressed id:8e3e — it exited 2 on an empty worktree, but a zero-commit branch is a *legitimate* completed review (child audited, found nothing), and handing back there re-dispatches forever (observed 3× 2026-07-01). "Worktree empty" is the signature of BOTH a legit no-op AND a breach. Owner ratified the **main-HEAD discriminator**: `base = merge-base(worktree, main)` IS the dispatch-time main HEAD, so breach = `empty && main advanced by a NON-merge commit` — needs no pool plumbing. `--first-parent` keeps a sibling unit's merged branch commits from reading as a breach. Accepted false-positive documented, not chased: a no-op review racing a supervised id:15d5 direct-to-main commit defers (conservative, costs a re-dispatch). The gate ran live for the first time at this very integration and cleared its own author. **Root cause of the original gap** (worth remembering): id:f682's design *said* "the integrator runs it" but its acceptance criteria only tested the SCRIPT's behaviour and never asserted a CALL SITE — so the executor built it, passed every test, and ticked the box legitimately while the wiring existed only as prose. `tests/test_isolation_gate_wired.sh` therefore asserts the wiring first (incl. that the call precedes `merge --no-ff` — a gate after the merge guards nothing). Suite 248/0. See also chidiai `2026-07-16-ratified-decision-applied-to-the-prose-not-the-executable-spec` (same disease, filed independently the same day). Relates id:f682, id:8e3e, id:c6c8, id:15d5, id:c5ed, id:93bd (the concurrent-suite gap found while integrating this). <!-- id:7612 -->
- [x] **OWNER LOOK — yinyang-puzzle id:e01f landed by cherry-pick, not `--no-ff` merge** — **ACCEPTED by owner 2026-07-16, this instance only** (not a precedent for future cherry-pick integrations), on the stated condition that the outcome matched what the merge would have produced. **Condition VERIFIED before acceptance:** the pre-cherry-pick original survives as unreachable commit `b07cfcc`; its tree `e660e850…` is **byte-identical** to what landed (`0bb1da4`), and both share parent `90240be` — main had not diverged, so a `--no-ff` merge would have wrapped that exact tree. `git diff b07cfcc 0bb1da4` is empty. Only *topology* was lost (no merge commit recording branch ancestry), not content. Owner context: this was a session with background agents working alongside foreground plumbing — corroborated by the unreachable objects, which show an Agent-tool `worktree-agent-af63a8aab76d916c5` worktree plus `WIP on main` stashes. Root cause of the cherry-pick itself was not pursued (accepted as a one-off). <!-- id:388f --> Reflog is decisive: `0bb1da4 main@{2026-07-15 17:06:31}: cherry-pick: feat(etch): consistent shared-edge arc-count etching (id:e01f)`. The child DID work in an isolated worktree (so `verify-isolation.sh` would have passed — this is **not** an isolation breach), but the integrator cherry-picked onto main instead of `--no-ff` merging, then deleted the branch (no ref survives). Related: commit `bc58a8f`'s subject claims "verify + **merge** arc_etch" but git shows no merge — the message is inaccurate though the work landed. **Cannot be adjudicated mechanically:** RELAY_LOG labels e01f's executor `(claude-sonnet, relay-loop)` while its handoff entry says `reviewer handoff (claude-opus-4-8, Q4-mockup session)` — a supervised session, and per id:15d5 supervised direct-to-main writes are legitimate by design. So this is either a merge-protocol deviation or a sanctioned supervised integration; **the distinction is the owner's call**, not a delegated verdict. If it was the pool, the `--no-ff` invariant (guardrail 1) was bypassed and that wants a root-cause. <!-- id:388f --> `zkWhale` is the **only** `git-annex` repo across all own repos (`annex.uuid` set; no other repo has `.git/annex`), and it was the **only** repo with lingering relay worktrees (3 dirs, **1.2G**, from runs 07-04/07-10/07-11 — all clean, all fully merged, holding no unmerged work). **Mechanism:** git-annex replaces a linked worktree's `.git` **file** with a **symlink** to the admin dir, so `git worktree remove` fails validation permanently: `fatal: validation failed, cannot remove working tree: '<wt>/.git' is not a .git file, error code 10`. This is structural — `--force` would not help (validation precedes it), so no force-free *or* forced retirement can ever succeed. Every relay run on an annex repo therefore leaks a worktree forever. **Second defect (reporting):** `worktree-retire.sh` maps this to `exit 3 "worktree dirty/unremovable — LEFT on disk"` and advises "commit real work / gitignore throwaway" — actively misleading, since `git status --porcelain` is EMPTY. It conflates a dirty tree (a correct surface-and-leave) with a structural failure (a permanent leak that no amount of committing fixes). **Fix candidates:** (a) detect the symlink-`.git` case explicitly and emit a distinct exit code + accurate message naming git-annex; (b) decide the sanctioned repair — restoring the standard `gitdir:` file makes the canonical removal work, but that is git-internals surgery and was DENIED by the destructive-op guardrail when attempted here (correctly — it forces past a safety gate), so it needs an explicit owner decision + a guardrail-legible path; (c) consider whether relay should worktree annex repos at all. **NOTE:** the 3 zkWhale worktrees are still on disk (1.2G unreclaimed) pending (b). Cosmetic: the deferral message prints a malformed `git -C <repo> -C <worktree> status` (double `-C`). Relates id:373e (force-free retirement), id:6e02 (single-target scope). <!-- id:de4a -->
- [x] **`test_isolation_gate_wired.sh` was FLAKY: a >64 KB `printf | grep -q` under `set -o pipefail` spuriously red-lit the whole suite** (found + fixed 2026-07-16 by the relay review child, run relay-20260716-125514-23493) — the test piped `$loop_code` (relay-loop.js, 91697 B) into `grep -q`. The pipe buffer is 65536 B, so `printf` BLOCKS mid-write while `grep -q` matches at byte 63529 and exits FIRST; `printf` then dies of SIGPIPE (141) and `pipefail` promotes that to a pipeline failure — **even though grep matched** (proven: `rc=141 PIPESTATUS=[0]`). Pure race: measured **1/25 idle, 22/40 under CPU load**. Blast radius was the definition-of-done gate itself — a spurious red that indicts the id:7612 isolation gate rather than the test, exactly the signal the suite exists to make trustworthy. **Fixed**: herestrings (no early-exiting reader ⇒ no SIGPIPE) + `grep -m1` for `| head -1`; 0/60 after, and all 3 mutations (gate call removed / repo-relative path / ABORT stripped) still correctly caught, so the spec was not weakened. Note the diagnosis needs a real `bash` — the agent Bash tool is `zsh`, where `PIPESTATUS` is empty and the race does not reproduce. <!-- id:b780 -->
- [x] **`unpromoted-scan.sh` twin check is a BARE SUBSTRING grep → any prose mention of a token silently HIDES that item from the backlog scan** (found 2026-07-16 by the zkm handoff child, which self-inflicted then caught it). Line 262: `grep -qF "id:$token" <<<"$roadmap_content" && continue` — matched against ALL of ROADMAP.md, not anchored to an item's own trailing `<!-- id:XXXX -->` marker. So a C2 author writing explanatory prose like "…separate seam tracked as id:2b63" in ROADMAP.md makes 2b63 register as *already twinned* → it drops out of the scan entirely (observed: `df4e` went `laned` → 0 rows). This is EXACTLY the id:2dea hidden-backlog failure mode the scan exists to prevent, reachable by ordinary prose. Same hazard class as the `inbox-done` substring match ([[todo-routing]]) and md-merge's fail-open append ([[md-merge-1b1a]]); `scan-routed.sh` already anchors its twin check to the marker for this reason — unpromoted-scan never got the same fix. **Fix:** anchor to the trailing-marker form (mirror scan-routed's anchoring), and add a test asserting a prose `id:XXXX` mention does NOT twin the item. Interim convention (applied in zkm ROADMAP 4dd5a02): name tokens BARE in prose ("token df4e"), reserve `id:XXXX` for an item's own marker — a convention is not a guard, hence this item. <!-- id:1312 -->
- [x] **`scan-routed.sh` prints "(DRY-RUN)" on a real apply run (label-only bug)** — line 199 uses `echo "=== routed dead-letters — APPLY mode${DRY_RUN:+' (DRY-RUN)'} ==="`, but `${VAR:+...}` expands when the var is **non-empty**, and the default is `DRY_RUN=0` — a non-empty string. So the header claims DRY-RUN on **every** run, including `--apply` runs that genuinely write + commit. The write-gating itself is CORRECT (lines 297/329 test `-eq 1`), so this is cosmetic — but it makes an apply run's output read as a no-op preview, which is exactly backwards for an audit trail (observed 2026-07-16: header said DRY-RUN while the run committed 5 INBOUND stubs across puzzle-pwa/toesnail/zkWhale/dotclaude-skills). Fix: `$([[ $DRY_RUN -eq 1 ]] && echo ' (DRY-RUN)')` or equivalent. Cheap; add a test asserting the apply-mode header does NOT contain DRY-RUN. <!-- id:d515 -->
- [x] **relay-loop: a suppressed/surfaced repo must not count as "backlog drained"** — SHIPPED 2026-07-17 (executor session; `tests/test_relay_loop_drain_vs_blocked.sh` green, full suite green). split out of id:1735 2026-07-17, DISTINCT root (id:1735's "stale discovery snapshot" hypothesis is FALSIFIED). Run `relay-20260717-100452-13146`: `reconcile-repo.sh:192` (id:1f53 orphan suppress-redispatch) correctly refused to re-dispatch loderite for 4c02's parked partial work, `add_surfaced` surfaced the whole REPO → classify skipped → 2 dry rounds → "backlog drained" while 8 open `[ROUTINE]` items sat there; `classifyDrainBacklog` had no bucket for the suppression reason (logged bare "1 other") and `stopReason` was never set. Fix specced as `[ROUTINE]` in ROADMAP (id:4ca8): dry-round predicate must see `surfaced`/suppressed work as non-dry, add a `suppressed` bucket, always set `stopReason`. Scope guard: do NOT touch `reconcile-repo.sh`'s suppression behaviour (id:1f53 works as designed); the whole-repo blast radius of one parked orphan stalling 7 unrelated items is a separate `/meeting` question, not specced here. Relates id:1f53, id:689c, id:1735. <!-- id:4ca8 -->
- [x] **[INBOUND routed:f1f5] `roadmap-lint.sh` id extraction is an UNANCHORED first-match grep** (`id_re='id:[0-9a-fA-F]{4}'`, lines 217/323/447) — it captures the FIRST `id:XXXX` on the line, not the canonical trailing `<!-- id:XXXX -->`. Two confirmed symptoms: (1) MISATTRIBUTION — zkWhale ROADMAP id:4148 (prefix cites "dep: id:1643") reports its violation as `[id:1643]`, sending a human to the wrong item; (2) FALSE-NEGATIVE (reproduced hermetically) — an item with NO own id token passes clause-2 clean if its prose cites any other id, so the loud-reject never fires. Fix: anchor to the canonical `<!-- id:XXXX -->` comment (prefer LAST match / comment-anchored). **Same defect class as id:1312** (unpromoted-scan) — both are unanchored token greps over prose-bearing lines; consider fixing them together and factoring one shared anchored-extraction helper rather than a third copy. Ingested 2026-07-16 by the relay review child from the cross-project inbox dead-letter (relay-doctor `scan-routed` finding); `routed:f1f5` twin retained so `append.sh inbox-done f1f5` can resolve it. — **SHIPPED 2026-07-17**, commit `2429c32`: all 5 sites anchored to the trailing `<!-- id:XXXX -->` marker via a new shared `relay/scripts/lib-anchored-id.sh`. Covered by `tests/test_roadmap_lint_id_anchoring.sh` (green; full suite 253 passed / 0 failed / 0 expected-red, re-verified by the apex session, not just self-reported). **Partial on the factoring ask:** `scan-routed.sh`/`unpromoted-scan.sh` were deliberately NOT converted onto the helper (they answer "does this KNOWN token appear as some line's own marker" — a different shape from "extract the UNKNOWN owning id from this line"); rationale recorded in the lib header + RELAY_LOG. So this is three copies → two, not → one; a full unification is a follow-up, not part of this. Inbox `routed:f1f5` resolved via `append.sh inbox-done f1f5`. <!-- id:521f -->
- [x] [ROUTINE] **`make status` already detects install drift per-file — nothing ever RUNS it, so a stale install broke `roadmap-lint.sh` for every consumer** — surfaced by the user 2026-07-17 from a loderite session ("roadmap-lint.sh is broken independently of this repo — it sources lib-anchored-id.sh, which doesn't exist in ~/.claude/skills/relay/scripts/"). **INCIDENT (verified, reproduced, now FIXED):** `roadmap-lint.sh` sources `lib-anchored-id.sh`; the latter existed in the repo (added 2026-07-17 10:51) but not in the installed tree, so the lint died with `No such file or directory` and cost a loderite session a broken lint plus a false "relay-machinery bug" diagnosis. Measured drift was **62 installed vs 64 declared**, the delta being exactly `lib-anchored-id.sh` + `handback-summary.mjs`. Fixed in-session by `make install-relay`; `roadmap-lint.sh` now exits 0. Relay was the only stale skill (meeting / git-diary-workflow / todo-update / projects all verified in sync). **TWO FALSIFIED HYPOTHESES, recorded not deleted (both were mine, both were asserted before checking — the checks took one command each):** (1) *"the hand-maintained `relay_FILES` enumeration silently drops new files"* — **FALSE**: both files ARE correctly listed, and a hermetic `make DEST_DIR=$(mktemp -d) install-relay` installs all 64. The Makefile is CORRECT; there is no list to fix and no derive-from-glob redesign to do. (2) *"`make status` reports per-skill, not per-file, so it could not have caught a 2-of-64 gap"* — **FALSE**: `make status` honors `DEST_DIR` AND reports per file; against a simulated drop it prints the exact line `!! scripts/lib-anchored-id.sh (not installed)`. **ACTUAL ROOT CAUSE:** the detector already exists and is already precise — **nothing invokes it**. The repo is consumed through per-file symlinks in `~/.claude`, so any new file is invisible to every consumer until a human remembers `make install`, and the failure surfaces loudly only later, in an unrelated repo's session. This is the SAME class as [[id:de36]] (`todo-conformance.sh` correctly flagged the acc7 line; nothing ran it) and the same rule: CLAUDE.md mechanize-first — *a loud detector whose invocation is optional is not a detector*. **Scope: wire the existing check into a routine flow — do NOT build a new detector.** Recommended home: `relay/scripts/relay-doctor.sh` (`/relay health`, id:9bec), which already exists to answer "is this environment sound?" — an install-drift check is exactly its remit. **A drift test in `tests/` is NOT the answer**: CLAUDE.md §Testing requires tests be hermetic and never touch `~/.claude`, so a suite test cannot read the real install tree; the hermetic half (does `make install-<skill>` install everything declared?) already passes today and would NOT have caught this. The check must inspect the LIVE environment, which is `relay-doctor`'s job, not the suite's. **Mandatory wrinkle — the guard must not cry wolf:** `make status` currently reports one legitimate `!!` — `meeting-cross: SKILL.md (not installed)` — because that alias skill is **deliberately** uninstalled pending deletion ([[id:4f5f]]). Any drift check MUST treat deprecated/intentionally-absent skills as expected, or it fires on day one and everyone learns to ignore it. See `docs/meeting-notes/2026-07-17-1450-acc7-phantom-append-write-integrity.md` (session context); relates [[edit-canonical]] — canonical edits are correct but only reach consumers via an install step nothing enforces. <!-- id:1102 -->
- [x] [ROUTINE] **`make test` RED — bare `rm -f` in `meeting/append.sh:384` fails `check-no-bare-rm-f.sh --enforce`** — REVIEW FINDING 2026-07-17. `febb0c3` (integrate id:34c2) added `rm -f -- "$tmp_check"` (a known-present `mktemp` file), breaking the repo's own lint (baseline 0) so `make test` aborts at the lint tier. Unit tier green (256 passed). Fix: `rm -- "$tmp_check"` per CLAUDE.md destructive-op hygiene. Done-check: `make test` green. Execution twin in ROADMAP.md (single-id-two-views). <!-- id:a286 -->
- [x] **BUG — relay-loop returns `handbacks: []` while its OWN event log records a handback: a parked orphan is silently dropped from the run summary** — user 2026-07-17 ("file the bug"), found only because a human happened to run `/relay reconcile .`. REPRODUCED END-TO-END on run `relay-20260717-100452-13146` (loderite, `/relay --intensive .`): the execute child fixed `id:4c02` (portrait menu overflow), committed `ea0a7c5`, and the integrator hit a **ROADMAP.md merge conflict**, correctly `git merge --abort`ed, and parked the branch as `relay/orphan/relay-20260717-100452-13146-execute`. The event log captured it perfectly — `{"kind":"handback","repo":"loderite","mode":"execute","reason":"Merge conflict in ROADMAP.md when running `git merge --no-ff …` (RELAY_LOG.md auto-merged fine). Ran `git merge --abort`; main is clean… left on disk for supervised reconcile"}` — **but the Workflow's returned summary said `completed:[{mode:"review"}], handbacks:[], queuedRemaining:[]`**, i.e. the run reported total success while a green, wanted fix sat unmerged on a parked ref. The front door prints "the HANDBACK count" from that summary (SKILL.md step 4), so the operator is told **zero**. This is precisely the CLAUDE.md *"a deterministic detector that FINDS a problem but whose resolution silently no-ops"* anti-pattern, in its most expensive form: detection was loud AND durable, the summary silently swallowed it. VERIFIED the parked work is genuine, not gamed: `tests/menu-scroll.test.ts` (the `4c02` RED spec) is **4-failed on main, 4-passed on the branch**; the diff implements the two `throw new Error("not implemented (roadmap:4c02)")` stubs in `src/hud/menu-layout.ts` and wires them into `src/menu.ts`. FIX: the returned summary MUST be derived from (or reconciled against) the same event stream that recorded the handback — a `kind:"handback"` event with no corresponding entry in `handbacks[]` should be impossible; consider asserting that invariant in `relay-loop.js` before return, and failing loudly if it trips. ROOT CAUSE of the conflict itself (separate but co-dispatched): the pool ran an **execute AND a review for the SAME repo in the same run** — the execute child ticked `4c02`'s box while the review child rewrote `ROADMAP.md` to promote `7ccf`+`a78b`, and ROADMAP.md is explicitly NOT `merge=union` (checkbox toggles can't union — SKILL.md Guardrails). So an execute+review pair on one repo is a near-guaranteed ledger collision; the D3 class order (execute→review) sequences the *dispatch* but nothing stops both landing in one run against a shared ledger. THIRD SYMPTOM from the same run (may be the same root or separate — **do not assume**): the loop then exited on "two consecutive empty discoveries (backlog drained)" while **8 open executor-actionable `[ROUTINE]` items** sat in ROADMAP.md (incl. `4c02` itself, whose fix was parked) — a discovery snapshot taken before the review's promotions landed. Same family as the `handoff-check-todo-not-roadmap-closure` lesson (a ledger read one step behind reality), but within a single run. - [x] [ROUTINE] **append.sh inbox write-path integrity — validate, mint-inside, echo-what-was-written** — decided 2026-07-17 (meeting `docs/meeting-notes/2026-07-17-1450-acc7-phantom-append-write-integrity.md`, D2/D3). `routed:acc7` was minted, reported as filed, and never written: the loderite hand-run escaped `$ID` in the payload but not in its own `echo "filed routed:$ID"`, so bash wrote a literal `$ID` while the receipt said `acc7` — and `loderite/TODO.md:40` (id:0c54) then cited a token that existed nowhere. Root cause is not the escaping: `-t inbox` validates nothing and prints nothing on success (`meeting/append.sh:283-287`), so the caller invents its own receipt (id:1735 class — and the item being filed was *about* that class). Three changes, `-t inbox` only: **(A)** reject a non-conforming entry (non-zero, nothing appended); **(B)** `--route-to <repo> -e "<desc>"` mints + builds + writes the line itself; **(C)** always echo the token *actually written* (minted, or parsed back from the appended line). Fold-in: the mint collision-checks the **routed** namespace via a new `scan-routed-tokens <target>` verb mirroring `scan-ids` — `scan_ids` greps `id:[0-9a-f]{4}` over `<root>` only, so `routed:` tokens are minted unchecked today. Out of scope: restricting the raw `-e` form; validating `discoveries`/`personas`. RED spec `tests/test_inbox_write_integrity.sh` (verified red 2026-07-17 — it reproduces the acc7 incident against current code). Execution twin in ROADMAP.md (single-id-two-views D2). <!-- id:34c2 -->
- [x] [ROUTINE] **Conformance lint at the /meeting inbox surface** — decided 2026-07-17 (meeting note above, D3). `todo-conformance.sh --inbox` correctly flagged the broken acc7 line as `orphan` — the sole non-conformer of 13 — but **nothing routine runs it**: it is reachable only via `scan-routed.sh` (report-only, gated per id:678e) or a manual global-CLAUDE.md step. The detector existed and the defect shipped anyway; a loud detector whose invocation is optional is not a detector. Extend `/meeting` SKILL.md step 7b (which already greps + surfaces the inbox) to run the lint and surface non-conformers under a distinct heading. Surface-only — no auto-fix, no blocking; invoke the detector, never reimplement its regex (cf. `scan-routed.sh:19`). RED spec `tests/test_meeting_inbox_lint_surface.sh` (verified red 2026-07-17). Honest limit: SKILL.md is prose, so the test pins the instruction's presence, not a live meeting's obedience. Execution twin in ROADMAP.md. <!-- id:de36 -->

- [x] **[INPUT — meeting] `CHANGELOG.md` across the fleet — user-facing changes (+ relevant backend), bucketed by sensible time ranges (e.g. daily)** (user 2026-07-16) — "especially for the semver repos but in general". Today **25 own repos carry `v*` tags** (loderite 49 tags, zkm 23, zkm-ner 22, zkm-eml 16, zkWhale 13, … down to 1-tag repos) and **none has a CHANGELOG**: the only per-version record is the tag message + `git log`, which is unreadable as a "what changed for me" summary. Scope per the user: the most important **user-facing** changes, but **also relevant backend/internal** ones (not a strict keep-a-changelog "user-visible only" filter) — and **summarized by sensible buckets / time ranges (e.g. daily)** rather than one line per commit. **Why now:** the 2026-07-16 session bumped loderite 0.40→0.45, chidiai 0.1→0.13, zkm 0.18→0.21 — i.e. **20 releases' worth of closes** landed with no human-readable record of what each contained; the version numbers alone carry no information. **Design questions for the meeting:** (1) **derive vs hand-write** — the relay already knows every close (`workedIds` per integrate, the `relay-ckpt-*` tag messages, RELAY_LOG.md), so a CHANGELOG is arguably *derivable* rather than authored (prefer derive-or-guard per id:415b; a hand-maintained changelog drifts exactly like the diagrams in id:a17a). (2) **bucketing** — daily vs per-release vs both (a release spans days; a day spans repos). (3) **the user-facing/backend split** — one list with tags, or two sections? (4) **who writes it** — this is a **sibling of [[id:e647]]**, which already rules that the version bump is *the reviewer's job at integrate, NOT the executor in a worktree* (parallel worktrees collide on the manifest/lockfile); a changelog entry has the **identical** collision profile, so it should almost certainly ride the same integrate-time hook rather than being an executor duty. Decide e647 and this together. (5) **per-repo vs central** — 25 repos × a CHANGELOG each, or the `claude-diary` as the cross-repo narrative with per-repo files only where a consumer exists? (6) **retroactivity** — generate history from existing tags/RELAY_LOG, or start from now? **NOTE — this REOPENS a ratified decision for THIS repo only:** `CLAUDE.md` §Versioning records "A public-repo `CHANGELOG.md` is **deferred** — trigger is the first external consumer who needs to pin a version for a reproducible install (none exists today; `git log` covers the rest)" (D-A/id:8ef3, meeting 2026-07-12-1030). dotclaude-skills also has **no repo version by design** (git = SSOT), so it is the *weakest* candidate in the fleet. The user's ask is fleet-wide and names the semver repos specifically; it does not obviously overturn 8ef3's reasoning for the manifest-less repo. **Do not silently override 8ef3** — the meeting must either honour it (dotclaude-skills exempt, semver repos adopt) or explicitly amend it with the owner's say.  **DECIDED 2026-07-17 (meeting `docs/meeting-notes/2026-07-17-1541-semver-trigger-and-fleet-changelog.md`, D2/D3/D4 — owner-ratified):** the CHANGELOG is **DERIVED at integrate** from existing relay state (`workedIds`, `relay-ckpt-*` tag messages, `RELAY_LOG.md`), **starting from now — never backfilled** (per-close tags are unrecoverable after the 2026-07-16 batch, so generated history would be plausible and unverifiable). **If it needs a new per-item 'user-facing?' field, this decision is WRONG and must come back** — that is authoring with extra steps. Home: **per-repo CHANGELOG.md in every semver repo**, release-bucketed (D4). **id:8ef3 EXPLICITLY AMENDED (D3)**: dotclaude-skills DOES get a CHANGELOG but still **no version** — date-bucketed per relay integrate, since with no manifest there is no bump to key off. Amendment basis on record: 8ef3 weighed only EXTERNAL consumers and correctly found none; this repo is consumed INTERNALLY via per-file symlinks by every other repo's sessions — a class it never considered. An explicit amendment on an unconsidered premise, NOT a reinterpretation. CLAUDE.md §Versioning updated to match. Ships together with id:e647. READY TO BUILD. **DERIVER LANDED 2026-07-18 (this repo only, stays OPEN):** `relay/scripts/changelog-append.sh` (deterministic, flock'd, atomic; date-bucket for version-less repos + `--version` release-bucket for semver; **opt-in — no-op unless CHANGELOG.md already exists**, which is the D4-safety that keeps it OFF semver repos until e647 bootstraps them) + integrator wiring in `relay-loop.js` step 2b (derive-at-integrate from `report.summary`+worked-ids, scoped-add commit) + this repo's `CHANGELOG.md` bootstrapped date-bucketed per D3 + `tests/test_changelog_derive.sh` (8 cases, green; full suite 258/0). **REMAINS (D4 ships-together):** id:e647 bump-trigger + per-repo `CHANGELOG.md` bootstrap across the semver fleet (release-bucketed). **OWNER SEQUENCING DECISION PENDING:** the deriver is proven for version-less repos; whether to build e647 next (D4 together) or let the deriver bake here first (the recorded Riku/Orla dissent) is the owner's call. **UPDATE 2026-07-18 — MECHANISM COMPLETE (e647 landed, `6c5f84f`, suite 260/0):** the deriver AND the release-bucket wiring are both on main — `version-bump.sh` (step 2a) now supplies `--version` to `changelog-append.sh` (step 2b), so a semver repo release-buckets and a version-less repo date-buckets, automatically at integrate. The b8fa code deliverable (D2 deriver + D4 ship-with-e647) is DONE. **The ONLY remaining piece is operational, not code:** each semver repo opts in the first time its `CHANGELOG.md` is created (the gate is a no-op until then — nothing auto-creates it across the ~25 semver repos). **RECOMMEND: close b8fa (mechanism delivered) and, if the fleet rollout is wanted, file a small follow-up to bootstrap `CHANGELOG.md` in the semver repos.** Left open pending the owner's close/split call. **CLOSED 2026-07-18 (owner-ratified):** mechanism delivered (deriver + e647 bump, both on main). Fleet rollout done directly — a bootstrap tool created a release-bucketed `CHANGELOG.md` in each manifest-bearing own repo (gated: not the active-session repos chidiai/zelegator/loderite/it-infra, clean tree only, no live relay claim, skip if already present). <!-- id:b8fa -->

- [x] **[design] Close the CHANGELOG onboarding loop for FUTURE repos — the opt-in gate means a newly `/relay`-onboarded or newly-semver'd repo does NOT auto-get a changelog** (user 2026-07-18). **DONE 2026-07-18 — owner chose (B):** `changelog-append.sh` now AUTO-CREATES a release-bucketed `CHANGELOG.md` when called with `--version` and the file is absent (a semver repo self-onboards on its FIRST release; e647 supplies the version at integrate). Version-less closes (no `--version`) keep the opt-in no-op, so manifest-less repos (dotclaude-skills, D3) stay a deliberate bootstrap — never auto-created. Retires the D4 opt-in gate for the bump case only (its "don't fire before e647" constraint has lapsed). Integrator step 2b prose updated (commits a newly-created file too); tested `tests/test_changelog_derive.sh` cases (1)/(1b). Relationship to the 19 `routed:` inbox self-onboard items: **complementary, not redundant** — (B) onboards a repo on its FIRST RELEASE; the inbox items let a repo (esp. the active-session ones that may not release soon) onboard EARLIER by hand. They stay valid and drain as adopted. Confirmed 2026-07-18: `changelog-append.sh` hard no-ops without a `CHANGELOG.md` (deliberate D4 opt-in), and nothing in `/relay` handoff/discovery/onboarding creates the file. So `version-bump.sh` WILL start bumping+tagging any manifest repo at integrate, but the changelog stays silent until someone bootstraps `CHANGELOG.md`. Today's 22-repo rollout + the 19 `routed:` inbox self-onboard items (chidiai `73b5` / zelegator `8688` / loderite `1739` + 16 unreleased-manifest) are **one-time catch-up** — the gap reopens for the next new repo. **Decision (owner's — do NOT silently pick):** (A) `/relay handoff` auto-creates `CHANGELOG.md` when it confirms a manifest/semver repo (onboarding closes the loop); (B) the integrator's first bump (step 2a) auto-creates `CHANGELOG.md`, i.e. "the moment a repo first releases, it gets a changelog" — cleanest self-closing loop, but it **dissolves the deliberate opt-in gate** (whose only purpose, D4, was to not fire before e647 shipped — now moot, so this is defensible); (C) keep opt-in + rely on the per-repo inbox pattern forever (manual, drifts). Lean B, but it touches the D4 opt-in semantics so it wants a ratified call. If B: the bootstrap = write the header (release- or date-bucketed by manifest presence) then proceed with the append. See dotclaude-skills `docs/meeting-notes/2026-07-17-1541-semver-trigger-and-fleet-changelog.md`. <!-- id:7d20 -->

- [x] **Drop plan mode from `/meeting` — the `EnterPlanMode` step is vestigial without `/opusplan`** (user 2026-07-17, decided: "file TODO: drop plan mode from meeting"). **DONE 2026-07-18 — PREMISE AMENDED by owner (2026-07-18): not DROPPED, made model-CONDITIONAL.** The filed premise "user does not run /opusplan → drop" was corrected by the owner to "the user MAY use /opusplan, where plan mode upgrades Sonnet→Opus — so keep it, gated on model." Implemented (uniform across subject-mode C2 + C3, owner-chosen): `format.md` §Plan-mode gate — Opus/Fable SKIP `EnterPlanMode`/`ExitPlanMode` (already strong tier; and plan mode's read-only guard blocks background-executor integration), Sonnet/Haiku USE it (opusplan upgrade / read-only guard preserved); SKILL.md step 3 + C2 dispatch + end-of-meeting `ExitPlanMode` all gated; the three stale "post-`ExitPlanMode` tier IS Sonnet" assertions corrected to tier-varies-by-harness; constraints reworded to hold as prose when plan mode is skipped. Tested: `tests/test_meeting_plan_mode_gate.sh`. Landed with [[id:af5a]] (same SKILL.md region). SKILL.md subject-mode step 3 mandates `EnterPlanMode`, and end-of-meeting step 1 mandates `ExitPlanMode`. **Constraint archaeology — what did this dodge, and does it still bind?** Four jobs, of which two are dead and one is redundant now that the user does not run `/opusplan`: (1) **tier switch** — Opus plans, Sonnet executes post-`ExitPlanMode`. This was the main rationale and it is GONE; SKILL.md still asserts it three times ("the post-`ExitPlanMode` tier IS Sonnet" — D4 nudge, D7 dispatch, Class-1 dispatch), and those lines are now **false** for this setup and must be corrected regardless of the outcome here. (2) **plan file as transcript accumulator** — REDUNDANT: `format.md` §Interactive mode requires the discussion be emitted as visible chat AND the meeting note is the durable record, so the plan file is a third copy nobody reads. (3) **mechanical read-only guard** enforcing "no file edits except the plan file and the final meeting note" — the ONLY surviving argument, and weak: it is dropped at end-of-meeting step 1 before the only writes that matter, so it guards the discussion phase only. (4) **`ExitPlanMode` approval gate** — REDUNDANT: the per-decision `AskUserQuestion` points already gate at finer granularity. **NEW COST that forced this (2026-07-17):** a session holding background relay agents CANNOT go read-only — plan mode would block integrating their branches. Plan mode assumes the session does one thing; the supervised hand-run (routed:5ee9) breaks that assumption. **Scope questions for the implementer:** (a) does this drop apply to **Class 2 dispatch** too (SKILL.md no-arg step 5 uses `EnterPlanMode` for native explore→design→present — arguably still legitimate there, since C2 IS a planning flow), or **C3 subject-meetings only**? (b) if the read-only guard goes, does the "no implementation mid-meeting" constraint need any replacement, or is prose sufficient (it is prose everywhere else)? (c) the three stale Sonnet-tier assertions must be fixed either way. See `docs/meeting-notes/2026-07-17-1450-acc7-phantom-append-write-integrity.md` (session context). <!-- id:fc0f -->

- [x] **`/meeting` must end with an explicit "are we done?" check before the end-of-meeting steps** (user 2026-07-17: "the meeting must end with 'are we done?' instead of just starting off when the user might have amendments"). **DONE 2026-07-18 (landed with [[id:fc0f]], user re-requested):** SKILL.md end-of-meeting **step 0 — Closure gate (id:af5a)**, runs FIRST before any durable write: `AskUserQuestion [wrap up and record / amend a decision / add an agenda item]` (Fable → inline prose). Sub-questions resolved with defaults: (a) fires uniformly (C2+C3 — it is the SOLE closure checkpoint for a C2 whose `ExitPlanMode` approval was skipped on Opus/Fable); (b) an amendment APPENDS a superseding entry citing what it supersedes, never silently rewrites the ratified original (decision-provenance); (c) Fable uses the inline-prose protocol. Tested: `tests/test_meeting_plan_mode_gate.sh` (af5a cases). Today SKILL.md runs the last agenda item's decision → straight into end-of-meeting (`ExitPlanMode` → write note → ledger write-back → profile/memory classification) with **no gate**: the model unilaterally decides the agenda is exhausted. The user may have amendments, a late objection, or a topic the agenda missed — and once the note is written and the ledgers are committed, raising them means re-opening a recorded decision rather than shaping it. Note the format spec ALREADY anticipates this and has no mechanism: `format.md` §Constraints says "New topics that arise mid-meeting must be captured as **Amendment session** in the transcript" — but nothing ever ASKS whether there are any, so the amendment path is unreachable in practice. **Design:** add a final `AskUserQuestion` gate before end-of-meeting step 1 — e.g. [wrap up / amend a decision / add an agenda item] — looping back into the meeting on the latter two. Questions: (a) does it also fire for Class 2 planning records, or C3 meetings only? (b) does an amendment after a ratified decision REPLACE it, or append a superseding entry (decision-provenance: the ratified original should probably survive as history rather than be silently rewritten)? (c) interaction with the Fable inline-prose protocol, which cannot use `AskUserQuestion`. Sibling of [[id:fc0f]] — both are end-of-meeting flow changes to the same SKILL.md region; decide/land them together to avoid two rewrites of the same steps. <!-- id:af5a -->

- [x] [ROUTINE] **`append.sh -t inbox` swallows its validator's diagnostic when the relay skill is absent** — REVIEW FINDING 2026-07-17 from the id:34c2 integration (verified, not speculative). id:34c2's executor validates by shelling out to `relay/scripts/todo-conformance.sh --inbox` (good reuse — one definition of "conforming line", no NIH), which introduces a NEW runtime coupling from the **meeting** skill to the **relay** skill. Probed the dependency-absent case (`make install-meeting` without relay, i.e. no sibling `relay/scripts/`): behaviour is **FAIL-CLOSED and therefore SAFE** — exit 127, nothing appended, no acc7 reintroduction, because `set -e` catches the failed command substitution. **But the diagnostic is SWALLOWED**: `conf_out="$("$conf_sh" --inbox "$tmp_check" 2>&1)"` captures "No such file or directory" into `conf_out`, which is discarded when `set -e` fires — so the caller gets a bare exit 127 with **zero explanation**. That is a silent-swallow, which this repo bans (CLAUDE.md no-silent-swallow / mechanize-first "fail LOUDLY"; [[no-swallow-stderr]]). **Root cause is the RED spec (`tests/test_inbox_write_integrity.sh`, authored by the 2026-07-17 handoff) never covering the dependency-absent case** — the executor met the contract as written; this is a spec gap, not executor misbehaviour. Fix: probe `[[ -x "$conf_sh" ]]` first and fail LOUDLY with a message naming the missing dependency; and decide whether the meeting→relay coupling is acceptable at all, or whether the conforming-form grammar should live somewhere both skills can reach. Extend the RED spec to pin the loud failure. See `docs/meeting-notes/2026-07-17-1450-acc7-phantom-append-write-integrity.md`. — DONE 2026-07-18, covered by tests (tests/test_inbox_write_integrity.sh bbb2 case): `append.sh` now probes `[[ -x "$conf_sh" ]]` BEFORE the command substitution and fails LOUDLY (exit 3) naming the missing `todo-conformance.sh`/relay dependency, appending nothing — the silent exit-127 swallow is gone. (The meeting→relay coupling itself is accepted as-is; no relocation of the conforming-form grammar.) <!-- id:bbb2 -->

- [x] [INBOUND routed:38db from truncocraft 2026-06-29] Make 'bump version on closed-work trigger' a global default (or discuss): global CLAUDE.md Versioning defines how to bump but never when, so repos sit at 0.1.0 indefinitely (truncocraft: 296 commits, 0 vX.Y.Z tags); decide global trigger (per closed ROADMAP item) vs per-repo opt-in. RELAY DIMENSION: bump must be the reviewer's job at integrate (post --no-ff merge, on main, same checkpoint turn) NOT the executor in a worktree (parallel worktrees collide on package.json/lockfile); bump commit must leave tree clean or clean-tree-gate.sh defers the repo; readers must use `git describe --match 'v[0-9]*'` since relay-ckpt-* tags outnumber v*. Keep prose-only for a few cycles (observe-before-preventing) before any relay-loop mechanization. **OBSERVATION EVIDENCE 2026-07-16 (the cycles this item was waiting for — the drift is now measured, not hypothesized).** A single relay session shipped 23 units across 12 repos; three repos were checked for version drift and **all three had shipped every close unversioned**: **loderite** 5 user-observable closes past v0.40.0 (its own CLAUDE.md mandates a bump per user-observable close), **zkm** 3 past v0.18.0, **chidiai** 12 past v0.1.0. Owner ruled "bump now, one per close" → loderite **v0.45.0**, chidiai **v0.13.0**, zkm **v0.21.0** (retroactive batch commits; per-close tags are unrecoverable after the fact). Fleet context: **25 own repos carry `v*` tags**, so this is not a three-repo problem. **Three findings that sharpen this item's design:** (a) **the diagnosis "the executor contract is silent" is a red herring** — the silence is CORRECT per this item's own ruling (the executor must NOT bump); the gap is that the *reviewer* side was never built, so nobody bumps. Anyone re-reading this should not "fix" the executor contract. (b) **`git describe --match 'v[0-9]*'` — this item's prescribed reader — SILENTLY MISREPORTS on zkm**: its `[autotag]` hook creates **lightweight** tags (`git cat-file -t v0.21.0` → `commit`, not `tag`; same for v0.18.0/v0.17.0; only the ancient v0.2.0 is annotated), and `git describe` ignores lightweight tags without `--tags` → it reported **`v0.2.0-622-g448580f`** on a HEAD tagged v0.21.0, i.e. 622 commits and 19 minor versions stale. **The reader must be `git describe --tags --match 'v[0-9]*'`**, or the hook must annotate. Worth its own fix — a version reader that is confidently 19 versions wrong is worse than none. (c) **the cascade is already tooled and the bump must go through it**: zkm's pre-push hook BLOCKS a bump whose 18 plugin `uv.lock`s are stale and names `scripts/relock-plugins.sh --push`; any mechanization here must call that tool, not re-implement the cascade (a hand-rolled loop relocked correctly but skipped the commits, leaving 17 repos dirty — chidiai case `improvised-a-tool-past-the-repo-s-own`). **Sibling: [[id:b8fa]]** (fleet CHANGELOG) has the IDENTICAL collision profile (an integrate-time write to a shared file that parallel worktrees would clobber) — decide the two together; one integrate-time hook plausibly serves both.  **DECIDED 2026-07-17 (meeting `docs/meeting-notes/2026-07-17-1541-semver-trigger-and-fleet-changelog.md`, D1/D4 — owner-ratified):** the standing trigger is a **global default**: the **reviewer bumps at integrate, once per user-observable close**, tagging it and regenerating the lockfile in the SAME commit. The judgement stays with the reviewer (a refactor close must NOT bump — 'user-observable' is not derivable, which is why full derivation was rejected). Confirms this item's own relay dimension: never the executor in a worktree (manifest+lockfile collide across parallel worktrees). Per D4 this ships **together with id:b8fa**, overriding the recommended pilot-first sequencing — recorded dissent: it lands in the expensive reviewer turn already doing roadmap re-derivation + test audit + merge, a botched bump stalls the repo via clean-tree-gate.sh, and zkm's parent bump cascades to every plugin uv.lock. READY TO BUILD. **DONE 2026-07-18** (background-built, apex-reviewed, merged `6c5f84f`; suite 260/0): `relay/scripts/version-bump.sh` — reviewer bumps at integrate per user-observable close (`--level` is the reviewer's non-derivable judgement, D1), manifest detect (pyproject/package.json) with version-less repos a no-op, loose-0.x, injectable lockfile regen (`$VERSION_BUMP_LOCK_CMD`), scoped clean-tree commit (id:debf), **annotated** `vX.Y.Z` tag (finding b), zkm cascade via the repo's own `scripts/relock-plugins.sh` (finding c, never reimplemented). Integrator `relay-loop.js` step 2a threads `bumpVersion` → the b8fa changelog `--version` for release-bucketing; refactor-only closes skip both. Tested `tests/test_version_bump.sh` (9 cases). Executor contract untouched (correct — executor never bumps). <!-- id:e647 -->

- [x] [ROUTINE] **Something silently re-nests top-level `title:`/`hook:` under `metadata:` on memory-file writes, defeating `memory-index.py`'s field resolution** — OBSERVED TWICE this session (2026-07-17), verified not speculative. Two memory files were authored with `title:` and `hook:` as TOP-LEVEL frontmatter keys (the shape id:2e6d's `tools/memory-index.py` reads: most-specific-first `title:`→`name:`→stem, `hook:`→`description:`). On the next read both keys had been relocated **under the `metadata:` block** (`metadata.title`, `metadata.hook`), so `memory-index.py` found neither at top level and **silently fell back** to `description:` (for the hook) and the filename (for the title) — producing a verbose, wrong index line with no error. Fixed each time by re-hoisting the keys and regenerating, but the corruption is silent and recurring. **UNKNOWN AND MUST BE ESTABLISHED BEFORE FIXING (do not guess the culprit — this session already mis-diagnosed id:1102 twice by asserting a cause before checking):** WHICH writer re-nests them — candidates are the `Write`/`Edit` tool path, a frontmatter normalizer/linter, the `claude-sessions-backup` commit, or `memory-index.py --write` itself round-tripping. Reproduce by writing a file with top-level `title:`/`hook:`, then observing which operation moves them. **Then decide the fix per the mechanize-first rule:** either make the responsible writer preserve key position, OR make `memory-index.py` resolve `title:`/`hook:` from BOTH top-level AND `metadata.*` (tolerate the nesting rather than fight it) — the latter dissolves it if the re-nester can't be controlled, but must be a conscious choice, not a shrug. This is a [[memory-index-derived-2e6d]] frontmatter-format hazard: the index is only correct if its inputs survive a write. Relates the [[no-swallow-stderr]] doctrine — the fallback to `description:` should arguably be LOUD (warn when a file has `metadata.hook` but no top-level `hook:`, which is almost certainly this bug). <!-- id:e875 -->
- [x] [ROUTINE] **`tests/test_resource_claim_pid.sh` is FLAKY in the full suite (breaks the "make test green = done" gate)** — observed 2026-06-25 while shipping id:2dea: the test passes 3/3 in isolation but fails ~50% of full-suite runs (byte-identical reruns gave fail / pass-104 / fail) with "a stale-mtime claim with a LIVE live_pid was stolen — PID-liveness not honored" — i.e. `claim.sh::is_live` intermittently false-negatives on test 1's live `--pid` claim. Suspect root cause: `pid_alive()` (claim.sh:99-105) reads `jq -r '.live_pid' "$f" 2>/dev/null` and treats ANY transient read failure as empty→dead (the `2>/dev/null` swallow, cf. [[feedback-mechanize-no-swallow-stderr]]); under full-suite load a slow/interrupted jq or a background-`sleep`/PID-reuse race in the test harness (the `( trap - EXIT; exec sleep 600 ) & LIVE_PID=$!` anchor at L36) makes `kill -0` miss. **Fix directions:** (a) harden the harness — verify `kill -0 $LIVE_PID` succeeds right before the assertion and retry/poll instead of one-shot, and confirm no other test's cleanup signals it; (b) make `pid_alive` distinguish "jq failed to read" from "live_pid absent" (don't swallow a read error into a false "dead"); (c) if it's genuine non-determinism, mark the timing-sensitive assertion accordingly. A flaky claim-liveness test is corrosive because every future item's done-check runs `make test`. Pre-existing in id:1b11 (NOT introduced by id:2dea — that test is green every run). <!-- id:ab5c -->
- [x] [ROUTINE] **Relay executor children must run with a FAILING askpass so a sub-process `sudo` can never pop a GUI password prompt** — OBSERVED 2026-07-18 (run relay-20260717-182134-8632): a loderite `execute` child running headless-browser e2e/screenshot work (`npx playwright test` for id:e43a) triggered a graphical sudo password dialog at 01:03:36 (`sudo[2330298]: auth could not identify password`) — almost certainly Playwright's `install-deps` reaching for `sudo apt-get`. The user was prompted and dismissed it (no privilege granted, child continued green). **The executor contract already says "missing dep → handback, not sudo" ([[no-sudo-pamac]]) — but that is PROSE, and prose can't stop a *sub-process* (playwright) from calling sudo.** Mechanical fix: dispatch every relay child (and ideally the whole pool env) with `SUDO_ASKPASS=/bin/false` (or run children where `sudo` resolves to `sudo -n`), so any sudo attempt fails INSTANTLY and non-interactively — no dialog ever reaches the user; the child just gets a non-zero exit and hands back the missing dep. Verify the child still surfaces the dep in its handback rather than silently swallowing the failure ([[feedback-mechanize-no-swallow-stderr]]). Relates the id:077d mechanize-role-boundaries idea, but note a pre-commit hook does NOT catch this (sudo is a runtime command, not a git write). Done-check: a child whose task shells out to `sudo` gets an immediate non-interactive failure (no GUI prompt) and hands back. **PARTIAL FIX 2026-07-19 (relay human):** `SUDO_ASKPASS=/bin/false` added to `~/.claude/settings.json` `env` — an AFK-safe naive-case catch (a subprocess sudo inheriting the env fails non-interactively → child hands back, no GUI dialog). A `deny Bash(sudo *)` rule was REJECTED: the permission matcher sees the wrapper command (`npx playwright test`), NOT the inner subprocess sudo, so it would miss this exact case — and it blocks AFK. This is defense-in-depth, NOT containment (any process can re-export `SUDO_ASKPASS`); real containment = the separate-OS-user route **id:5937**. Marked done as a partial fix; id:5937 is the real one. <!-- id:eb46 -->
- [x] [ROUTINE] **model-probe.sh: grade arm uses `echo "$output"` (flag/escape swallowing)** — audit id:401c Run 11 (Pass 1 finding). `echo "$output" | grep -qP "$regex"` mis-handles output starting with `-n`/`-e`/`-E` or containing backslashes; `printf '%s\n' "$output"` is robust. Zero impact today (battery goldens are numbers/words) but behaviour-adjacent forward-robustness. LOW severity, -sized (one-line change + a battery case with a leading-dash/backslash golden). <!-- id:b9b5 -->
- [x] **Tier-coverage checkpoint gate** `[HARD — pool]` (meeting 2026-07-19-1058, from `routed:3684`-1; chidiai `2026-07-18-during-relay-review-loderite-skipped-the`) — mechanize enforcement of `review.md §3` (id:f032, already fully specified but per-turn-LLM-trusted): a review-checkpoint script (candidate host `ckpt-tag.sh`/`review-gate.sh`, possibly co-located with id:07dc's prune) enumerates the repo's **declared** test tiers from its own manifests (`package.json` scripts, Makefile targets, CI config) and **refuses the checkpoint** unless the log/checkpoint entry carries, per tier, either a result token (e.g. `e2e: 27 passed`) or `SKIPPED-TIER: <tier> — <reason>`. - [x] **State-diagrams for `/relay` and `/meeting` (and other skills/tools where adequate), kept in sync with the actual flow** (user 2026-06-30) — the relay front-door (modes: autonomous pool / handoff / review / human / next / reconcile / stop / executor; verdict classes execute→review→hard→handoff; the self-feeding loop + quota/stop gates) and the meeting workflow (classify → personas → broker → write-back/plan-gate → orphan-scan assertion) are complex enough that a **state/flow diagram** (Mermaid `stateDiagram`/`flowchart`, rendered in-repo) would document them far better than prose. Do the same for other custom skills/tools here **where the flow is non-trivial enough to warrant it** (judgment per skill — git-diary-workflow, todo-update archive logic, the discovery-shard/integrator pipeline; skip trivial single-path scripts). **The hard part is keeping them up to date** — a diagram that drifts from the code is worse than none. Options: (a) generate the diagram FROM a machine-readable source (the SKILL.md mode table / relay-loop.js state machine) so it can't drift; (b) a `make`/test check that fails when the diagram's referenced states/modes don't match the skill's actual mode set; (c) hand-maintained + a review-step reminder (weakest). Prefer (a)/(b) — derive or guard, don't trust hand-sync (cf. mechanization-audit id:415b). Likely `[HARD — meeting]` to pick the diagram set + sync strategy. **AMENDED 2026-07-01 (folded into the id:4da4 pt2 meeting `docs/meeting-notes/2026-07-01-2142-relay-state-machine-invalid-state-detector.md`):** that meeting produced the machine-readable-ish SOURCE this item was missing — a file:line artifact×actor×transition matrix + invariants I1–I9, and the relay-doctor detector checks (id:8018/188c/333c) that name the real state set (clean-tree-gate / verdict enum / last_ckpt). **Strategy narrowed to drift-GUARD** (a test that a diagram's states match the detector's/loop's actual mode+verdict set) over hand-sync; the /relay side of the diagram derives from that note as source-of-truth. Stays its own build item. **UML venue (id:a6e1, 2026-07-12):** this item is the designated venue to explore broader UML/diagram application across skills — UML adoption elsewhere is deferred pending what a17a decides (`docs/meeting-notes/2026-07-12-1030-repo-self-governance-versioning-formal-docs.md`). **SCOPE DECIDED 2026-07-19 (meeting mtg-1726, D1+D2):** THREE linked Mermaid diagrams — **(a)** id/ledger lifecycle (TODO↔ROADMAP↔REVIEW_ME↔inbox, single-id-two-views), **(b)** relay dispatch state machine, **(c)** meeting classification tree (C1/C2/C3; broker/meeting-rpg = a conditional sub-branch, NOT its own diagram). Diagram (b) MUST show all THREE execution substrates (LLM Workflow pool / mechanical daemon+`.timer` OUTSIDE the Workflow / human) + the `[MECHANICAL]` lane + a conditional version/changelog side-branch on the integrate transition (semver → bump+tag+changelog per e647/b8fa; versionless repos → changelog-only, no version), and **reconcile with the id:4da4 pt2 matrix (invariants I1–I9) as source-of-truth** (`docs/meeting-notes/2026-07-01-2142-relay-state-machine-invalid-state-detector.md`). **Sync = GUARD TEST** (D2): a `make test` check that fails when a diagram's named states/modes/verdicts diverge from the machine-readable set (SKILL.md mode list + `classify-verdict.sh` enum + the 4da4 matrix) — NOT full generation (2ec4 sandbox un-runnable from bash), NOT hand-sync. Design the guard as a candidate consumer of the inflownistration/info-flow idea (id:aae4), don't build it to fight that. Out of scope this build: diagrams for git-diary-workflow/todo-update/discovery-shard (judgment per skill, later). Now buildable → `[HARD — pool]` after a handoff writes the guard-test RED spec. <!-- id:a17a -->

- [x] **[HIGH PRIORITY — bug] `gather-human-backlog.sh` aborts the WHOLE cross-repo scan on a single LOUD-reject** (found 2026-07-19, relay human) — an open `[HARD]` with an unrecognized lane tag (observed: zkWhale id:770d/f0e5 carrying `[HARD — strong model]`) makes the gather script exit nonzero and STOP, silently truncating every repo it had not yet processed. Concretely: the 2026-07-19 `/relay human --all` first surfaced only ~12 repos / ~20 review_me boxes; after the one bad tag was fixed, the SAME scan completed with **36 repos / 64 review_me boxes** — ~2/3 of the human backlog was invisible. This is the [[no-swallow-stderr]] / fail-LOUD-but-CONTINUE class: a malformed item must be surfaced AND skipped, never allowed to blind the rest of the sweep. Fix: make the lane-reject per-item (collect rejects, print them at the end as a distinct ERROR block, exit nonzero for CI, but ALWAYS finish emitting every repo's boxes). Add a test: a fixture repo with one bad-lane item must not suppress a second fixture repo's boxes. — **DONE 2026-07-20 (execute+Opus-review SHIP, relay-ckpt-20260720-1317):** RED tests fail-on-base/pass-on-branch, no test weakened, suite 273/0. <!-- id:fa5c -->
