- [x] [ROUTINE] @container **[HIGH PRIORITY] Memory-index integrity [DONE 2026-07-21 drain run-2 — verified: memory-index.py check_hook_invariants + test_memory_index.sh test 12 (id:7d97) shipped; INPUT child id:9c2c (over-ceiling) stays open]: nothing verifies that `MEMORY.md` still points at every memory** (user 2026-07-10, prompted by an LLM compaction pass rewriting all 105 index hooks with only *prompt-level*, self-checked invariants — the "documented, not enforced" anti-pattern [[feedback-enforce-not-document-contracts]] names). **The failure is NOT hypothetical and NOT compaction-specific: measured 2026-07-10, the dir holds 108 memory files but the index carries 105 pointers — `feedback-canonical-paths.md`, `feedback-fable-optional-not-gate.md`, `relay-fable-down-two-switch.md` have NO index line and are therefore invisible to recall** (the index is what loads each session; the files are dead weight). Verified pre-existing (absent from the pre-compaction snapshot), so the leak is the **append** path — a memory file written, its `MEMORY.md` pointer never added — not a rewrite. **Cheap because the before-state is free:** `~/.claude/projects` is its own git repo and `MEMORY.md` IS tracked there (hourly `sessions(<host>)` auto-commits), so a check diffs the worktree against `git show HEAD:<path>` — no snapshot plumbing, and today's losses are recoverable from history. **MECHANICAL tier (pure function, ≥2 consumers = append path + any compaction/hand-edit ⇒ passes the id:415b determinism gate):** a `memory-index-lint.sh` asserting (1) dir↔index **bijection** (file with no pointer / pointer with no file — catches the 3 live orphans), (2) vs HEAD: entry count, the set of `(name.md)` link targets, every `id:`/`routed:` token and `[[wiki-link]]` **per entry**, and status lexemes (SHIPPED/DEFERRED/REJECTED/CLOSED/GATED/NO-GO) preserved verbatim, (3) size ceiling. Fail LOUD, non-zero. Wire as a PostToolUse hook on Write/Edit to `MEMORY.md` so it *enforces* rather than advises. **IRREDUCIBLE residual → independent review, narrowly scoped:** a hook can preserve every token yet drift in meaning ("defer refuter fleet" → "refuter fleet" inverts it). Mechanical cannot decide that; an LLM reviewer should fire ONLY on entries whose hook text changed, and must fail loudly ([[feedback-mechanize-reliability-not-cost]] — no LLM where mechanical is ≥ as good). **STRUCTURAL DISSOLUTION (decide first — this may delete the guard):** every one of the 108 files already carries `description:` frontmatter, so `MEMORY.md` could be **GENERATED** by enumerating the directory, making a dropped pointer *unrepresentable* rather than merely detected — exactly [[ledger-derived-index-decision-2840]]'s md=SSOT/index=cache shape, and "prefer dissolving a problem structurally over guarding it". **Open fork:** hooks are hand-tuned and terser than `description:` today, so generation needs either (a) `description:` becomes the hook (one source, lose the terse/verbose split) or (b) a new `hook:` frontmatter field. Pick the fork before building the lint, else the lint guards a file that's about to become derived. **PARTIAL FIX LANDED 2026-07-10** (~/.claude/projects commit 83d81fa0): the 3 orphans are resolved — `relay-fable-down-two-switch` + `feedback-fable-optional-not-gate` now carry index lines (the former SUPERSEDES `fable-down-flag`: it records the user calling `-d` mis-designed after an outage cost 18 handbacks — it was invisible to recall for ~4 weeks), and `feedback-canonical-paths` was an exact duplicate of `feedback-edit-canonical-paths` and was deleted. The size pressure was resolved by **archiving, not truncating**: 10 settled index lines moved to `MEMORY.archive.md` (files untouched, `[[wiki-links]]` still resolve), 20852→17002 bytes with ZERO hook degradation — the same doctrine as `archive-done.sh`. **This CHANGES the lint's contract:** the dir↔index bijection must union `MEMORY.md` ∪ `MEMORY.archive.md`, and the size ceiling applies to `MEMORY.md` alone. Also add an archive-eligibility rule (never archive a `feedback-*` entry — durable user preferences) and a supersession check (an entry whose hook says SUPERSEDES X should sort above X, or X should be archived). The one-shot invariant checker used for this pass (entries / link targets / per-entry `id:`+`routed:`+`[[link]]`+status-lexeme diff vs baseline) is the lint's spec — mechanize it. **FORK DECIDED + DISSOLUTION SHIPPED 2026-07-10** (dotclaude-skills a4cb9b4; ~/.claude/projects 409ea3cc): chose a new optional `hook:` frontmatter field over reusing `description:` — descriptions are as long or longer than the curated hooks, so `description:`-as-hook would GROW the file the ceiling is trying to shrink, and the two fields have different jobs (relevance summary vs index one-liner). Also added `title:` (the real index carries 103 hand-shortened display titles) and `metadata.archived: true` (archive state lives with the file, no split-brain). Resolution is most-specific-first: `title:`→`name:`→stem, `hook:`→`description:`. `tools/memory-index.py` now GENERATES both index files by enumerating the dir: `--check` exits 1 with a diff (the enforcing lint), `--write` regenerates atomically + idempotently. A dropped pointer is now unrepresentable — verified by deleting one and watching `--check` flag it and `--write` restore it. Ordering uses the existing pointers as a cache; git add-date could NOT serve as the key (41 of 107 files share one bulk-backfill timestamp). `feedback-*` + archived = loud exit 2. Ceiling is measured in UTF-8 BYTES not characters (em-dashes cost 3 — an earlier 'under target' claim was a char count and was actually 214 bytes over). 12 hermetic tests; suite 210 green. **LESSON, recorded because it validates the tiering:** the stopped compaction agent passed every mechanical invariant (tokens/links/status/entry-count) yet silently stripped the `user:` prefix from 14 of 15 feedback hooks and flattened emphasis in 62 more — the exact 'all tokens preserved, meaning drifted' residual. All 105 hooks were restored VERBATIM from the pre-compaction baseline; bytes come from archiving (17 entries now), never from prose. **HOOK SHIPPED** (de5b1a5): `hooks/memory-index-sync.py` (PostToolUse) regenerates the index on any memory-file write, closing the append path that caused the 3 orphans; PostToolUse cannot block, so loud = stderr + exit 2, and a missing/crashing generator is LOUD too (the file is already known to be a memory ⇒ the index is stale by construction; id:4347). `meeting/memory-append.sh` now REFUSES (exit 3) to hand-append to a generated index. REMAINING: (a) **queued as ROADMAP id:7d97 ** — add the `user:`-prefix + emphasis-preservation invariants to `--check` (diff vs `git show HEAD:<path>`); (b) **[INPUT — user]** install the PostToolUse snippet from `hooks/README.md` into `settings.json` — **DONE 2026-07-20 (owner-approved):** `memory-index-sync.py` symlinked into `~/.claude/hooks/` + registered under `hooks.PostToolUse` (matcher `Write|Edit|NotebookEdit`); Makefile `install-hooks` target fixed to include the previously-omitted symlink. Hook is now LIVE (fail-open smoke-tested). Only child id:7d97 remains open on 2e6d. <!-- children:7d97 --> <!-- id:2e6d -->

- [x] **mechanical-proxy `model:"bash"` wedge — ROOT CAUSE was proxy SSE NON-TERMINATION, not engine-level (my initial diagnosis was WRONG; a Fable adversarial recheck caught it)** — `_serve_mechanical_sse` sent `Connection: keep-alive` under a default-HTTP/1.0 server with no Content-Length + no chunked terminator + no close, so after `message_stop` the socket stayed open, the client's SSE reader never saw EOF, `agent()` hung, and the engine's 180s watchdog retried into the same hang (exact-180s retry intervals were the fingerprint; the pass-through path never wedged because it strips Connection as hop-by-hop → HTTP/1.0 default close). FIXED 2026-07-23 (commit 3afa162): send `Connection: close` + force `handler.close_connection = True` after message_stop; `tests/test_mechanical_proxy.sh` asserts it. VERIFIED LIVE: `/relay --once MECH_FALLBACK=proceed` (real model:bash) now completes 12/12 agents 0 errors vs wedge-at-agent-1 before — the model:bash mechanical-proxy substrate (id:176f) is FUNCTIONAL. [[id:3557]] (empty-stdout sentinel) was a separate real fix. NOTE: the proxy in-path still DISABLES /remote-control (proxy-out needed to keep that). Relates [[relay-model-proxy-probe-gated-substrate]], id:176f, id:94b8. <!-- id:a36e -->

- [x] [HARD — pool] **Build BOUNDED auto-integrate for stranded orphans (UN-DEMOTED 2026-07-23 --fabled A1)** — needs RED spec. The relay loop auto-completes an orphan that is COMPLETE + clean-3-way-merge + non-diverged + FULL-suite-green (the interrupted-integration case, e.g. ce50 died at the push step), so a single-item-only-orphan repo never STALLS pending human reconcile (the Fable hole-2 regression). Everything else — partial/mid-cutoff, any conflict, any red, any divergence — stays human-gated `/relay reconcile`. This IS the bounded, owner-ratified amendment to a4e9-D1's "NO auto-integration" (meeting `docs/meeting-notes/2026-07-23-1735-relay-orphan-existence-never-blocks.md`, A1). Extends the id:2370 ledger-only auto-integrate to code-bearing. Pairs with id:bc49 (existence-never-blocks). <!-- id:1048 -->
- [x] **[CRITICAL] Discovery shard suppresses a clean-verdict repo when it is orphan-heavy** — OBSERVED 2026-07-23 (`/relay --intensive --priority dotclaude-skills,loderite`, run relay-20260723-110229-15341): loderite was absent from dispatch entirely (not Completed/Queued/Skipped/Blocked in RELAY_STATUS.md) despite `classify-repo.sh --repo loderite` returning `{verdict:execute, actionable_routine_open:4, ambiguous:false}` AND being priority-rank #1. The discovery shard returned `units:[]` for loderite, surfacing only its 15+ parked `relay/orphan/*` branches — i.e. orphan-surfacing crowded out the deterministic execute unit. classify-repo's verdict is the SSOT predicate; the shard must emit the execute unit REGARDLESS of how many orphans it also surfaces (surfacing orphans and emitting the work unit are independent outputs). Worked around this run by `inject.sh add loderite --verdict execute` (inj-20260723-114058), but inject-per-run is a band-aid. Fix: shard emits the classify-repo unit whenever `ambiguous:false`, and orphan-surfacing is additive, never a substitute. Same class as [[review-me-relay-discipline-cluster-16e3-3684]] (prose rule silently no-ops) — a loud deterministic verdict must not be silently dropped. Relates id:75b3 (drain review-gate discretionary), id:3a7a (unpromoted-scan worktree-name miss). **RECURRED 2026-07-23 on dotclaude-skills** (run relay-20260723-165121, `/relay --intensive`): a single parked orphan for id:ce50 made the WHOLE repo emit `units:[]` and never be classified — blocking the independent open ROUTINE (id:dfb9) and the user's handed-off HARD id:24ec. - [x] **Probe `model:"bash"` availability at relay launch (mirror the Fable probe) — warn or auto-fall-back to Haiku** — **[DONE 2026-07-24 (cross-ledger reconcile): the ROADMAP twin id:99a4 shipped (`probe-mech-proxy.sh discriminate` + `tests/test_mech_proxy_probe.sh`); the preflight consumer id:4239 (`mech-preflight.sh`) and the always-on service id:69f6 (`mechanical-proxy.service` + WatchdogSec id:4044) are all `[x]` — the full directive is satisfied.]** the FIX for [[id:7e6d]] (the mechanical-runner tier silently erroring in background Workflow runs). Just as step-0 probes Fable once (cached, 2 h staleness) and picks the strong tier, the front door / relay-loop should probe whether `model:"bash"` resolves in the current execution context BEFORE the run relies on it for quota gates / inject-take / heartbeat / file-surface. Two outcomes (owner directive 2026-07-23): (a) if it resolves → proceed as today; (b) if it errors ("model may not exist / no access") → EITHER surface a LOUD launch warning that the mechanical glue will degrade (quota shows unknown, inject-take flaky), OR automatically fall the `model:"bash"` steps back to Haiku (the pre-echo-runner tier — this is a REVERT to the original mechanical model, not a new one). Prefer auto-fallback-to-Haiku so the run stays functional, with the warning as the floor if even that is unavailable. Cache the probe like the Fable one to avoid per-round cost. **TWO-MODE DISCRIMINATOR (owner refinement 2026-07-23) — the probe must distinguish, because the remedy differs:** (a) **base-URL/env misconfig** — `ANTHROPIC_BASE_URL` empty or ≠ `http://127.0.0.1:<MECH_PROXY_PORT default 61843>`: the session was not launched through the proxy, so `model:"bash"` hits the real API. **UNFIXABLE in-session** (the harness binds the global base URL at startup) → LOUD warning naming the exact restart env (`ANTHROPIC_BASE_URL=http://127.0.0.1:61843`, start proxy first) + Haiku-fallback for this run. Detect by reading the env var. (b) **proxy down/broken** — base URL IS loopback but a liveness probe (TCP-connect the port / trivial `model:"bash"` echo that must return stdout, not a real-API model error) fails. CORRECTION 2026-07-23 (owner): this is NOT a Haiku-fallback case — a dead proxy at the session's base URL means NOTHING is reachable (normal `agent()` traffic also transits it), so the whole session is broken and Haiku is equally unreachable. Remedy: attempt an in-session (re)start of `mechanical-proxy.py` (which the id:69f6 always-on service should make automatic), re-probe; if it still cannot be brought up, **LOUD ABORT** ("proxy down, session non-functional") — never silently degrade. Haiku fallback applies ONLY to mode (a), where the real API IS reachable directly. id:69f6 (always-on service) is the durable fix that should make mode (b) rare; this check is the defense-in-depth detector. Relates [[relay-model-proxy-probe-gated-substrate]], mechanical-proxy.py (id:176f), echo-runner. Fixes [[id:7e6d]]. <!-- id:99a4 -->

- [x] **Nothing detects an uninstalled canonical script — 5 were missing symlinks, incl. the id:f682 isolation gate** — DONE 2026-07-24: fix candidate (a)/(c) built — `relay/scripts/check-install-drift.sh --canonical <dir> --installed <dir>` diffs canonical scripts against the install AND follows each `source`/`.` line, exiting non-zero + naming every missing script (covers both the directly-invoked "command not found" class AND the routed:35eb `source`-target false-green); RED spec `tests/test_install_drift_guard.sh` green; wired into `relay_FILES`/`relay_EXEC`/`relay_ALLOW`. — found 2026-07-16. Skills install as per-file symlinks, so a newly-added canonical script needs a `make install-<skill>` run to appear in `~/.claude/skills/`. Five relay scripts had **no symlink** and were silently absent at call time: `worktree-retire.sh` + `verify-isolation.sh` + `handback-guard.mjs` + `mechanical-orphan-scan.sh` + `mechanical-orphan-draft.sh` (57 installed vs 63 canonical). `verify-isolation.sh` is the **safety gate SKILL.md invariant 5 mandates** (id:f682 — aborts a merge when a child wrote to the main checkout instead of its worktree); it was uninstalled, i.e. the documented gate could not have been running. `worktree-retire.sh` (added 07-15 for id:373e) was likewise never installed, so integrator step 5 was calling a nonexistent path. **Refined 2026-07-16 after investigation — the manifests were NEVER the problem:** `git log -S'verify-isolation' -- Makefile` returns exactly one commit (`05042a4`), the same one that added the script, and it registered it in `relay_FILES`/`relay_EXEC`/`relay_ALLOW` simultaneously. So this is the milder finding: `make install-relay` simply wasn't re-run for ~21h, NOT a manifest omission that made installation impossible. **The drift is two-surfaced, though** — the same staleness hit the **allowlist**: `worktree-retire.sh` and `verify-isolation.sh` were in `relay_ALLOW` but had **0** entries in `~/.claude/settings.json` (vs 8 for `ckpt-tag.sh`) because `make install-allowlist` hadn't been run either. An un-allowlisted helper prompts, and an unattended pool cannot answer a prompt — so allowlist drift silently degrades the pool exactly like symlink drift does. Both fixed this session (`make install-relay` + `make install-allowlist`; 16 narrow script-scoped entries added, `rm -f`/`-rf` ask-guards untouched). The **class** is still unfixed: `make status` reports skill-level symlink state, and nothing fails when a canonical script has no installed counterpart — the failure surfaces only as a runtime "command not found" inside an agent, where it is easy to miss. **Fix candidates:** (a) a test that diffs canonical `relay/scripts/*` against `~/.claude/skills/relay/scripts/*` and fails on drift (generalize per-skill; skip `__pycache__`); (b) extend `make status` to report per-file drift, not just skill-level; (c) have `relay-doctor.sh` check that every script the loop invokes actually resolves (it already checks reference-doc install completeness, id:69ef — same shape); (d) make a missing helper a LOUD failure at call site rather than a silent skip; (e) **cover the allowlist surface too** — assert every `<skill>_ALLOW` script has its 8 entries in the live `settings.json`, else the pool prompts and stalls unattended. Note (a)/(b) are the cheap mechanical wins; both drift surfaces share one root — *the manifest is the SSOT but nothing verifies the installed artifact matches it*. Relates id:69ef (reference-doc install check), id:373e, id:f682, id:7612 (the gate that isn't wired at all — a superset problem: installing a script nothing calls fixes nothing). **RECURRED 2026-07-24 (routed:35eb, loderite relay run) — 2nd instance in 8 days, and NASTIER:** `lib-state-claim.sh` (added to `relay_FILES` by `9687dce` the same day) was absent from the install because `make install-relay` had not been re-run since 23 Jul; the INSTALLED `roadmap-lint.sh` AND `todo-conformance.sh` both `source "$script_dir/lib-state-claim.sh"` and died mid-run (`set -e`, source not found) — a FALSE-GREEN inside id:5533's own anti-false-green tooling. Unlike the original 5 (plain "command not found"), a missing **`source` target** kills the script silently, so a caller reading only stdout sees clean. Recurrence confirms fix (a)/(c) is demonstrated-needed, and (a) must cover the source-target case (a script whose `source` line points at an uninstalled sibling), not just directly-invoked scripts. Fixed operationally this session by `make install-relay`. <!-- routed:35eb --> <!-- id:c5ed -->

- [x] **Quota-gate BOOTSTRAP DEADLOCK: the extrapolation rescue path could never fire after ~2 h idle** — FIXED + tested 2026-07-28. The `extrapolate_or_stop` fallback (id:0175) read ONLY `~/.config/relay/quota-samples.jsonl`, which is written **exclusively by `quota-stop.sh` itself during a live run** (gated on `RELAY_RUN_ID`). So after a few idle hours that series was always past the 7200 s recency bound — and refilling it required a run that got past this very gate. Combined with a stale `/tmp/claude-usage-cache.json` (the statusline owns it and was in max 600 s API backoff), every launch hard-stopped: observed live on run `relay-20260728-104330-9348` — cache 2859 s stale, last primary sample 138777 s (38.5 h) old → `exit 2 REASON=quota-cache-unreadable`, **0 units dispatched, 19 s wall**. Note `--quota-7d 100` did NOT help: the fail-safe fires *before* any threshold comparison, so a bucket explicitly capped at 100% still stopped the run. Fix: `RELAY_QUOTA_SAMPLES_FALLBACK` (default `${DIARY_REPO_DIR:-~/src/claude-diary}/quota/quota-samples.jsonl`) — the systemd sampler's stream (id:d267), same schema, written every 15 min independent of relay activity. The recency check moved INSIDE the candidate loop so a too-old primary falls THROUGH instead of stopping. Loud log on every fallback use; both sources named when both fail. Tests `(f)/(f2)/(f3)` in `tests/test_quota_extrapolation_fallback.sh` pin proceed-via-fallback, still-stops-on-high-burn (source swap, not a bypass), and both-stale-keeps-exit-2; existing cases pinned `RELAY_QUOTA_SAMPLES_FALLBACK=""` for hermeticity. Suite 309/0. <!-- id:18e2 -->

- [x] [ROUTINE] **`claim.sh`: add a `release --run <runId>` sweep verb** (D1-A) — releases every key whose shard JSON records that run. This is what makes per-unit `releaseLease` (`relay-loop.js:2415`) a *latency optimization* instead of load-bearing: today a blocked per-unit release strands a lease until `CLAIM_TTL` (the observed loderite harm — a lease held by dead pid 2988585 until a human intervened); with an exit sweep it costs latency on one repo and nothing else. Contract: after a run holding N keys exits, one sweep call releases all N, idempotent on an already-released key. <!-- children-of:51f0 --> <!-- id:89d6 -->
- [x] **Self-model guard: default to executor-only after timeout in unattended mode** — **SUPERSEDED 2026-06-15 by the Opus-apex pivot (id:da26, f64c28b):** the self-guard (warn+sleep) is removed; the front door now probes Fable once/2h and defaults to Opus apex (Fable a bonus, never a gate). Resolves the item's intent (never dispatch a doomed strong unit; the long-requested auto-probe shipped at spec level) — kept open only until the probe is a tested helper rather than a front-door instruction. Original: user directive 2026-06-13: when on Opus without `-d` AND the strong tier (Fable) is unavailable, the SKILL.md step-0 guard currently does "warn + `sleep 10` + proceed" — but that "proceed" dispatches strong-model review/handoff units on `claude-fable-5`, which fail when Fable is down (observed thrice 2026-06-13; the 3rd was an **attended `handoff` run** — all 5 fable children returned "Fable 5 unavailable" at launch, operator asked the user → Opus pilot of 2/5). The attended case worked correctly (asked), but reinforces two design facts: (i) **handoff has NO executor fallback** — creating the roadmap IS the strong work — so unattended fable-down must DEFER handoff units entirely (the `-d` semantics already say this; verify the autonomous front door honors it), not merely demote review→execute; (ii) auto-probe would have avoided spawning 5 doomed children. The guard should, **after the timeout with no human abort (i.e. unattended), DEFAULT TO EXECUTOR-ONLY** (`fableDown=true`) rather than launching doomed strong-model units OR auto-escalating to expensive Opus reviews (which must stay explicit `--strong-tier opus` opt-in). Design qs to resolve: (a) how does the front door detect "unattended" vs an interactive operator who'd Ctrl-C? (b) does this fold into the deferred Fable auto-probe ([[fable-down-flag]] notes auto-probe deferred — demand now confirmed thrice)? (c) interaction with `--interactive` flag. Conservative default everywhere: never auto-spend Opus, never dispatch a strong unit you expect to fail. — **CLOSED 2026-07-30 (`/relay human`): the residual condition is discharged by REMOVAL, not by the route this line anticipated.** It was kept open "only until the probe is a tested helper rather than a front-door instruction" — but the Fable probe was RETIRED WHOLESALE 2026-07-28 (id:aa26, constraint archaeology): `relay/scripts/probe-fable.sh`, the `~/.config/relay/fable-probe.json` cache and the spawn-a-tiny-agent step-0 procedure are all gone, because Fable became a fixed part of the Max subscription and there is no availability left to probe. What replaced it IS a tested helper — `relay/scripts/fable-config.sh check` (a plain config read: no cache, no staleness window, no spawned agent) with a dedicated spec at `tests/test_fable_config_aa26.sh`. So both halves of the item's intent hold: no doomed strong unit is ever dispatched (Opus is apex, `-d` is a no-op on it) and the front-door instruction is now a tested script. Verified 2026-07-30: `probe-fable.sh` absent, `fable-config.sh` + its test present. <!-- id:f649 -->

Fix: add `@container` to the per-line conservative exclusion in all three dispatch collectors (same under-dispatch-safe direction as `@manual`, so it cannot mis-dispatch), and — per `lib-state-claim.sh`'s same-answer rule — land them together. RED spec `tests/test_container_dispatch_exclusion.sh` 3-passed/4-failed on base → 7/0 on the fix; suite 327/0/7-xred, no existing test weakened. Completeness swept: `grep` over `relay/scripts/*.sh` confirms **no fourth copy** of the per-line exclusion predicate. Verified second effect: `roadmap_actionable_open` now also drops `@container` items, which is correct — a ROADMAP whose only open items are containers IS effectively drained (id:9014), and `is_human` is used only negatively, so no false human-backlog surface is created. <!-- routed:02d9 --> <!-- id:0cf5 -->

- [x] [INBOUND routed:2ae7 from it-infra] relay review.md needs an OVER-REACH check: does the diff do MORE than the ratified source authorized? Today §2b's four judgment-residue checks (resurrection, fixture special-casing, unmarked green guards, skipped-as-pass) all assume the SPEC is correct and the executor might cheat it, and §4 spec-drift checks only repo-internal ARCHITECTURE.md/README — so nothing catches a HONEST implementation that is a strict superset of what was authorized, with a fully green suite. Live miss 2026-07-29 (it-infra id:3177): handoff wrote a RED spec whose test 8 only covered a repo pushing nothing but main, executor reasonably generalized to 'any refs/heads/* != main is a publication channel', suite went 8/8 green, review returned substantive:false — but loderite meeting D1 ratified exactly ONE channel (an ff-only 'stable' bookmark), and loderite's bare repo carries 5 live relay/exec-* branches that would each have gotten a worktree + full npm ci && build on the Pi (the disk/CPU cost meeting D3 explicitly flagged, multiplied per branch). Caught only by a human re-read afterwards; fixed in it-infra 1cf9b8b via a PUBLICATION_CHANNELS allowlist + a new test 9. Proposed: a §2b.5 or §4c step that re-reads the ROADMAP item's CITED ratified source (often a meeting note in ANOTHER repo, as here) and asks whether the diff's behaviour is a superset of it — load-bearing precisely when the handoff authored the spec in the same relay lineage, since then the reviewer is the ONLY independent check and 'tests pass' re-uses the same blind spot. Anchor: this is the implementation-side twin of the existing global heuristic 'check a derived doc against its ratified source before letting a pre-registered rule fire'. Distinct from id:c17c (review-agent anti-spam brief) — **DONE 2026-07-29** (relay pool `relay-20260729-142725-13077`); ROADMAP twin already `[x]`, ticking the TODO twin to clear the single-id-two-views drift `orphan-scan --cross-ledger` flagged. <!-- id:b460 -->

Distinct from a paused repo — paused is a deliberate state the tools honor; this is a registry entry pointing at nothing. Wanted: decide per repo (deleted → prune the entry; relocated → add `# path:`; renamed → fix), and add a cheap relay-doctor check that LOUD-surfaces an `own` entry whose resolved path is not a git repo, so the next sweep cannot silently skip it. Note the no-silent-swallow rule: the scripts that skip them today do so without complaint, which is the actual defect. — **✗ FALSE PREMISE — CLOSED 2026-07-29, same day it was filed. This item described a bug that does not exist.** `droidclaw` and `helferClaw` were **never** `classification = "own"`: both have carried `classification = "excluded"` with dated explanatory comments since **2026-06-13** (droidclaw "ON ICE", helferClaw "on hiatus + container-managed UID 100999"). The "own but absent on disk" premise came entirely from my own ad-hoc TOML parser, which substring-matched the literal `classification = "own"` inside those very comments (`# Re-include by setting classification = "own"`) — i.e. the comment explaining the exclusion is what made my parser report them as included. No registry drift exists and no per-repo owner decision is needed. **Owner confirmed 2026-07-29 that both projects are retired**; both were additionally marked `status = "paused"` via the canonical `relay-state-write.sh` (never a hand-edit), which is now accurate bookkeeping rather than a fix. **The one residual worth keeping is NOT this item's:** `relay-state-write.sh` has no delete/remove verb (only `toml-set`/`status-write`), so there is no canonical way to DROP a repo block — retirement can only be expressed as `excluded`, never as removal. That is a real gap if the registry is ever to shrink; not filing it as work today since `excluded` is semantically correct and reversible. See the corrected measurement on id:7df1. — **TICKED 2026-07-30 (handoff C2, ledger hygiene).** No work was done and none is needed: the item's own body already records `✗ FALSE PREMISE — CLOSED 2026-07-29`, and the checkbox was simply never flipped, so it kept re-surfacing to `unpromoted-scan.sh` as a `promote` row and would have been sized into ROADMAP as executor work that does not exist. Re-verified in this worktree before ticking: nothing in the tree asserts the drift the item describes. The residual the body flags (`relay-state-write.sh` has no delete/remove verb, so a repo block can only be `excluded`, never dropped) is deliberately NOT carried forward as work — the body already argues `excluded` is semantically correct and reversible. <!-- id:6a0d -->

- [x] [INPUT — decision] **`classify.sh` had no class for `[ROUTINE]` or `[MECHANICAL]`, so every open executor/daemon-lane item was a pick-eligible `/meeting` candidate** — found 2026-07-30 right after the routed:f1e1 fix, by noticing a freshly-filed `[ROUTINE]` item classify as `C3`. The lane floor read `[HARD]`/`[INPUT — …]` but the capability-keyed taxonomy (id:4f02) has FOUR lanes; the other two fell through to the link/keyword heuristic. **⚠ MY FIRST MEASUREMENT WAS WRONG AND IS CORRECTED HERE.** This item originally claimed *"46 of this repo's 48 open pool/daemon items"* from *"47 `[ROUTINE]` + 3 `[MECHANICAL]` → 29 C3, 14 C2, 3 C1"*. Those came from `grep -cE '^- \[ \].*\[ROUTINE\]'`, which matches the tag ANYWHERE on the line — so it counted OTHER-lane items merely DISCUSSING `[ROUTINE]`/`[MECHANICAL]` in prose (this very line is one of them). **ANCHORED truth: 31 `[ROUTINE]` + 1 `[MECHANICAL]` = 32 items; BEFORE 19 C3 + 11 C2 + 2 C1 → AFTER 31 EXEC + 1 MECH.** The naive count overstated the blast radius by ~50%, and it is exactly the id:5648 hijack trap — measured without the head-anchor while designing the fix whose whole point is the head-anchor. Recorded rather than quietly corrected. **DECIDED + SHIPPED 2026-07-30 (owner-ratified, after a Fable second opinion overturned my first recommendation):** (a) `[MECHANICAL]` → **`MECH`**, its own SURFACED skip class — NOT folded into POOL and never a silent drop, because a `[MECHANICAL]` item is pool-inert AND human-inert (`gather-human-backlog.sh` excludes it from every human bucket) and its host daemon is *"A3, gated — not built"*, so `/meeting`'s printout is currently the ONLY surface a human sees it on (id:4347 no-silent-swallow). (b) `[ROUTINE]` → **`EXEC`**, skipped **ALWAYS — including with a link + `## Decisions`**. I had recommended keeping those C1-eligible; **Fable refuted it and was right**: bare `[HARD]` with link+Decisions is already `POOL` (skipped even when impl-ready), so a C1 carve-out would grant the CHEAPER-tier lane MORE meeting access than the apex lane, inverting the capability logic — `[ROUTINE]` means a cheap executor suffices, so the pool is its home a fortiori. It would also push the highest-collision-probability items through SKILL.md step 4's C1 path, which does real CODE work (full suite green) while `/meeting`'s advisory claim sits on the DISTINCT key `meeting:<repo>` **specifically so a parallel executor is NEVER refused** (id:0ee1) — a non-blocking design justified by `claim.sh`'s SCOPE INVARIANT that *a meeting is ledger-only* (id:179e). Under single-id-two-views the same id can be live in ROADMAP, so a pool executor could be working it concurrently. That tension is PRE-EXISTING and broader than this item — filed separately as **id:91d1**. Nothing is orphaned by the skip: `unpromoted-scan.sh` (id:2dea) surfaces open TODO items and handoff C2 promotes them; `/meeting` was never how `[ROUTINE]` work reached an executor, and an explicit `/meeting <topic>` still reaches any item on demand. REJECTED (both I and Fable agreed): flagging the C3-routine items as possibly mis-laned — this repo just ran a 22-item advisory at ~9% true-positive (id:bf19), so another such advisory buys noise. Acceptance: `tests/test_classify_hard_lanes.sh` extended with 7 new fixtures incl. the load-bearing "link+Decisions must STILL be EXEC" guard and a head-anchoring case for the two new lanes; `meeting/SKILL.md` skip-list + bucket-print updated (MECH must always print). `verify: cd ~/src/dotclaude-skills && ./meeting/classify.sh . | awk -F'\t' '{print $1}' | sort | uniq -c` — done 2026-07-30 <!-- id:4e3b -->
- [x] [INBOUND routed:0931 from 643f] ESCALATION of routed:643f — the safety classifier now blocks the pool's OWN teardown+liveness hops, and it LEAKS state across runs: loderite run relay-20260728-211354-12764 (wf_0665ebca-7c9) had **6 of 28 agents blocked** — 3x `heartbeat-beat`, 2x `release:loderite:claim`, 1x `heartbeat-stop` — every one reasoning that the runId 'was never established as created via any Workflow call in this session'. **That premise is false**: the front door MINTS the runId in shell and passes it as `args.RUN_ID` (SKILL.md step 3, id:c5ba), so it provably cannot appear in any prior transcript — the classifier is treating the loop's normal design as evidence of forgery. Two concrete harms, both observed: (1) **a stuck lease** — the blocked `release` left `claim.sh peek` showing loderite held by a run whose pid (2988585) was already dead; a human had to release it by hand, and until then every later pool/session would DEFER the repo (the exact collision the lease exists to prevent); (2) **escalation by delegation-suspicion** — one block cites `[Auto-Mode Bypass]` and calls the retry 'tunneling the same underlying manipulation ... via sub-agent delegation to obscure it', i.e. the loop's ordinary retry path is being read as evasion, so the more the pool retries the more adversarial it looks. It also quotes an unrelated USER UTTERANCE from the surrounding conversation ('Continue until it's truly drained!') as grounds — conversational context must not gate mechanical hops. Wanted: move heartbeat/claim-release to a non-LLM `model:"bash"` mechanical hop with no classifier in the path, OR make runId provenance self-evident (the loop minted it — assert that lineage rather than leaving it to inference). Interim: the pool should SURFACE a blocked release in `RELAY_STATUS.md` so a stuck lease is visible instead of silently wedging the next run. Not promoted separately — two `[INPUT — meeting]` items on one root cause. Evidence + the `RELAY_STATUS.md` interim ask now live in the 51f0 block; track there. **CLOSED 2026-07-30 (`/relay human` sweep): the fold is VERIFIED, so this separate row is spent.** Its own text already said it was FOLDED into ROADMAP `id:51f0` and "not promoted separately — track there"; confirmed 2026-07-30 that `id:51f0` is present and OPEN in `ROADMAP.md` and that its block genuinely carries this item's evidence (`ee61` appears 4x and the `6 of 28` measurement 2x in `ROADMAP.md`). Closed as FOLDED-AND-TRACKED-ELSEWHERE, not as built — the work is live under id:51f0. Found during the id:6b1c sweep: this row was mis-reported by `unpromoted-scan.sh` as `laned` because its ONLY lane-shaped token is the phrase "two `[INPUT — meeting]` items on one root cause" sitting **2173 characters** into the line — the deepest prose-lane misread in the corpus, and a vivid instance of the id:6b1c path-3 defect. <!-- id:ee61 -->

- [x] [ROUTINE] **A `gated-on:` marker whose target is not a dispatchable ROADMAP item reads as "waiting" but means "never" — lint it** — origin fix, filed 2026-07-31 after THREE instances in one day: `a955`→`87f5` (target lived only in TODO), `8123`→`1a34` (same), `f6d5`→`8ba1` (target retired 2026-07-24). - [x] [ROUTINE] **PRIMARY FIX — amend `id:c919`'s `workCreated` predicate to VERDICT-CLASS CHANGE (drain quits on the exact round its verdict would have flipped)** — from meeting `docs/meeting-notes/2026-07-31-1231-execute-review-cadence-starvation.md` (D3, re-ranked primary: it is the ONLY decision from that meeting that addresses the observed incident). `id:c919` is CLOSED (`ROADMAP.md:1753`) and **deliberately** excluded gate-writing handbacks — *"they re-tag the parent into a classifier-EXCLUDED lane, which REMOVES work rather than adding it"* — so `workCreated = report.route === 'hard-split' && hbSplit > 0` (`relay-loop.js:2266`). That reasoning counts only *dispatchable `[ROUTINE]`* work, but dropping `actionable_routine_open` to 0 flips the verdict `execute → review`: the round created a **review** unit c919 scored as nothing. Loderite trace, run `relay-20260730-173701-17132`: rounds 8-9 handed back, wrote the gates, both counted dry, K=2 tripped, pool quit on the exact round the verdict changed. **Three MANDATORY clauses** (each closes a hole Fable found): (i) **direct `classify-verdict.sh` invocation, CACHE-BYPASSED** — without it the `id:c3a6` signature cache always answers "unchanged" and this ships as a silent no-op, the banned detector-without-resolution anti-pattern; (ii) predicate scoped **repo-wide** ("this round changed the repo's verdict class"), NOT per-handback causality, since in-repo parallelism (`id:1f4f`) lets another unit flip the class mid-round; (iii) an **oscillation guard** — verdict-class flapping within a window counts as dry, or at minimum the `--max-rounds` seatbelt exit reason distinguishes this case so it fails loudly instead of looking like normal termination. **Out of scope**: changing the K=2 dry threshold. - [x] [ROUTINE] **Amend `id:cc90`: chain-end classifier RE-ASK replaces the `chainDepth === K` forced-review trigger, and correct two false-premise sites** — from meeting `docs/meeting-notes/2026-07-31-1231-execute-review-cadence-starvation.md` (A1 supersedes that meeting's own D1+D2; D2b pins reset). **Why the original trigger was wrong**: executes never chain today (`relay-loop.js:2437-2445`, *"Only reviews chain — an execute never re-enqueues"*), so `chainDepth ≡ 1` and a `chainDepth === K` trigger would NOT have fired for the 16-unaudited-checkpoint incident it was designed to fix; every chain ending *below* K (mid-chain handback, one-unit-per-repo-per-round topology, `contract_met:false`, quota-stop, agent error) escapes it. **What to build**: at chain end the loop re-invokes `classify-verdict.sh`, and the cascade is amended so `review` is reachable while `[ROUTINE]` work is open — the loop supplies the FACT that a chain ended, the classifier decides, so purity is intact and there is no bypass. cc90's K≤3 still bounds chain length but is **no longer the audit trigger**; no separate aging term is built. **Reset semantics (D2b)**: `chainDepth` resets on **strong-audit watermark ADVANCE**, not on review dispatch (dispatch-reset would silently degrade the guard to nothing under apex outage), plus a named escape so an outage degrades to surfaced-and-skipped rather than a silent halt at K per repo. **DEPENDS on `id:1a34`** (stale `last_strong_ckpt`) being fixed or the watermark validated as resolvable first, else a *successful* review still wedges the chain. **Correct BOTH false-premise sites in the same change** — cc90's semantic (a) (*"exactly as a single execute's are today"*) and the `relay-loop.js:2441` comment (*"the execute's own commits are reviewed next pool"*); neither is true, and two places assert it. **Recorded ambiguity for the RED spec to pin**: making `review` UNCONDITIONALLY reachable would restore the 1:1 apex-per-execute cost the meeting rejected. **Accepted cost**: K=3 against 18 open items ⇒ a fixed ~25% apex duty cycle from a hardcoded K. Contract: a chain ending BELOW K still yields a review, AND `review` does not become unconditionally reachable. <!-- id:8123 -->

Both re-verified ticked 2026-07-31. Nothing left to observe or fix here.  <!-- id:7e6d -->

- [x] [ROUTINE] 🔴 **The DOCUMENTED `ckpt-tag.sh` label format never advances `last_strong_ckpt` — id:0a3b's fix is unreachable from its own usage line, and supervised strong checkpoints silently leave the watermark stale** — found 2026-07-30 by integrating a handoff by hand and then checking relay.toml. **The mismatch:** `ckpt-tag.sh:104` detects the strong model with `model="$(grep -oE 'claude-[a-z0-9.-]+' <<<"$label" | head -n1 || true)"`, i.e. it requires a FULL `claude-*` model id in the label; only then does it `toml-set last_strong_ckpt`/`strong_model` (`:106-109`). But the script's OWN usage line (`:5`) documents `[-l "reviewer (fable)"]` and `relay/SKILL.md`'s integrate step says `-l "reviewer (<model>)"` — a BARE model name, which never matches, so the sync is skipped **with no warning** (the `|| true` and the `if` mean the non-match is indistinguishable from success; the WARNING branches at `:107`/`:109` only fire when a matched sync FAILS, never when detection produced nothing). **EVIDENCE it is live, not theoretical — `RELAY_LOG.md` shows both dialects side by side:** the pool writes `## 2026-07-29 15:00 — reviewer (claude-opus-4-8, fable-standin, relay-loop)` (full id → sync fires), while supervised sessions write `## 2026-07-30 00:25 — reviewer (opus)` and `## 2026-07-30 18:57 — reviewer (opus)` (bare → sync silently skipped). **BOTH of 2026-07-30's strong checkpoints failed to advance the watermark**, which is why `last_strong_ckpt` still read `relay-ckpt-20260729-1500` after two same-day strong passes. **This is precisely the failure id:0a3b was built to prevent** — its own rationale reads *"before this, supervised-session checkpoints left the watermark stale and the pool re-dispatched duplicate strong reviews, 2026-07-01"* — so id:0a3b fixed the code path but left it reachable ONLY via an undocumented label spelling. Consequence: a stale `last_strong_ckpt` makes `substantive_unaudited` compute against an old audit ref, so the pool can re-dispatch a duplicate strong review (wasted apex spend) or mis-judge review debt. **Repaired by hand for this checkpoint** (`relay-state-write.sh toml-set … last_strong_ckpt/strong_model`), so relay.toml is currently correct — but the next supervised checkpoint reintroduces it. **Fix — prefer making the documented thing work over documenting the undocumented thing:** accept a bare tier name (`opus`/`fable`/`sonnet`/`haiku`) in the label and map it to the canonical id, keeping the full-id form working; and make a strong-looking label whose model cannot be resolved fail LOUDLY rather than silently no-op (a `reviewer (…)` label that produces no model is a contract gap, not a normal case — id:4347 no-silent-swallow). Align `ckpt-tag.sh:5` and `relay/SKILL.md`'s integrate step on whatever spelling wins. **SEPARATE, smaller observation from the same check:** `relay/SKILL.md`'s model map says `opus` → `claude-opus-4-8`, but this session runs Opus 5 (`claude-opus-5`), so the mapping table is itself stale; I recorded the truthful `strong_model = "claude-opus-5"` rather than the mapped value. Decide whether the map should track the live tier or be dropped in favour of recording the actual model string. Relates id:0a3b, id:e030 (the durable Fable-recheck queue these fields ARE), id:a883. <!-- id:1a34 -->

- [x] [INBOUND routed:08eb from loderite hand-run relay-manual-20260717-110236-21] /relay integrator: try an OCTOPUS merge when N verified units land together, falling back to serial --no-ff on refusal — user suggestion 2026-07-17 ('feel free to try an octopus merge if multiple finish within a minute or two'). Fits the integrator's fail-safe posture: git's octopus strategy REFUSES a merge needing manual resolution rather than half-merging, so it is self-gating — clean multi-head auto-merge or nothing. Preconditions that MUST hold first (do not skip): per-child contract_met + done-check re-derivation + verify-isolation.sh run BEFORE any merge (octopus merges the already-verified set, it never replaces verification). Known trade-off to decide: one octopus commit loses the per-unit --no-ff granularity the ckpt/revert story relies on, and one checkpoint tag then covers N units — so a single bad unit is harder to back out cleanly. Open q: worth it for wall-time, or is serial --no-ff's granularity the better default with octopus only for >=3 same-wave units? — **CLOSED 2026-07-31 — SUPERSEDED by id:b873**, which states verbatim that it "supersedes the open question in routed:08eb with an empirical answer" — and this item IS `routed:08eb`. b873 tested octopus on 2026-07-17, found git's octopus strategy bypasses the `merge=union` driver so every multi-child wave conflicts on RELAY_LOG.md, and concluded: keep serial `--no-ff`. b873 is closed in this same pass. Verified 2026-07-31.  <!-- id:3616 -->
- [x] [INBOUND routed:c3a3 from loderite hand-run relay-manual-20260717-110236-21] OCTOPUS MERGE TESTED 2026-07-17 AND IT DOES NOT WORK FOR RELAY — supersedes the open question in routed:08eb with an empirical answer. git's octopus strategy does NOT honor custom merge drivers: RELAY_LOG.md is declared 'merge=union' in .gitattributes (git check-attr confirms it ACTIVE), yet 'git merge --no-ff <7ccf> <ba3c>' failed with 'ERROR: content conflict in RELAY_LOG.md / fatal: merge program failed'. Since EVERY relay child appends to RELAY_LOG.md by contract, an octopus merge conflicts on EVERY multi-child wave — the union driver that exists precisely to make append-only ledgers mergeable is silently bypassed by that strategy. Notably ROADMAP.md auto-merged CLEAN (the two children ticked disjoint lines), confirming the routed:c28e disjointness thesis holds; RELAY_LOG.md was the sole blocker. Fell back to serial 'git merge --no-ff' per branch (ort strategy, honors union) — both merged cleanly, zero conflicts. CONCLUSION: keep serial --no-ff as the integrator recipe; octopus buys nothing here and loses per-unit revert granularity. If a future wave-merge is still wanted, it must first solve the union-driver bypass (candidates: pre-merge RELAY_LOG concat, or drop RELAY_LOG from the child contract and have the integrator write it). The failure is FAIL-SAFE — octopus aborted cleanly, main untouched, HEAD intact. — **CLOSED 2026-07-31 — this is a FINDING, and the finding is already the shipped status quo.** Re-verified 2026-07-31: `grep -i octopus relay/scripts/relay-loop.js` → **0 hits** (octopus was never wired; the integrator is serial `--no-ff`), and the conclusion is cited as settled fact in `docs/meeting-notes/2026-07-17-1541-semver-trigger-and-fleet-changelog.md:35` ("`routed:c3a3` proved today octopus can't rescue a shared-file collision"). The union-driver-bypass precondition recorded above stands as the gate on any future wave-merge attempt.  <!-- id:b873 -->

- [x] [INBOUND routed:6fd8 from loderite] Additional evidence FOR id:4a5c (issue-tracker substrate), from a LIVE incident not a structural argument: the inbox's vanish-on-resolve semantics make ABSENCE AMBIGUOUS between never-filed and already-resolved, and that ambiguity produced a wrong diagnosis on 2026-07-29. A loderite session filed routed 4d2b, a parallel dotclaude-skills session adopted it as id:8c6f (commit c8a5a77) and ran inbox-done, which DELETES the line; the first session later grepped the inbox, found nothing, and concluded append.sh had silently lost the entry while exiting 0 - then filed a FALSE data-loss defect against a working tool and told the owner past sessions may have lost entries. Root cause is substrate, not carelessness: a queue whose only completion signal is deletion cannot distinguish the two states, so every reader must cross-check a twin marker in another repo to interpret an absence. A tracker makes open-to-closed a first-class transition with history, so absence never needs interpreting. Note this is the SAME class as the dc5b precedent already cited under id:4a5c (get the state out of the shared file) but observed on the ROUTING queue rather than the checkbox ledger, i.e. a second independent instance — **CLOSED 2026-07-31 — MERGED into id:4a5c**, which SURVIVES and stays live. - [x] [ROUTINE] **`commit-ledger.sh` takes a PATH but `human.md` documents it as `<repo>` — a bare repo NAME fails with `ERROR: not a git repo:`** (hit live 2026-07-28 during `/relay human .`: `commit-ledger.sh dotclaude-skills -m … ROADMAP.md TODO.md REVIEW_ME.md` errored out; re-running with `.` succeeded). Fix EITHER side, not both: (a) make `commit-ledger.sh` resolve a bare name against `relay.toml` (honoring `# path:`, per the canonical-own-set rule) and keep accepting a path — preferred, since every other relay front-door surface takes a repo NAME; or (b) correct human.md to `<repo-path>` and leave the script alone. - [x] [ROUTINE] **Make `make install`'s `RELAY_QUOTA_DECAY_7D` default agree with `relay/SKILL.md` — adopt the RISING schedule `0.30:0.90`** (filed 2026-07-23 as `[INPUT — decision]`; **OWNER DECISION 2026-07-24 (relay human): option (a)** — the Makefile default is the STALE source, the SKILL.md doctrine wins) — `Makefile:208` pins `RELAY_ENV_DEFAULTS := RELAY_QUOTA_DECAY_7D=0.30:0.08` under a comment dated *user policy 2026-06-16* ("front-load early, taper to ~8% near reset"), and `install-relay-env` rides the default `install` target, so **every `make install` on every machine rewrites the live value** — observed live 2026-07-23, restored to `0.30:0.90` by hand. The contradicting SKILL.md knob-table doctrine post-dates it and rests on EVIDENCE: weekly quota is use-it-or-lose-it, so the cap must RISE toward reset (`START < END`); a falling `0.40:0.18` schedule false-stopped a healthy run at 24% 7d-util with ~22 h to reset, forfeiting 76% (2026-06-22). **BUILD:** (1) set `RELAY_ENV_DEFAULTS` to `RELAY_QUOTA_DECAY_7D=0.30:0.90`; (2) replace the Makefile comment with a supersession note citing the 2026-06-22 observation, so the 2026-06-16 policy is visibly superseded rather than silently overwritten; (3) **RED spec** — a test that parses the Makefile default's `START:END` and asserts `START < END`, AND asserts `relay/SKILL.md`'s knob table still documents the rising direction, so a future edit to EITHER side fails loudly instead of drifting apart again. Note: the live `~/.claude/settings.json` already holds `0.30:0.90`, so this stops the regression rather than changing today's runtime behaviour on zomni. Instance of the CLAUDE.md *derived doc vs ratified source* rule. <!-- id:74e7 -->
- [x] [ROUTINE] **`relay-doctor`'s install-drift check (id:1102) SILENTLY SKIPS in its normal invocation — the detector for the built-but-not-installed class cannot fire** — `install_drift_check()` (`relay/scripts/relay-doctor.sh:489-493`) does `local mk="$REPO_ROOT/Makefile"` and returns `SKIP` when absent. `REPO_ROOT` is derived from the SCRIPT's own location, so when relay-doctor is invoked through its INSTALLED symlink (`~/.claude/skills/relay/scripts/relay-doctor.sh`, i.e. the way `/relay health` and every doc example call it), `REPO_ROOT` resolves to `~/.claude/skills` — which has no Makefile — and the check skips **every time**. Verified live 2026-07-29: installed path → `SKIP — Makefile not found under /home/tobias/.claude/skills`; source path (`relay/scripts/relay-doctor.sh .`) → `clean (every manifested entry is installed)`. Same run, opposite outcomes, purely from invocation path. **Cost, measured the same day: FOUR undetected install-drift instances** — `fable-config.sh` (broke `/meeting --fabled` step 0f with exit 127, id:18ed/ba27), the `pre-commit-lane-vocab.sh` ratchet (built + CLOSED as id:9ef7 on 2026-07-21 but never symlinked, which is the empirical cause of id:7df1's 106→164 divergence over 8 days), and `diagram-edge-coverage.sh` (landed as id:a225 THIS SESSION, uninstalled within hours). All four were found by `make status` or by hand — never by the check that exists for exactly this. This is the "loud detection whose resolution silently no-ops" anti-pattern in its purest form: it does not even detect. **Fix:** resolve the manifest source independently of the invocation path — locate the Makefile via the symlink TARGET (`readlink -f "$0"` → up to the repo root) rather than via `$REPO_ROOT`, or fall back to `make status`'s manifest walk. **A SKIP on this check must never be silent** — if the manifest genuinely cannot be located, say so as a loud WARN, because "skipped" currently reads identically to "clean" in the summary. Acceptance: invoking relay-doctor via the installed symlink produces the SAME install-drift verdict as invoking it from the repo; a deliberately-unlinked manifest entry is reported MISSING through BOTH paths; and an unlocatable manifest emits a WARN, not a silent skip. Note the sibling SKIP directly above it — `reference-install completeness (id:69ef)` — fails on the same `$REPO_ROOT` assumption and should be fixed in the same change. <!-- children-of:1102 --> <!-- id:cbd2 -->
- [x] [ROUTINE] **`gather-human-backlog.sh` buckets an item as `manual` on a bare `@manual` SUBSTRING, so an item merely *discussing* the `@manual` grammar lands on the "you run these" checklist** — observed live 2026-07-29 during `/relay human .`: `id:af48` (`[HARD — meeting]` "**`drained` machine-verdict + `@wire`/`@manual` grammar split**") was collected as `kind = manual`, i.e. presented as a scenario a human must RUN, when it is a design item ABOUT the tag. Harmless here because a human read it, but the "you run these" list is the one tier the owner acts on directly, so polluting it with design items erodes exactly the list that must stay trustworthy. Same unanchored-substring defect family that `id:bf19` tracks for the roadmap-lint state-claim rules and that `id:0d58`/`id:d259` hit for lane tags — the fix shape is the same: anchor on the tag in its grammatical position (a standalone `@manual` marker on the item line) rather than a bare `grep`. Regression fixture: an item whose body merely mentions `@manual` in prose must NOT bucket as `manual`. <!-- id:05b0 -->
- [x] [ROUTINE] **HIGH PRIORITY — `relay-reconcile.sh --integrate` hardcodes a checkpoint label with NO model id, so every reconcile-integrate silently leaves `last_strong_ckpt` stale** — found 2026-07-31 by the agent that integrated the c480 orphan; owner asked for it to be fixed soon. **The defect, exactly**: `relay/scripts/relay-reconcile.sh:278` calls `"$CKPT_TAG" "$repo" -m "reconcile integrate: $subj" -l "reconcile (auto/human)"` — a hardcoded literal containing no `claude-*` string. `relay/scripts/ckpt-tag.sh:104` decides whether a checkpoint is STRONG by `grep -oE 'claude-[a-z0-9.-]+' <<<"$label"`; on no match it skips the `last_strong_ckpt` / `strong_model` sync at `:106-109` entirely. **And it skips SILENTLY** — the only `WARNING` there fires when a `toml-set` *fails*, never when the sync is *never attempted* — so a reconcile-integrate looks completely successful while the watermark it should have advanced stays where it was. Confirmed live: `relay-ckpt-20260731-1147` (this repo, the c480 orphan integrate) is labelled `reconcile (auto/human)`; the tag is already pushed so it cannot be rewritten. This is the banked `[[ckpt-tag-label-needs-full-model-id]]` hazard, but note the difference that makes it worse: that finding is about a HUMAN typing the documented-but-wrong bare form (`reviewer (opus)`), which a careful operator can avoid — here the bad label is **baked into the script**, so it recurs on *every* reconcile-integrate no matter who runs it and cannot be avoided by care. Compounds `routed:f77d`/`id:3c34` (a `last_strong_ckpt` that does not resolve): one item makes the watermark point at a missing tag, this one makes it fail to advance at all. **What to build**: (1) make `relay-reconcile.sh` record the ACTUAL apex model of the integrating session in the label rather than a fixed string — *open question worth one line of thought, not a guess: a reconcile-integrate may be run by a human, by an apex session, or by `--auto`, so decide whether the model id is passed in by the caller, read from the environment, or whether a reconcile checkpoint should deliberately NOT count as strong at all* (if the last is right, the fix is to say so explicitly in the label + a comment, not to leave it looking like an oversight); (2) regardless of (1), make `ckpt-tag.sh` **LOUD** when a label yields no `claude-*` match — a skipped strong-sync must print to stderr naming the label, never pass silently (`[[no-swallow-stderr]]`, mechanize-first "loud failure, never silent fallback"). Regression fixture: a checkpoint label with no `claude-*` substring must emit a stderr line and must not silently leave `last_strong_ckpt` unchanged-without-comment. Relates `id:3c34`, `id:2e64`, `id:0a3b`. <!-- id:c500 -->
- [x] [ROUTINE] **A DIRTY, uncommitted context-death worktree is still surfaced-and-LEFT, not recovered — the exact case that fired on 2026-07-29 still needs a human** — `id:4df8` closed and is genuinely working for the case it was specced for, but the close does NOT cover the incident that motivated it. `worktree-retire.sh`'s three outcomes are: committed work → branch PARKED as `relay/orphan/<bn>` with the ref kept (**this is fixed and works**); clean+commitless → reaped, no litter (**fixed**); **dirty/unremovable → SURFACED and LEFT on disk, exit 3, "never stash, clean, reset, or force"** (`worktree-retire.sh:13-14`). The RED spec's fixture (c) only asserts the dirty tree is *not silently destroyed* (`tests/…_4df8.sh:68-77`) — so the implementation correctly meets its spec. But the ORIGINAL ask in `routed:3f22` was stronger: *"if the worktree has commits **OR a dirty tree**, **commit-or-park** it as `relay/orphan/<runId>-<verdict>`"*. **Live evidence the gap is real:** run `relay-20260729-111723-7520`'s execute child died with **0 commits and a dirty tree** carrying 47 lines (the id:4df8 implementation itself); it survived only because a human noticed and hand-committed it as `46b260e`. Under the shipped fix that same incident would still end at "surfaced and left" — better than reaped, and it does survive `git worktree prune` since the directory still exists, but recovery is still manual. **Wanted:** on the null-report path, when the tree is dirty and the branch is a relay-owned `relay/<runId>-*` ref, commit the residue to that branch with a loud `WIP … UNVERIFIED` message and park it, so `/relay reconcile` can list it — turning a human-noticed rescue into a mechanical one. **Force-free is preserved**: committing residue onto its own branch destroys nothing, unlike stash/clean/reset which id:373e rightly bans — the ban is on DISCARDING, and this is the opposite. Decide whether this belongs in `worktree-retire.sh` (a 4th outcome) or in the relay-loop caller; the helper's single-target contract (id:6e02) argues for the helper. Acceptance: extend fixture (c) so a dirty worktree yields a reachable ref whose tip contains the residue, and the surfaced line names that ref. <!-- children-of:4df8 --> <!-- id:f272 -->
- [x] [ROUTINE] **`gather-human-backlog.sh` lane detection has NO head-anchoring, so an UNQUOTED lane tag in an item's prose HIJACKS its lane — and in TODO mode that SILENTLY DROPS the item** — found 2026-07-30 while triaging this repo's meeting backlog. `emit_hard_lanes`'s lane chain tests the whole backtick-stripped line and checks the POOL branch FIRST, with no positional constraint. So an item whose own head tag is `[HARD — meeting]` but whose body merely *discusses* `[HARD — pool]` is bucketed `hard_pool`; and because TODO mode drops pool rows (`if (bucket == "pool" || bucket == "untagged") next` — the pool runs those unattended), the item becomes **invisible to `/relay human` entirely**, with no reject line and nothing on stderr. **DEMONSTRATED:** `id:d84f` (the `/filetodo` skill item, TODO.md line 78) is a `[HARD — meeting]` item whose body asks *"which relay lane the research seam gets ([HARD — pool]? a new [RESEARCH] capability…)"* — an unquoted mention, so it is dropped. It was the ONE item my independent own-id extraction found that the collector never emitted in any bucket. **`classify.sh` already solves exactly this** with id:0d58/id:4da4 head-anchoring (read the lane only from the first 120 chars, route on the extracted tag) — gather relies solely on id:306d/id:1bbd backtick-stripping, which helps only when the prose mention happens to be backticked. This is the third instance of the [[lane-tag-hotfix]] "a second substring reader bypasses the id:4da4 anchoring" class. **Fix:** port classify.sh's head-anchoring into `emit_hard_lanes` (extract the lane tag from the item head, then route on the extracted tag rather than substring-matching the whole line), keeping the dual-vocab precedence and the `route:` aliases (which legitimately appear anywhere in the body, so those must keep scanning the full line). **Why this is riskier than it looks and needs its own pass:** anchoring changes the bucket of every item whose head tag and prose disagree, so it must land with a before/after row-count diff per bucket across all own repos, plus fixtures for (a) an unquoted prose mention that must NOT win, (b) a real head tag that must win, (c) a `route:` alias still recognized deep in the body. `tests/test_gather_lane_anchor.sh` exists and must be extended, not replaced. Same silent-under-report family as id:fa5c ([[relay-human-gather-underreport]]) and as the own-id dedup bug fixed alongside this filing. <!-- id:5648 -->
- [x] [ROUTINE] **`unpromoted-scan.sh`'s `primary_lane` fallback reads a lane tag from PROSE on NON-BOLD items, labelling an UNLANED item `promote` — which its own contract forbids** — found 2026-07-30 while diagnosing why a pool never emitted a `review` verdict. `primary_lane` has three paths: (1) tag-before-bold-title `- [ ] [TAG] **title**` — anchored, correct; (2) bold-titled `- [ ] **title** [TAG]` — STRICTLY anchored since id:fb7f, returning NO lane if the tag is not immediately after the closing `**` (that fix was made for exactly this class: *"A bare leftmost-tag scan mislabeled bold-titled items whose ONLY bracket-tag mention was prose deep in the body … as `promote` — 33c2/a505/7b23/b8ae, 2026-07-02"*); (3) **everything else → a leftmost-tag-ANYWHERE scan over the whole line**. Path 3's stated justification is that *"TODO.md's non-bold prose-summary items carry no genuine tag either way"* — **and that assumption is false for the `[INBOUND routed:XXXX from Y] …` class**, which is non-bold, prose-heavy, and *routinely quotes lane tags because such items are frequently bug reports ABOUT lanes. **DEMONSTRATED on id:3e14** (the review-starvation item): its line is non-bold, carries no lane tag, and mentions `[ROUTINE]` at byte offset **296** in the sentence *"while even ONE executor-actionable [ROUTINE] item stays open"* — so path 3 returned `[ROUTINE]` and the scan reported disposition **`promote`**, i.e. "executable lane already tagged → directly promotable". **That directly violates the script's own acceptance #3**, which states it *"NEVER auto-promotes an untagged item with a guessed lane"* — a handoff C2 acting on that row would promote a design-decision item into the executor queue. Head-anchored `classify.sh` read the same line correctly (`C2`), so the two readers disagreed — the tell. (Worked around at the source by laning id:3e14 explicitly; the scanner bug is unfixed.) **Fix:** extend the id:fb7f strictness to path 3 — for a non-bold item, accept a lane tag only in the leading position (immediately after `- [ ] `, allowing a preceding `[INBOUND …]`/`[<target>]` prefix bracket), never a tag found at arbitrary depth; return empty (→ `surface`) otherwise, which is the conservative disposition the contract already prefers. **NOTE this is the THIRD reader in this family** — after `meeting/classify.sh` (fixed 2026-07-30, routed:f1e1 + id:4e3b) and `gather-human-backlog.sh` (id:5648, filed) — so consider whether the anchored primary-lane parse belongs in ONE shared helper the three readers call, rather than a fourth independent reimplementation (id:415b determinism gate: ≥2 consumers is long since satisfied). Existing `tests/test_unpromoted_scan_anchoring.sh` (roadmap:1312) covers a DIFFERENT anchoring defect — the twin-check `grep -qF "id:$token"` — so extend it or add a sibling; do not assume it guards this. **FULL-REPO SWEEP DONE 2026-07-30 — blast radius MEASURED, and it is small but real: 3 of 56 rows.** Checked every `promote` and `laned` row from `unpromoted-scan.sh` against a properly head-anchored lane read. **`promote`: 24 rows, ALL genuine** (every one has its lane tag at offset 0) — so the dangerous direction, a design item wrongly entering the executor queue, hit exactly ONE item (id:3e14, already laned at source). **`laned`: 32 rows, 30 genuine, 2 MISREAD** — and this is the quieter, more insidious direction, because `laned` means "lane already decided, verdict-neutral", so an unlaned item reads as RESOLVED and never reaches triage at all: (i) **id:be40** — `[INPUT — access]` picked from offset 132, inside the parenthetical *"while [INPUT — access] items exist"*; genuinely UNLANED, deliberately left unlaned here rather than guessed (the scan's own acceptance #3), so it is on the next handoff C2 lane-triage list; (ii) **id:ee61** — `[INPUT — meeting]` picked from **offset 2173**, from the phrase *"two `[INPUT — meeting]` items on one root cause"*; that row is now CLOSED (its fold into id:51f0 was verified), so it needs no lane. **ALL THREE affected items are the `[INBOUND routed:XXXX …]` shape**, exactly as predicted — non-bold, prose-heavy, and quoting lane tags because they are reports ABOUT lanes. That confirms the diagnosis and bounds the fix: path 3 only ever misfires on this class today, but the class is growing (10 INBOUND items were routed into this repo on 2026-07-30 alone), so the defect gets worse with use rather than better. <!-- id:6b1c -->
- [x] [INBOUND routed:4f48 from loderite] orphan-scan.sh resolves children:/gated-on: tokens against TODO.md ∪ TODO.archive.md ONLY (header line 40), but in a relay repo the SEAMS live in ROADMAP.md — so a real dependency on a ROADMAP-only id is untypeable and the item can only be marked gate-prose-only, losing the typed edge; loderite hit this on id:3d11 (gate on ca44) and id:5d00 (hard-split umbrella whose 14 seams ALL exist in ROADMAP.md, so a children: marker would report every one as dangling); consider resolving tokens against ROADMAP.md too, or a typed marker for an out-of-namespace local id <!-- id:9be0 -->
- [x] [INBOUND routed:c27e from loderite] md-merge.py update-ids has no APPEND mode and its whole-line replace fails DESTRUCTIVELY-BUT-SILENTLY (exit 0): passing a partial line replaces the entire item, so an 'APPEND: <text>' payload wiped a 1400-char TODO item down to the fragment and still reported success; recovered only because the tree was committed. Add an append/suffix mode, or refuse a replacement line that lacks the item's leading '- [ ]'/'- [x]' checkbox and its <!-- id:XXXX --> tag <!-- id:0af4 -->

## 2026-08
- [x] [ROUTINE] **Completeness test: assert every `model:'bash'`-dispatched relay script is in `mechanical-proxy.py ALLOWED_RELAY_SCRIPTS`** — RECURRENCE GUARD (mechanize-first). This gap shipped TWICE: id:86a2's `discover-prelude.sh` was registered in the Makefile manifest but NOT in `ALLOWED_RELAY_SCRIPTS`, so its `model:'bash'` dispatch would 404 at the proxy (caught only by the id:24ec executor while adding `discover-chunk.sh`, then hand-fixed b89df99). A hermetic test should grep `relay-loop.js` for every `relay/scripts/<X>.sh` invoked inside a ` ```relay-mech `/`model:'bash'` fence and assert each `<X>.sh` ∈ `ALLOWED_RELAY_SCRIPTS` — a deterministic guard for exactly the class the final review flagged. Relates id:86a2, id:24ec, id:6176 (mech hops), id:4239 (mech-preflight). — **THIRD SHIP 2026-07-29, and this one cost real work.** `id:4df8`'s `retireDeadWorktree()` (merged `relay-ckpt-20260729-1255`) dispatches `~/.claude/skills/relay/scripts/worktree-retire.sh` inside a ` ```relay-mech ` fence as `model:MECH_MODEL`, but **`worktree-retire.sh` is NOT in `ALLOWED_RELAY_SCRIPTS`** (`grep -c worktree-retire relay/scripts/mechanical-proxy.py` = 0). The proxy therefore refuses the command and **fails OPEN to the real API**, where no model named `bash` exists → the hop dies with `There's an issue with the selected model (bash)`. Observed live in run `relay-20260729-142725-13077`: `[retire-death:dotclaude-skills] failed`. **Consequence: the context-death parking that id:4df8 exists to provide silently did not happen** — no `relay/orphan/*` ref was created, the worktree stayed registered, and an execute child's uncommitted `ROADMAP.md` + `roadmap-lint.sh` work was left one `git worktree prune` from deletion (hand-salvaged as `06cffba`). **Note the failure SHAPE, because it is the argument for this item**: a non-allowlisted command does not fail loudly at authoring time or in the suite — it fails at RUNTIME, in the exact rare path (a dying child) where nobody is watching and the cost is highest. The fail-open design is right for security; the missing completeness check is what makes it a silent trap. **This ship also passed a supervised apex review** (mine, same day): the review verified wiring, test-integrity, the done-check and the design, and did not check allowlist membership — evidence that human/LLM review does not substitute for this deterministic guard, which is precisely the mechanize-first argument. — verified green on 2026-07-31 (tests/test_mech_fence_allowlist_completeness_5bbb.sh re-run independently; shipped by the pool at 22:02) <!-- id:5bbb -->

- [x] [HARD — pool] 🔴 **`/relay stop` DID NOT STOP THE POOL — the sentinel was consumed and the loop ran 6+ more rounds over 4 hours** (observed live 2026-07-31, run `relay-20260731-174550-21952`). Timeline, all from artifacts not memory: owner asked to exclude a repo at ~17:50 → `: > ~/.config/relay/STOP` written 17:51 → `~/.claude/logs/relay-stop-sentinel.log` records `2026-07-31T15:54:39Z consumed STOP sentinel path=/home/tobias/.config/relay/STOP content=""` (17:54 local) and the file was gone → **yet `relay-events.jsonl` shows the same runId dispatching round 2 at 18:08, and rounds 6/7/8 through 22:02**, with a live `claim.sh` lease on dotclaude-skills at 22:01:52. A `/relay stop` at round ~2 that leaves the pool running to round 8 is a failure of the primary graceful-stop mechanism, and the operator has no signal — the consume log says "consumed", which reads as success. **CONSEQUENCE, the reason this is 🔴 and not a papercut**: the owner's exclusion never took effect. lodelore was re-dispatched `mode:hard` at round 2 (18:08:57) and INTEGRATED at 18:19 (`relay-ckpt-20260731-1818`, ids `["5825"]`) — i.e. the repo the owner had just killed a run on, and explicitly asked to exclude, was worked and pushed ~25 minutes after the stop was requested. **DIAGNOSED 2026-07-31 22:05 — branch (1) CONFIRMED, branch (2) REFUTED for this incident (owner asked the discriminating question directly).** Evidence chain, all artifacts: (i) this run's workflow journal contains **8 `stopRequested` readings across its 8 rounds, ALL `false`, zero `true`** — the intended pool never saw a stop, so the prelude→outer-loop plumbing is not at fault; (ii) `grep -rln stop-sentinel` shows **`discover-prelude.sh` is the only caller** of `stop-sentinel.sh`, so the consumer must have been another POOL's round-top prelude (the live `discovery-producer` systemd unit runs `discover-repos-mechanical.sh`, NOT the prelude — checked, it is not a consumer); (iii) the parallel run `relay-20260731-164158-16320` (the lodelore session the owner had just killed) last beat at **17:53:46** and its heartbeat was moved into `heartbeats.done/` at **17:55** (the `.done` DIRECTORY mtime — the file's own mtime is 17:53 because `mv` preserves it, a trap worth naming), i.e. it tore down ~20 s after the 17:54:39 consume; (iv) the only other run that day, `relay-20260731-150943-29325`, retired at 16:03, two hours earlier. **So the killed-and-winding-down lodelore pool consumed the owner's stop and stopped itself, while the pool he was aiming at ran on for 6 more rounds.** The self-consuming design — deliberate, an anti-wedge property so a stale sentinel cannot false-stop a later pool — is exactly what makes it steal-once. **SECOND, INDEPENDENT PATH TO THE SAME SYMPTOM, found while confirming the above (fix must close BOTH):** `discover-prelude.sh:136` calls the sentinel as `"$STOP_SENTINEL" check … || { echo '{"stopRequested":false}'; … }`, and `stop-sentinel.sh` performs its `rm -- "$path"` BEFORE the log-append and final `echo` — so ANY nonzero exit after the `rm` consumes the sentinel and reports "no stop requested", fail-SAFE in name but fail-OPEN in effect for the stop. A consumed-but-unhonored stop is unobservable either way, because the consume log records only path+content. **Original two candidates, retained for the record:** **TWO CANDIDATE CAUSES (as filed; (1) now confirmed, (2) refuted):** (1) **the sentinel path is GLOBAL and un-scoped** (`~/.config/relay/STOP`, one path, no runId/pid in the file or its name) while the SKILL's own id:11c6 singleton guard EXEMPTS `--afk` and every directed/scoped mode so multiple pools "run freely in parallel" — so a second live pool (here `relay-20260731-164158-16320`, whose lease was still being refreshed at 17:47:46) can reach a round boundary first, consume the sentinel, and stop ITSELF while the intended pool never sees it. Under this branch `/relay stop` is unaimable by construction whenever >1 pool is live, and the self-consuming design (a deliberate anti-wedge property) is exactly what makes it steal-once. (2) The intended pool consumed it but `stopRequested` did not propagate to the outer `while` (a prelude→loop plumbing break), in which case the log line is written before the decision it reports. **Discriminating probe**: the consume log records only path+content — add the CONSUMING runId+pid to that line and re-observe; branch (1) shows a runId that is not the intended pool's. **Fix directions (owner's call, not settled here)**: scope the sentinel per-run (`STOP.<runId>`, with the bare path retained as "stop them all"), and/or have the consuming prelude REFUSE a sentinel whose target runId is not its own and leave it in place. Either way the front door should ECHO which run it is stopping, and the pool should log `stopReason` where an operator can see it. Related: the same global-singleton-file shape is why a lingering unconsumed sentinel can silently stop the NEXT pool (already documented in SKILL.md Stop mode as a self-consume property — that mitigation is what fails here). — FIXED 2026-07-31 by id:cd94 (run-scoped sentinel + aimable stop-request.sh + consume-order reversal); covered by tests/test_stop_sentinel_run_scoped.sh + tests/test_stop_request_target.sh (23 assertions), suite 352/0; the new `run=` log field then named a real steal within the hour. <!-- id:31ce -->

- [x] [ROUTINE] **`DECIDED-LEFT-OPEN` (roadmap-lint rule 3(b), id:dafa/5533) has two false-positive classes that make it permanently noisy on exactly the items it should ignore** — found 2026-07-26 while fixing ai-codebench id:8bea. **(a) No `@container` exemption.** Rule 3(a) `DECOMPOSED-CONTAINER` explicitly exempts `@container` (`roadmap-lint.sh:352`, `"$line" != *@container*`), but rule 3(b) does not (`:368`, `state_claim_direction_i "$line"` with no marker check). A `@container` parent is BY CONSTRUCTION a decomposed item that records a decision and legitimately stays open until its seams close — so every correctly-marked container trips 3(b) forever, and the WARN is unfixable without deleting the decision record the container exists to carry. **(b) The scoped-assertion strip is too narrow.** `lib-state-claim.sh:73` strips only the exact form `id:XXXX[[:space:]]+is[[:space:]]+(TERMINAL|DECIDED <date>)`, i.e. it requires a literal " is ". Real prose about OTHER ids almost never uses it — ai-codebench 8bea's body says `id:244b CLOSED matrix-complete 2026-06-30` and `discovery gather-trim DONE + ROADMAP archiver DONE`, all terminal words scoped to other ids/artifacts, none matched by the strip, so the rule fires on an item asserting nothing about itself. Net effect on 8bea: a permanent WARN that auto-files a REVIEW_ME box (`**ROADMAP item id:8bea — roadmap-lint WARN: DECIDED-LEFT-OPEN** (review 2026-07-26)`) for a non-issue — the human triages noise, which is the failure mode id:dafa exists to prevent. Fix: (a) add the `@container` carve-out to 3(b) mirroring 3(a); (b) widen the strip to `id:XXXX\s+(is\s+)?(TERMINAL)` and/or require the terminal word to be near a SELF-reference before firing. Both changes must land in `lib-state-claim.sh`/`roadmap-lint.sh` together with a `todo-conformance.sh` twin-check update — the lib header states both consumers MUST return the same answer, so a one-sided fix silently diverges them. Regression fixtures: an `@container` line, and a line whose only terminal words are scoped to other ids. <!-- children-of:968c --> — **CO-TICKED 2026-08-01 (/meeting ledger-hygiene sweep): closed in ROADMAP.md:2123 as `[x]`, TODO twin was left `[ ]` (single-id-two-views drift flagged by `orphan-scan.sh --cross-ledger`).** Close verified before ticking, not assumed: `tests/test_state_claim_container_fp_bf19.sh` exists and PASSES, and the fix lives in `relay/scripts/lib-state-claim.sh`. <!-- id:bf19 -->
