- [x] [INPUT — meeting] **RESOLVED 2026-08-10 — meeting `docs/meeting-notes/2026-08-10-0906-tracker-substrate-replacing-markdown-ledgers.md`: tracker pilot ratified (option 2 direction), children `id:2bb1`/`94ce`/`90f2`/`c17d`/`8066`/`f116`/`a532`/`6f01`/`da1a`/`330d` filed above. TWO of this item's own questions were NOT weighed by that meeting — its option (3) (tracker as SSOT for the execution queue only) and the cross-machine/offline write path — both carried forward on `id:330d`, surfaced not decided.** — original brief follows: **The `TODO.md`/`ROADMAP.md` mechanism is too complicated — evaluate moving to a real issue tracker (Vikunja or equivalent)** (owner 2026-07-29, direct ask) — the markdown-ledger substrate has accreted past the point where its cost is obviously worth its benefit, and the owner wants build-vs-buy re-examined at the SUBSTRATE level. **Measured surface as of 2026-07-29 (this repo alone):** 3065 ledger lines across 4 files (`TODO.md` 491, `ROADMAP.md` 1901, `REVIEW_ME.md` 172, `TODO.archive.md` 501); **38 scripts** under `relay/scripts/`, `meeting/`, `todo-update/`, `tools/` read or write those files; **6 distinct HTML-comment marker grammars** (`id:`, `routed:`, `children-of:`, `children:`, `gated-on:`, `relay:`) plus a lane-tag vocabulary that is **mid-migration and diverging** (id:7df1: 164 old-vocab tags across 17 repos, up from 71 — see id:ad7c). Bespoke machinery a real tracker gives for free: `md-merge.py` (flock'd line-scoped writer, because two writers to one markdown file cannot merge — checkbox toggles are non-unionable), `commit-ledger.sh` (atomic scoped commit so a half-write doesn't wedge the pool's dirty-guard), `orphan-scan.sh --cross-ledger` (drift between the TWO ledgers holding the SAME id — the single-id-two-views model), `roadmap-lint.sh` + `todo-conformance.sh` (twin grammar validators that must return identical answers or silently diverge), `unpromoted-scan.sh`, `archive-done.sh`, `lane-convert.sh`, `scan-routed.sh`/`inbox-done` (a hand-rolled cross-repo routing queue). **This is a genuine build-vs-buy question, not a complaint** — most of the above is re-implementing relations, state transitions, dependencies, cross-project links, and concurrent-write safety that an issue tracker has as primitives. <!-- id:4a5c -->

  **REOPENS two prior decisions — the meeting must diff against them, not ignore them (derived-doc-vs-ratified-source rule):**
  - **2026-05-13 D1** (`docs/meeting-notes/2026-05-13-1950-global-todo-skill.md`): "lever-first (Option 5) + standing posture defer to GH Issues (Option 6)", with the GH-Issues path **gated on the topology-3 trigger** (first outside PR/issue). That trigger has **not** fired. This item reopens it on a DIFFERENT premise the 2026-05-13 meeting never weighed: it evaluated tracker-adoption as an *external-collaboration* need, and correctly found none — the new argument is *internal maintenance cost*, which is a different consumer class. Record any change as an explicit AMENDMENT on an unconsidered premise, never as a reinterpretation.
  - **id:36f1's option (C)** (this file, the DEMAND-PULLED 2026-06-25 block): already names **Vikunja / Focalboard / Plane** and steers **"A or B over C"** — but scoped as a *viewer* ("Treat as a viewer, not a source", one-way md→tool push, NEVER tool→md write-back). **This item asks the strictly larger question 36f1 deliberately did not:** should the tracker be the SOURCE, i.e. should md stop being SSOT? A "no" to 36f1(C)-as-viewer does not settle that.

  **The hard part, and why it needs a meeting rather than a spike:** `md = SSOT` is load-bearing for things a tracker does NOT obviously replace — the ledgers are **git-versioned** (history, blame, offline, no service to run), they are **agent-writable in-band** (every relay child, `/meeting`, and executor edits them as files under a flock, with git surfacing conflicts), and they are **diffable in review** (a relay `--no-ff` merge shows ledger changes alongside code). A tracker owning its own DB fights all three. Options the meeting should weigh, at minimum: **(1)** keep md as SSOT, buy only the VIEW (= id:36f1 as already steered); **(2)** tracker as SSOT with md generated read-only from it (inverts the current derived-index direction — relates id:2840/id:75db); **(3)** tracker as SSOT for a SUBSET (e.g. `ROADMAP.md`'s execution queue moves, `TODO.md`'s design ledger stays md — the two already have different shapes and different writers); **(4)** stay md, but aggressively DELETE machinery instead of adding (the honest null option — e.g. dissolving single-id-two-views would remove the cross-ledger scanner, the twin linters, and half the drift class outright). **Decide the criterion before the tool** — self-hosting cost, offline/agent write path, git-history loss, migration cost for ~3000 lines and 6 marker grammars across 54 own repos, and what happens to the `routed:` cross-repo inbox. Relates id:36f1, id:2840, id:75db, id:7df1/ad7c, id:2dea, id:a6e1.

  **STRONGEST ARGUMENT FOR, added by the owner 2026-07-29 — and it is NOT a fresh intuition, a prior meeting already ratified its general form.** Owner: *"parallel sessions make a more database-based approach even more sensible."* The supporting precedent is **id:dc5b (meeting `docs/meeting-notes/2026-07-19-1726-relay-meeting-state-machine-flowcharts.md`, D3/D3b)**, which established as a reusable invariant: a merge-time collision on a **non-union ledger file** (checkbox `[ ]`↔`[x]` toggles) is **INVARIANT under git topology** — worktrees, real-remotes, CoW copies and separate git-dirs all leave the three-way merge byte-identical, and an execute+review pair usually conflicts on the *same item's line*, which is semantically unmergeable in any topology. Its verbatim conclusion: **"Only getting the state out of the shared file dissolves a ledger-merge collision."** dc5b named C3/id:2840 (move checkbox+verdict state into a derived index) as "the true structural dissolution". **A real tracker DB is the stronger form of exactly that move** — so id:4a5c's options (2)/(3) are not a new direction, they are dc5b's ratified dissolution taken further, and the meeting should treat dc5b as supporting precedent rather than re-deriving it.

  **Quantified concurrency tax (measured 2026-07-29):** **28 of the 38** ledger-touching scripts use `flock` — `append.sh`, `claim.sh`, `commit-ledger.sh`, `md-merge.py`, `git-lock-push.sh`, `ckpt-tag.sh`, `relay-state-write.sh`, `memory-append.sh`, `heartbeat.sh`, `inject.sh`, `decision-queue.sh`, `scan-routed.sh`, `roadmap-archive.sh`, `persona-state.py`, … Only **2** files could be made union-mergeable (`RELAY_LOG.md`, `meeting/personas.md` — both append-only); everything with a checkbox cannot. On top of the locks sit a whole second layer of concurrency workarounds that exist ONLY because the substrate is files: the `claim.sh` lease + mtime-TTL + `peek`/`reap`, the distinct `meeting:<repo>` claim key (id:0ee1), peek-and-warn exempting ledger writes from the lease (id:c144), the deferred-writeback drop file (id:2c42), the tracked-dirty guard (id:aa93), the scoop window (id:debf), `orphan-scan --cross-ledger` as a drift backstop, dc5b's own C2 "never two units per repo per round" invariant, one-writer-to-main (id:5a39), and the REJECTION of worktree-per-`/meeting` (id:af04) *specifically because* checkbox merges are non-unionable. Row-level concurrency, transactions and atomic state transitions are primitives in any database; here each is a hand-built mechanism with its own failure mode.

  **REFRAMED BY THE OWNER 2026-07-29 — the DRIVER is missing CAPABILITY, not complexity-reduction.** Owner: *"worth considering, yes, but we'll see. Even switching to / using e.g. **fossil scm** for the issue tracking might be viable. Ultimately we need better **dependency (gated-on) tracking, what's in progress, what's elaborated on** and so on."* This supersedes the framing of the paragraphs above — they argue the substrate costs too much, which is a *reason to leave*; this is a *reason to arrive somewhere specific*, and it is the stronger brief. The meeting should evaluate candidates against these capabilities FIRST and treat the maintenance-cost argument as secondary. **Measured state of the three named capabilities (2026-07-29, this repo):**
    - **Dependency (`gated-on`) tracking — partial, ~9% coverage.** 40 typed `gated-on:`/`children-of:` edges (id:46f6) across 459 open+closed items; every other dependency is prose ("blocked until", "DEP:", a bare id reference) that no tool can traverse. There is no transitive query — "what does closing X unblock?" is answered by hand or not at all, which is exactly what id:c3f6 (keystone-unblock triage) and id:36f1 (blocking-DAG view) were filed to fix, both still open.
    - **What's in progress — NOT EXPRESSIBLE AT ALL.** The checkbox is a strict 2-state machine: 387 `- [ ]` + 72 `- [x]`, no third state anywhere in either ledger. In-flight work lives entirely OUTSIDE the ledger, in `claim.sh` claims + `RELAY_STATUS.md`, and **evaporates when the run ends** — so a ledger read can never distinguish "nobody has started this" from "an executor is on it right now" from "a child died holding it". `🚧` (24 uses) means *gated/blocked*, not in-progress, and the 6 ad-hoc "WIP"/"in progress" prose mentions are unparseable. This is the single clearest capability gap of the three.
    - **What's elaborated on — no state, only a lint proxy.** Nothing distinguishes a raw one-line idea from a designed item from one with a RED spec authored. The nearest thing is `roadmap-lint`'s `NO-ACCEPTANCE-NO-TWIN` WARN (id:213a), which infers un-elaboration from the absence of an Acceptance/Tests/Done-check clause — a heuristic proxy for a property that should just be tracked.
    - **"and so on" is deliberately unfinished** — completing the capability list is meeting input, not something to guess here. Obvious further candidates from this repo's own machinery: priority/ordering, per-item ownership (which actor: executor / apex / human / mechanical), age and staleness, and cross-repo links (currently the bespoke `routed:` inbox).

  **CANDIDATE ADDED BY THE OWNER: Fossil SCM's built-in ticket tracker** — and its data model is a notably good fit for the two objections raised above, which is worth stating precisely rather than as enthusiasm. Verified against the Fossil docs 2026-07-29: tickets are **ticket-change ARTIFACTS**, not files in the source tree; they push/pull/sync **exactly like every other artifact**, and *"independently entered changes merge automatically when artifacts are shared between repositories, and tickets do not branch"* ([bugtheory.wiki](https://fossil-scm.org/home/doc/tip/www/bugtheory.wiki), [sync.wiki](https://fossil-scm.org/home/doc/trunk/www/sync.wiki)). That is significant twice over: **(i)** an append-only event artifact IS union-mergeable, so it dissolves dc5b's non-union-checkbox collision class WITHOUT needing a central service — it is the *"or make the file union-mergeable"* branch of dc5b's own dissolution, which the paragraphs above under-weighted; and **(ii)** because it syncs like a commit, it keeps offline writes, git-like history, and no always-on dependency — **defusing the availability counter below**, which was the main argument against a DB. **Caveats, from the same docs:** Fossil's autosync is **not transitive**, so a genuinely distributed topology has no single source of truth (relevant with 4 machines — though fievel already acts as a de-facto hub); a clone does not receive private branches and may miss SQL-level ticket customizations the sync protocol cannot see ([backup.md](https://fossil-scm.org/home/doc/trunk/www/backup.md)). Also unevaluated: whether Fossil's ticket schema can express typed dependency EDGES and a real state machine, which is the actual requirement — a tracker that syncs beautifully but only offers open/closed would fail the reframe above. **Adoption shape is open**: Fossil can be used for tickets ONLY, alongside git for code (it is not an all-or-nothing SCM migration) — that variant should be costed explicitly.

  **SECOND INDEPENDENT INSTANCE, from a LIVE incident rather than a structural argument (id:f762 / `routed:6fd8`, filed same day):** the inbox's **vanish-on-resolve** semantics make ABSENCE AMBIGUOUS between never-filed and already-resolved — and that ambiguity produced a wrong diagnosis on 2026-07-29 **in this very session**. A loderite session filed `routed:4d2b`; this session adopted it as `id:8c6f` (commit `c8a5a77`) and ran `inbox-done`, which DELETES the line; the loderite session later grepped the inbox, found nothing, concluded `append.sh` had silently lost the entry while exiting 0, and filed a FALSE data-loss defect against a working tool — telling the owner that past sessions may have lost entries. **Root cause is substrate, not carelessness:** a queue whose only completion signal is deletion cannot distinguish the two states, so every reader must cross-check a twin marker in another repo just to interpret an absence. A tracker makes open→closed a first-class transition **with history**, so absence never needs interpreting. This is the same class as the dc5b precedent already cited above (get the state out of the shared file) but observed on the **routing queue** rather than the checkbox ledger — i.e. the failure mode reproduces on a second, structurally different surface, which is stronger evidence than either instance alone.

  **Owner posture 2026-07-29: NOT DECIDED — "worth considering, yes, but we'll see."** Nothing here is settled; do not transcribe any candidate as chosen.

  **The honest counter the meeting must weigh (do not let the above go unopposed):** today's parallelism is **cross-machine** (zomni / cartmanjaro / fievel / pixel) and git syncs it for free with no service running, no availability requirement, and offline writes that reconcile later. A central DB needs a reachable, backed-up, always-on service — which converts a class of *merge conflicts* into a class of *availability failures*, and an agent that currently writes a file would need a CLI/API path that works from a Termux session on a phone. So the argument is not "DB strictly dominates": it is that the collision class dc5b identified is unfixable in the file substrate, while the availability class is a NEW cost the file substrate does not have. Option (3) — tracker as SSOT for the **execution queue only** (`ROADMAP.md`, the high-churn multi-writer surface), md retained for the design ledger (`TODO.md`, low-churn, mostly human/meeting-written) — is the shape that takes dc5b's dissolution where the collisions actually are while paying the availability cost only where it buys something.

Original: HIGH PRIORITY — `id:4df8` is ticked `[x]` but its machinery is INERT at runtime: add `worktree-retire.sh` to `ALLOWED_RELAY_SCRIPTS` and assert it** — `retireDeadWorktree()` (`relay-loop.js:2042`, merged `relay-ckpt-20260729-1255`) dispatches `~/.claude/skills/relay/scripts/worktree-retire.sh` in a ` ```relay-mech ` fence as `model:MECH_MODEL`, but that script is **absent from `mechanical-proxy.py`'s `ALLOWED_RELAY_SCRIPTS`**. - [x] [INPUT — decision] **RESOLVED 2026-08-10 (owner):** the two questions `id:4a5c` posed that the tracker meeting did not weigh. **(1) Option (3) — tracker as SSOT for the EXECUTION QUEUE ONLY (`ROADMAP.md` moves, `TODO.md` stays markdown): CONSIDERED AND DECLINED.** Reason: it *preserves* single-id-two-views — the duplication whose dissolution was the meeting's central insight ("promotion is just a status") — and relocates the drift **across a substrate boundary**, where `orphan-scan --cross-ledger` cannot span it; you would have to build a new md↔DB drift checker to replace the guard you lose. That is a worse duplication than today's. Its appeal (design rationale staying in git) survives regardless: the owner's objection was to burying *issue tracking* in the source repo, and `docs/meeting-notes/` is a separate artifact that is not moving. **Kept named as a fallback shape** if the availability cost proves prohibitive. **(2) Cross-machine / offline write path: converted from a design question into a PILOT ACCEPTANCE CRITERION** — during the shadow window, log every occasion you would have written from a machine that could not reach the tracker. A tunnelled API is about as reachable from Termux as a git push (`cloudflare-tunnel-deploy`, `ssh-lan-fallback`, fievel as de-facto hub); what is genuinely lost is **offline** writes, and that is cheap to measure rather than argue. <!-- children-of:4a5c --> <!-- id:330d -->
- [x] [ROUTINE] **Lint the quoting hazard in embedded foreign-language literals — an apostrophe inside `python3 -c '…'` silently corrupts the program, and `bash -n` passes** (filed 2026-07-30 from the id:0cf5 relay review, where I caused and then caught it) — `discover-repo.sh` embeds a ~90-line Python program inside a single-quoted `python3 -c '...'`. Adding an explanatory comment containing `lib-state-claim.sh's` closed the shell quote at that apostrophe; the file remained **valid bash** (`bash -n` clean), so nothing local complained — the corruption only surfaced at runtime as `IndentationError: expected an indented block after 'if' statement`, in a Python traceback pointing at a line the author never wrote (`# lib-state-claim.shs`). The full suite caught it (5 red: `test_discover_repo`, `test_discover_repos_mechanical`, `test_discover_chunk_mechanized_24ec`, `test_discovery_producer_heartbeat`, `test_orphan_additive_surface_bc49`), so the backstop works — but only for embedded programs that a test actually executes, and only after a full `make test`. **Third instance of one class**, which is what lifts it over the id:415b determinism gate rather than being a one-off: (1) `broker-curl.sh` JSON — apostrophes breaking single-quoted literals, already a CLAUDE.md Gotcha; (2) id:5bac/aec5 — `relay-loop.js` prompt-template runtime crashes that `node --check` + grep miss; (3) this. All three are *a foreign language embedded in a host literal, where the host's syntax check cannot see the guest*. Wanted: a cheap mechanical check over `relay/scripts/*.sh` that extracts each `python3 -c '…'` / `awk '…'` body and runs the guest's own syntax checker (`python3 -c "compile(...)"`, `awk --version`-style parse), so the corruption is caught at lint time rather than by whichever test happens to execute that path. Note the existing `tests/test_workflow_template_lint.sh` is the same idea for `relay-loop.js` templates — extend that shape, do not start fresh. Acceptance: a fixture script with an apostrophe-corrupted embedded Python body is REJECTED by the lint; the current tree passes. <!-- id:ef9e -->
- [x] [ROUTINE] 🔴 **HIGH — `changelog-append.sh` leaves a `.changelog.lock` that most repos do NOT gitignore, so running the changelog deriver PERMANENTLY dirties a repo and the dirty-guard then silently excludes it from every future pool round** — relay inflicts this on itself. `changelog-append.sh:85` creates `$repo/.changelog.lock`; its own comment at `:23` asserts the lock "matches the `*.lock` gitignore" — **that assertion is false for the repos it runs in** (CLAUDE.md's "a claim about CODE behaviour is a derived doc too"). Verified 2026-08-10: `puzzle-pwa/.gitignore` has **no lock entry at all**, and `zkm/plugins/zkm-stt/.gitignore` ignores only `.git-lock-push.lock` — not `*.lock`, not `.changelog.lock`. Both repos' entire dirty state is exactly one untracked `.changelog.lock`. **Consequence (observed run `relay-20260810-103858-20326`)**: both were dropped from the round before classification and appear NOWHERE in `RELAY_STATUS.md` — not Skipped, not Blocked — so the exclusion is invisible and self-perpetuating (nothing will ever clean a lock nobody can see). **`puzzle-pwa` is `income = true`**, so this silently parks revenue work. ~~**Fix direction — prefer dissolving over guarding**: have `changelog-append.sh` remove its own lockfile on exit (a flock does not need the file to persist), so no gitignore coordination is needed in ~50 repos; failing that, `--fix`-style ensure the entry.~~ — **THAT DIRECTION WAS RATIFIED UNSOUND AT HANDOFF AND IS NOT WHAT SHIPPED.** Remove-on-exit fails for two independent reasons: (1) unlinking a flock'd path is the classic unlink race — a waiter holds the old inode while a new arrival creates and locks a different one, putting two writers in the critical section; (2) decisively, the dirty-guard is an **asynchronous sampler** (`gather-repo-state.sh` runs `git status` from another process), so a lock present only *during* the append is still observable and still parks the repo — remove-on-exit does not even solve the stated problem. **What shipped instead**: the lock moved to the target's GIT DIR (`<gitdir>/changelog-append.lock`) via `rev-parse --absolute-git-dir`, matching the pattern three siblings already use (`ckpt-tag.sh:155`, `version-bump.sh:147`, `diary-append.sh:116`) — nothing is unlinked, nothing needs gitignoring in ~50 repos, and `git status` can never see it. A `$TMPDIR` fallback keyed on the resolved path covers non-git targets (`test_changelog_derive.sh` builds fixtures with plain `mkdir`, so a bare `rev-parse` under `set -e` would have killed the script). Also sweep for already-stranded locks fleet-wide. Sibling: `git-lock-push.sh`'s `.git-lock-push.lock` IS conventionally gitignored — check every other flock'd helper for the same shape. Relates id:aa93 (dirty-guard), id:8c85 (the invisibility half). <!-- id:798b -->
- [x] [ROUTINE] 🔴 **HIGH — `RELAY_STATUS.md` renders only dispatched + config-excluded repos; EVERY other non-dispatch reason is dropped, so the pool reports a false clean and the owner cannot see why nothing ran** — the id:4347 no-silent-swallow anti-pattern, at the fleet's primary observability surface. Found 2026-08-10 investigating why run `relay-20260810-103858-20326` dispatched 2 units across 54 own repos. **Four distinct classes are silently invisible, all verified against the run's own `journal.jsonl` and status file:** **(a) dirty-deferred** — `puzzle-pwa`, `yinyang-puzzle`, `zkm-stt` were dropped pre-classification for uncommitted files (2 of them merely a stray `.changelog.lock`, id:798b) and appear in NO section; **(b) in-flight-suppressed** — `loderite` (the owner's main project, clean tree) produced no unit because another session was working it, and likewise appears nowhere; in-flight suppression is *substitutive* by design (`[[relay-orphan-existence-never-blocks]]`), but substitutive must still mean "surfaced with a reason", not "erased"; **(c) `human` verdict** — `zkWhale` WAS classified `human` ("Surface-only TODO backlog: 5 surface items, 0 promotable — lane-triage filing needed") and correctly not dispatched by the pool, but has **zero mentions** in the status file, so the routing signal to run `/relay human` never reaches anyone; **(d) HANDBACK** — the `dotclaude-skills` execute handback is in the Workflow's return value, yet the status file says `dispatched=2 / completed=1 / blocked=0` and `## Blocked / HANDBACKs _(none)_`. **(d) is a REGRESSION of an already-"fixed" bug**: `relay-loop.js:1961`'s id:4f9b comment names this exact false clean ("on 2026-08-01, as `## Blocked / HANDBACKs _(none)_` — a false clean") as motivation, and it recurred on 2026-08-10. ~~Note the status file's last write was `10:49:00` while the child died ~`10:54` — so the likely mechanism is that the run ended before the debounced `scheduleStatusWrite` flushed, i.e. **the final write is not forced at run end**.~~ — **THAT HYPOTHESIS IS FALSE; corrected 2026-08-10 by the handoff pass and re-verified in code.** `relay-loop.js:3020` is `await statusTail`, which DOES force the debounced flush before the run returns. **The real mechanism is a snapshot field omission.** `snapshotState()` (`:407-418`) copies `blocked` — which nothing writes — and copies **neither `surfaced` NOR `handbacks`**, both of which `buildRelayStatus` reads (`:291`, `:329`) and `writeRelayStatus` reads (`:375`). Since **id:cb50** routed every status write through that snapshot to move it off the critical path, every write has rendered `## Blocked / HANDBACKs _(none)_` and `blocked=0` ever since. **That single omission explains three of the four classes**: dirty-deferred (`classify-verdict.sh:151` → `discover-repo.sh:135` → `surfaced`), in-flight-suppressed (`discover-repo.sh:94-101`, substitutive → `surfaced`), and HANDBACK (`state.handbacks`). The **fourth** class is separate: `human`-verdict units are pulled from `actionable` (`:~1741`) and added to neither `state.queued` nor `state.skipped` — and the comment at `:1823-1825` *claims* they surface in Skipped while no code does it, which is its own instance of this repo's "a claim about CODE behaviour is a derived doc too" rule. Note the shape: a pure **performance** optimisation (id:cb50, getting the write off the critical path) silently dropped two fields and blinded the fleet's primary observability surface — nothing failed, the report just quietly went empty. **Acceptance**: every own repo appears in exactly one section of `RELAY_STATUS.md` every round with a reason — a mechanical `own_count == sum(sections)` invariant asserted in a test, so a repo can never again fall out of the accounting. Also fix the Claims renderer, which printed `-   mode=handoff run=…` with an EMPTY repo field for the loderite claim (it reads `repo`, which is `""` for a keyed claim; use `key`). Relates id:798b, id:61fa (death-cause discarded), id:1432. <!-- id:8c85 -->

Three coupled gaps: **(1)** `id:c17d` kept `schema_version` at `1.0.0` — defensible by SCHEMA.md §5's letter, since nothing was removed or newly required — but the schema **replaced `verdict`'s documented value space** (3 of the old 5 values moved to the new `board_column`) under an unchanged version, and with no `enum` keyword no validator catches it, so a consumer written against 1.0.0 is **silently wrong**. The ratchet actively creates the blind spot by pushing every repo into the spelling these tests don't recognize. FIX: canonical capability-keyed tags on the emit side (`[INPUT — decision]`), extended matchers on the read side (bare `[HARD]`, retaining `[HARD — pool]` for the migration window), and a fixture per lane spelling on BOTH sides so the next vocabulary move fails loudly. The pre-commit hook was already CORRECT — the emitter was wrong, which inverts the obvious reading of routed:8858. Anti-recurrence: a cross-check asserts every marker in `hard-lanes.md` appears in the scanner's tag list, so the next vocabulary move fails loudly there. Staged-write independence fixed in `md-merge.py` (rolls back BOTH working-tree text and index entry from pre-write snapshots taken under the flock, exit 3 — no stash/checkout/reset), and `durableHandbackFollowup` now parses the exit code it used to throw away. **Behaviour change worth knowing:** the canonical `[INPUT — decision]` gate tag buckets as `human_decision` in `gather-human-backlog.sh`, where `[HARD — decision gate]` bucketed as `hard_meeting` — auto-gated size-outs now appear on the human-decision list, not the meeting list. - [x] [HARD — hands] **Unblock the Plane proxy: the rootless container stack cannot create new firewall chains on the pilot host** (blocks `id:a532`'s Plane half; Vikunja is unaffected and live). Empirically isolated 2026-08-10 — publishing a port on the *default* container network SUCCEEDS, while both the quadlet-created network AND a freshly CLI-created bridge FAIL; the documented `firewall_driver` override is IGNORED (identical error either way), so it was reverted rather than left as a phantom fix. No container on this host had ever published a port before, so this is newly-exposed, not a regression. **PRIMARY (b): treat as a host networking defect.** Cheapest probe FIRST: reboot, then re-run the one-line create-network-and-publish-a-port discriminator — if the port binds, the chain table simply needed a privileged rebuild and starting the proxy finishes the deployment. If it still fails, capture the ruleset as root plus the debug log and treat it as an upstream report. **FALLBACK (a), only if (b) is exhausted:** move all the stack's units to the default network — known to work today, at the cost of network isolation from unrelated containers; that isolation loss is why it is the fallback and not the first move. Contract: the proxy serves HTTP 200 locally, and the result — including a negative — is recorded on `id:a532`. **Exact versions, ports and paths are in the private memory `tracker-pilot-host-topology`, not here.** **RESOLVED 2026-08-10 by REBOOT (primary path (b) — the isolation-losing fallback (a) was NOT needed).** Post-reboot the discriminator BINDS: a freshly created network publishes a port successfully, so the chain table simply needed a privileged rebuild, exactly as (b) predicted. Full stack brought up in dependency order; the proxy is active and serves **HTTP 200** locally. This unblocks `id:90f2`'s Plane half, which could not be verified while nothing bound. <!-- children-of:a532 --> <!-- id:02f7 --> <!-- gated-on:a532 -->
- [x] [ROUTINE] 🔴 **The INTEGRATE checkpoint and a real REVIEW pass share the label `reviewer (<model>)`, so integrating your own executors' work silently marks it AUDITED** — found 2026-07-31 while trying to spawn a review of that same session's work. **The mechanism:** `relay/SKILL.md`'s integrate step (invariant 5) prescribes `ckpt-tag.sh -l "reviewer (<model>)"`, and `ckpt-tag.sh:104` treats any label carrying a full `claude-*` id as a STRONG audit, syncing `last_strong_ckpt`. `gather-repo-state.sh:~298` then anchors the audit window on that watermark and only advances it for `reviewer*`/`strong-execute*` labels. So an INTEGRATE — which verifies `contract_met` and merges, but performs **no** test-integrity audit, no spec-drift check, no roadmap re-derivation — advances the strong-audit watermark exactly as a genuine review would. **Observed consequence, this session:** three integrate checkpoints (`relay-ckpt-20260731-1403/1519/1613`) were written while integrating five executor units; `last_strong_ckpt..HEAD` was then **0 commits**, so a `/relay review` spawned immediately after would have audited an EMPTY window and reported clean. The true unreviewed window was **42 substantive commits** back to `relay-ckpt-20260730-2018`. **Why this is the anti-gaming hole and not a cosmetic label bug:** review exists to catch an executor that gamed a test; the integrator is frequently the same session that dispatched those executors, so self-integration marking work audited removes the only independent check. Note yesterday's `relay-ckpt-20260730-2018` was labelled `reviewer (claude-opus-5, manual-integrate)` — someone already felt the need to disambiguate by hand, informally. **What to build:** a DISTINCT label for integration (e.g. `integrate (<model>)`) that `ckpt-tag.sh` does NOT treat as a strong audit, so only a real review pass advances `last_strong_ckpt`; update `SKILL.md` invariant 5 and `references/review.md` accordingly; and consider having `/relay review` LOUDLY refuse (not silently pass) when its computed window is empty, since a vacuous clean report is worse than none. **Relates**: `id:1a34` (the sibling label-format defect, fixed today), `id:c500` (reconcile deliberately non-strong — the same axis, already ratified), `id:d147`/`id:907e`/`id:8123` (the starvation cluster this was found while auditing). <!-- id:ecce -->

(A field-vocabulary scan for a `**Files**`/`**Touches**` heading returned 0 and was the wrong instrument — owner-corrected in-meeting.) No new authored field for now; promote to an authored `**Touches**` field only if the miss rate justifies it. **Hardening (`--fabled` F3):** empty extraction ⇒ **run-alone**, never greenlight-all (an empty set is disjoint from everything — the maximal under-extraction). Log BOTH the under-extraction rate (extracted ⊂ actual merged diff, computable from `drain-integrate.sh`'s merge-check) and the **false-serialization rate** (declared sets intersect but the actual diffs are disjoint) — `Context` paths are often citations rather than touches, so over-extraction would silently destroy the throughput win, and a subset-only metric measures half the evidence. **Recorded design rule (D3a):** the greenlight is a THROUGHPUT OPTIMIZER; the serialized integrator is the SAFETY NET — never relax integrate checks "because greenlight already proved disjointness". Contract: a test over fixture items from this repo and loderite; both metrics emitted. <!-- children-of:1f4f --> <!-- id:b099 -->
- [x] [ROUTINE] **Lint: flag `(DEP: <id>)` prose gate-annotations that lack a typed `<!-- gated-on:id -->` marker** — OBSERVED 2026-07-24 (run relay-20260724-160054-19815): zelegator's two open `[ROUTINE]` items (id:df0f, id:c106) gate on `(DEP: 0cd5)` **prose**, which `classify-repo.sh` correctly does NOT read as a gate (the id:65f5/4da4/0d58 settled rule: NEVER gate on a prose substring — only typed `<!-- gated-on:XXXX -->` edges are honored), so both were mis-dispatched as `execute` and handed back with zero actionable work. - [x] [ROUTINE] **`personas.md` has accreted 27 redundant entries (79 entries / 52 unique names) because `merge=union` can never reconcile a re-registration** — found 2026-07-31 while auditing the registry after a live collision (meeting `docs/meeting-notes/2026-07-31-1231-execute-review-cadence-starvation.md`). **Root cause is structural, not sloppiness**: `.gitattributes:1` sets `meeting/personas.md merge=union`, so when two sessions append the same persona — or one session re-registers an existing one with enriched wording — union merge keeps BOTH sides forever. 18 names are duplicated; **Sage** and **Otto** appear 3× each, **Cal** is an exact byte-duplicate. **The reassuring half, verified before filing: NO name means two different things** — every duplicate is a same-lens re-registration with progressively richer wording (same emoji, same core lens), so nothing is semantically ambiguous today. **The hazard is length, and it has already bitten**: at 79 entries the file is long enough that a partial read misses names, which is exactly how this session introduced a "new" 🧭 persona under the name **Quinn** — already registered since 2026-05-21 as the inference-server-internals lens — and had to rename mid-meeting to Wren. A registry whose duplicates are harmless can still cause a harmful collision by being too long to read. **What to build**: (1) a dedup pass keeping the RICHEST entry per name (merging the "extended <date> (<project>/<slug>)" provenance tails rather than dropping them — the enrichment history is the useful part); (2) decide whether `merge=union` is still right for this file *given that it cannot dedup* — the alternative is a normal merge plus an `append.sh`-side "name already registered, extending instead" path, which is where the reconciliation actually belongs (`append.sh -t personas` is already the sole sanctioned writer, so it can own this); (3) a conformance check that fails when a name appears twice. **Out of scope**: changing the registry's format, or auto-merging lenses that genuinely differ (none exist today — if one ever does, it must be a loud reject, not a silent merge). Contract: `grep -oE '\*\*[A-Za-z]+\*\*' personas.md | sort | uniq -d` is empty, and re-registering an existing name via `append.sh` extends rather than duplicates. <!-- id:069b -->
- [x] [ROUTINE] 🔴 **(DISSOLUTION — removes the reason AND the means) Parent creates + provisions the worktree before dispatch; the child is never told the main-checkout path** — needs no API support, so it is buildable today (this is the salvageable half of FIX A). Three parts: **(1)** move `git -C <repo> worktree add <wt> -b <branch> HEAD` from the child's first action into a pre-dispatch step in `relay-loop.js`, so the child receives a ready worktree and has no reason to reference `<repo>` at all; **(2)** provision the gitignored build artifacts the child would otherwise go to main for — symlink/reuse `node_modules`, `.venv`, caches, downloaded browser binaries — which children are already doing by hand (loderite `RELAY_LOG.md:2681`, and `:3022`/`:3422`/`:3486` show them dutifully removing the symlink before commit); **(3)** drop `main checkout: ${unit.path}` from the child prompt (`:1862`) once (1) and (2) land — until then it must stay, since a child that still creates its own worktree needs it. **Ordering matters: (3) is gated on (1)+(2)** — removing the path first would break exactly the repos that legitimately reuse main's artifacts (owner's objection, 2026-07-30). Acceptance: a dispatched child's prompt contains no main-checkout path; the worktree it receives already has the artifacts needed to build/test; `git worktree list` shows the parent as creator. — **NOT gated on id:d464 (edge removed 2026-07-30): this is a prompt + dispatch-plumbing change with NO hook and NO permission surface, so the afk-prompt hazard that put d464 into discussion does not touch it.** That makes this the only child of f91a that is safe to build as specified — but it is **also NOT queued** pending the owner's call, since the two were filed as one approach and the ordering is now his to set. Note it is dissolution, not enforcement: it removes the child's *reason* to reach into main and stops handing it the path, but a child that constructs the path anyway is still unopposed. <!-- children-of:f91a --> <!-- id:34b7 -->
- [x] [ROUTINE] **`open_hard_pool` counts `@container` epics, so a fully-decomposed repo draws a PHANTOM `hard` verdict forever** — reported 2026-07-31 by a HARD child that refused pre-start (run `relay-20260731-221039-22914`, loderite, worktree left completely clean, no ledger writes). `gather-repo-state.sh`'s `open_hard_pool` loop (~lines 407-421) counts open `- [ ] [HARD — pool]` lines lacking `🚧` / `"BLOCKED on"`, and `@container` lines pass that filter — so loderite's two container epics (id:16b2 portal-impl, id:ca44 portal-wiring, the latter explicitly "confirmed a MULTI-SESSION epic") are counted as dispatchable HARD leaves. Every descendant is either already `[x]`, `[HARD — decision gate]`, or a sequenced-gated `[ROUTINE]` seam — i.e. **there is no un-gated `[HARD — pool]` leaf for a HARD child to pick**, so the repo will keep drawing `hard`, dispatching an apex child, and handing back at zero work indefinitely. Note `ca44` also evades the block filter by writing `BLOCKED (b225…` — the glob only matches `BLOCKED on`, a second brittleness worth folding in. **Fix**: exclude `@container` from the `open_hard_pool` count — a container is never itself pool-dispatchable, its seams are the units. This is the SAME under-dispatch-safe marker direction `classify-repo.sh` already applies (id:0cf5 added `@container` to `is_human` there for exactly this reason) — the two counters simply disagree, which is the real defect. **Cost of leaving it**: an apex-tier Opus dispatch per round per affected repo, each returning nothing; it is also why prioritizing loderite (owner request, same evening) would have produced repeated no-op HARD dispatches rather than work. Recommendation is a delegated agent's, recorded as a recommendation — the fix is not ratified here. **ADOPTS `routed:d866`** — loderite filed the identical finding to the shared inbox from the same run; this line was written independently ~15 min earlier, so the two are merged here rather than filed twice (loderite's wording adds the source-verified line refs and that the run cost was a SIZE-OUT handback with zero commits). <!-- routed:d866 --> <!-- id:d808 -->
- [x] [ROUTINE] **`validate-flags.sh` does not understand `--flag=value`, so an explicit quota cap is SILENTLY LOOSENED to the default** — observed live 2026-07-31: the owner invoked `/relay --afk --quota-7d=90`; the guard printed *"unknown flag `--quota-7d=90` … warning and dropping it"* and emitted only `--afk`. `--quota-7d` IS in `known-flags-relay.tsv`, but the runtime guard only ever compares whole tokens — nowhere in the script is a token split on `=` (verified: `grep -n 'quota-7d' validate-flags.sh` → no hits; the arity-1 path consumes the *following* token only). Worse, the near-miss escalation cannot catch it either: `--quota-7d=90` is edit-distance **3** from `--quota-7d` (>2), and `--quota-7d` is in neither `MODE_FLAGS` nor `SCOPE_FLAGS`, so it warns-and-drops instead of escalating. **The hazard is directional**: dropping a cap always falls back to the *looser* `RELAY_QUOTA_THRESHOLD` default (0.90), so `--quota-7d=45` — a deliberate tightening — silently becomes 0.90 and the run burns twice the intended weekly budget with only a stderr line the operator may not read. Here it was harmless only by coincidence (90 == the default). **Fix**: split `--flag=value` into `(--flag, value)` BEFORE the known-flag lookup, for any arity-1 manifest flag; a `=` on an arity-0 flag stays an error. Consider also adding the quota flags to `SCOPE_FLAGS`-style escalation — a dropped budget cap is a scope inversion in the same sense `--except`/`--exclude` is. Contract a test verifies: `validate-flags.sh relay -- --afk --quota-7d=45` emits `--afk --quota-7d 45` and warns nothing. <!-- id:2047 -->
- [x] [ROUTINE] **A relay round that dispatches NOTHING leaves no trace of its verdict or reason — `relay-events.jsonl` records only `dispatch`/`integrate`/`handback`/`backstop`** — found 2026-07-30 when a live diagnosis became undecidable from the artifacts. `pushEvent` is called with exactly four kinds (`grep -oE "pushEvent\('[a-z]+'" relay/scripts/relay-loop.js`), all of which presuppose a unit EXISTS. Include the `cached` flag explicitly: the id:c3a6 discover-sig cache means a round may reuse last round's verdict without re-deriving it, and a reader currently cannot tell a fresh verdict from a replayed one. Keep it off the critical path like the other events (`relay-state-write.sh event-append`). **Distinct from** id:5749 (a BLOCKED agent leaves no on-disk artifact — that is about agent lifecycle) and from TODO's injected-unit-never-dispatched item (that is about `inject.sh take` specifically); this one is about the CLASSIFICATION decision going unrecorded for every repo, every round. Relates id:c8b6 (the observability artifacts), id:3e14 (the diagnosis this blocked), id:854c (the precedent — `backstop` events were added the same way). <!-- id:c7dc -->

F6 was right that the check had not been run and wrong to imply it would vindicate R1 — the check ran and confirms D1's original instinct. - [x] **`meeting/append.sh` personas extend was a whole-line REPLACE — silent data loss.** The `id:069b` branch printed the caller's line and `next`d past the old one, discarding all prior lens text while stderr said "extending" and it exited 0. FIXED 2026-08-13 (`89f6e1c`): `merge_persona_line()` makes the extend a lossless union (new-contains-old → take new; new-adds-nothing → keep old; else concatenate the new payload with its bullet/emoji/`**Name**` prefix stripped), with `--replace` as the explicit opt-in for a deliberate rewrite. Rationale: under `merge=union` a dropped fragment is unrecoverable while a duplicated phrase is cosmetic. **The report's own suggested guard — "reject a replacement shorter than the original" — was REJECTED as wrong and should not be re-proposed:** a reworded extension can legitimately be shorter, and an equal-or-longer line can still drop a clause, so the length heuristic both rejects valid writes and admits lossy ones. Verified against the ORIGINAL 2026-08-12 incident data (pre-loss Gil line from `624a7f8^` plus a reconstructed caller line), not only against the new test: pre-fix, `RELEASE-TAG PUSH SEMANTICS` count goes 1 → 0 and the line shrinks 970 → 809 bytes; post-fix both the old and new text survive in one entry. Test `tests/test_personas_extend_preserves_text_81b8.sh` confirmed non-vacuous (fails against pre-fix code). <!-- routed:81b8 -->
- [x] **`meeting/append.sh` personas extend DESTROYED the symlink — forked registry.** The same branch did `mktemp` + `mv -- "$tmp" "$dest"`, but `~/.claude/skills/meeting/personas.md` is a symlink into this repo, so the edit landed in a new detached regular file under `~/.claude`, the canonical file never received it, and the next reader silently saw a forked registry (also dropped 0644→0600 and crossed /tmp→home). FIXED 2026-08-13 (`89f6e1c`): write in place — truncation follows the symlink and preserves inode, link and mode, exactly like the plain-append `printf >> "$dest"` path below it that was always correct. This dissolves all three sub-harms rather than guarding them; there is no temp file left to place. **Sharper characterisation than the report gave:** under the real symlink topology the old code left the canonical file BYTE-IDENTICAL — it did not lose text there, it forked the registry, and the loss surfaced only when someone read the detached copy. That is why the incident was nearly invisible. Test `tests/test_personas_extend_follows_symlink_96da.sh` confirmed non-vacuous. <!-- routed:96da -->
- [x] [ROUTINE] **`meeting/append.sh`'s flock does NOT serialize two writers to `personas.md` — there are TWO lock files for one file.** `SKILL_DIR="$(cd "$(dirname "$0")" && pwd)"` (`append.sh:39`) resolves to the *invocation* directory, so `dest="$SKILL_DIR/personas.md"` (`:337`) and every write path locks on `"${dest}.lock"` (`:392`, `:521`, `:531`). Invoked through the install (`~/.claude/skills/meeting/append.sh`, itself a symlink) that is `~/.claude/skills/meeting/personas.md.lock`; invoked canonically it is `~/src/dotclaude-skills/meeting/personas.md.lock`. `personas.md` is a symlink so both `dest` paths resolve to ONE underlying file, but the two locks are independent — an install-path writer and a repo-path writer are not mutually excluded. **Verified on disk 2026-08-13**: both lock files exist, `~/.claude/…` dated 12. Aug 13:37 (the incident session) and `~/src/…` dated 13. Aug 15:26. Neither the `routed:81b8` nor the `routed:96da` report mentions this, and neither fix addresses it — `personas.md` is `merge=union`, so an interleaved write is exactly the unrecoverable case. Note the plain-append path has the same blindness (it creates a stray `.lock` beside the symlink). Contract: one lock per underlying file regardless of invocation path — resolve `dest` with `readlink -f` before deriving the lock path, or pin the lock to the canonical checkout. Needs a decision on which path is canonical for locking. Found while verifying the `81b8`/`96da` fixes. **✅ FIXED 2026-08-13 (`10fa7c7`) — `lock_path_for()` canonicalises with `readlink -f` before appending `.lock`, so one underlying file has one lock regardless of invocation path; applied to all write paths incl. `inbox-done`'s `${inbox}.lock`, a fourth site the report missed.** <!-- id:244f -->
- [x] [ROUTINE] **`test_provision_attempt_persists_315c.sh` guards the fix's SHAPE, not its behaviour — it passes against code where the fix is functionally disabled.** Sections 1–4 are greps over `relay-loop.js` source; section 5, labelled BEHAVIOURAL, writes a *copy* of the fix into a `sim.js` and runs THAT — the production `provisionWorktree` is never executed. **Mutation-tested 2026-08-13:** on the post-fix tree, neutering the seed while keeping every identifier the greps look for (`if (false && seeded > (unit.attempt || 0)) unit.attempt = seeded`) still yields `ALL PASS, EXIT=0`, including the assertion that claims to prove non-vacuity. So the suite currently gives **zero** assurance that `id:315c`'s watermark works in production — the fix reads correct on inspection, but it is the verification that is weak. This is the "same agent wrote code and test" hazard landing on the self-mirroring-simulation pattern specifically: a simulation authored from the implementation can only ever restate it. Contract: exercise the real `provisionWorktree` (extract it so it is importable, or drive it through a harness), or failing that make the assertions mutation-resistant — a test that survives `if (false && …)` is not testing the branch. Applies to any other `sim.js`-style test in the suite; worth a grep for the pattern while fixing. <!-- children-of:315c --> **✅ FIXED 2026-08-13 (`d2f645d`) — section 5 now extracts the REAL `provisionWorktree` body and helpers verbatim from `relay-loop.js` and evals them in a `vm` context; extraction failure exits 2. **PROVEN side-by-side on one mutated tree** (the finding's exact neutering): the OLD test PASSES, the NEW test FAILS reproducing the incident round-for-round. The retyped copy had also DRIFTED from production, a second reason it could never detect a regression.** <!-- id:3a50 -->
- [x] [ROUTINE] **`ef43739`'s "one definition, no second parser" claim is FALSE — a third copy of the parked-heading predicate survives in Python.** The commit extracted the bash predicate into `relay/scripts/lib-roadmap-sections.sh` and its test asserts *exactly one* `is_exempt_heading`, but that assertion greps for the **bash function name** only, so it is blind to `relay/scripts/classify-repo.sh:155`: `_EXEMPT_HEADING_RE = re.compile(r"(gated|deferred|done|icebox|archive|parked)", re.IGNORECASE)` (`id:356f`). Functionally equivalent today, so nothing is broken — but it is a live drift vector, and **a small divergence already exists**: the new lib's caller matches `#{1,6}` (so it includes H1) while `classify-repo.sh` matches `##+\s` only, meaning a top-level `# Gated` heading is exempt to one reader and not the other. Contract: either have the Python read the bash lib's vocabulary (single source), or document the duplication explicitly at both sites AND widen the test's one-definition assertion to catch a Python copy too — a "one definition" test that cannot see the second definition is the loud-detection-that-checks-nothing shape (`id:cbd2`). Reconcile the `#{1,6}` vs `##+` divergence either way. <!-- children-of:4b8f --> **✅ FIXED 2026-08-13 (`81c91a5`) — **the finding was partly wrong**: there were THREE recognizers, not a two-way split, and `roadmap-lint.sh` (also a lib caller) agreed with the Python side. Two divergences it missed: only `gather` tolerated leading whitespace, and python `\s` matches `\n`. The lib now owns the vocabulary + both patterns; `classify-repo.sh` sources it and reads them from the environment, KeyError-loud with no fallback. Guard detects a copy in ANY language and proves single-SOURCE consumption behaviourally.** <!-- id:bb32 -->
- [x] [ROUTINE] **`89f6e1c` traded ATOMICITY for symlink-following — the personas write is no longer atomic, and combined with `id:244f`'s two lock files a loser can now observe a TRUNCATED registry.** The old path was `mktemp` + `mv -- "$tmp" "$dest"` (an atomic rename); the fix replaced it with an in-place `Path.write_text()`, which is open-for-truncate-then-write. That change was *correct and necessary* — truncation is what follows the symlink, and it dissolves the fork defect rather than guarding it — but it silently moved the failure mode: previously a racing reader saw a stale-but-WHOLE file, now it can see a partially-written one. **This is strictly worse than `id:244f` alone and is not covered by it**: `id:244f` records that two writers are not mutually excluded; this records that an unexcluded write is now destructive rather than merely lossy. The two are one hazard and should be fixed together — resolving `dest` with `readlink -f` before deriving the lock path (`id:244f`'s contract) closes both, because a single lock makes non-atomicity unobservable. If that fix slips, restore atomicity independently by writing to a temp file **in the same directory as the symlink TARGET** and `mv`-ing onto the resolved path (never onto the symlink itself). Found by independent re-derivation during the 2026-08-13 `/relay review`; the divergent lock files were observed on disk, a torn read was NOT provoked (no race was attempted), so the hazard is demonstrated-by-construction, not by incident. <!-- gated-on:244f --> **✅ FIXED 2026-08-13 (`10fa7c7`) — realpath + `mkstemp` in the resolved target's OWN directory + write/fsync + chmod to the existing mode + `os.replace()`: atomic AND symlink-following, same filesystem, no /tmp→home hop. **Corrects this item's own text**: a single lock would NOT have made non-atomicity unobservable — only WRITERS take the flock, every reader takes none, so atomicity was independently necessary.** <!-- id:00b1 -->

Contract: anchor the match to the bolded-name position at the START of the entry (the `**Name**` prefix the merge already parses to strip its tail), not a bare substring, and add a fixture with a cross-referencing persona line. **✅ FIXED 2026-08-13 (`10fa7c7`) — definition-anchored targeting on BOTH halves; the report described only the Python side, but the bash `grep -qF -- "**Name**"` gate had the same blindness and decided whether the extend branch ran at all.** <!-- id:44c5 -->

- [x] [ROUTINE] **`roadmap-lint.sh`'s DECIDED-LEFT-OPEN scoping exemption only strips the ADJACENT form, so prose scoping a terminal word by a NOUN PHRASE false-positives.** `3f983f4`'s gate-re-target prose on `id:d4ca`/`id:e405` says that both of their gate TARGETS are ticked-and-archived, spelling the ticked state with the bare uppercase terminal word (the one in `STATE_CLAIM_TERMINAL_WORDS` that pairs with `[x]`) — a word describing the items' GATE TARGETS, not the items themselves — and both now fire DECIDED-LEFT-OPEN (they did not before that commit; confirmed by running the lint against `3f983f4^`'s ledger). Cause: `lib-state-claim.sh:state_claim_direction_i` strips only `id:XXXX (is )?<TERMINAL_WORD>`, so a terminal word not immediately preceded by an id token reads as a self-claim. **Isolated to the exact lexeme** with a 3-line synthetic ROADMAP: the terminal-word phrasing fires, the `GATE RE-TARGETED 2026-08-13` phrasing does not. Effect is lint NOISE only — the items are correctly still open and correctly still gated, and `--strict` was already non-zero from a pre-existing DECOMPOSED-CONTAINER, so nothing flipped green→red. Contract: either widen the strip to cover a scoping noun phrase (of the shape *"&lt;its targets/deps&gt; are &lt;terminal-word&gt;"*, where the terminal word is scoped by a preceding noun phrase rather than by an adjacent `id:` token) — noting an over-wide strip would blind the rule to genuine self-claims, so prefer a narrow, tested widening — or reword the two items' prose to avoid the bare terminal word. Prefer fixing the engine: this phrasing is natural and will recur. **✅ FIXED 2026-08-13 (`81c91a5`) — fixed in `lib-state-claim.sh`, the real engine (fleet-wide: `roadmap-lint.sh`, `todo-conformance.sh`, `discover-repo.sh` all consume it). A closed pointer-noun list + copula, unable to reach across sentence punctuation. Both directions verified independently on a fresh fixture: a genuine self-claim fires, a scoped one does not, and an adversarial line carrying BOTH still fires. Over the real `ROADMAP.md` it removes exactly `id:d4ca`/`id:e405` and nothing else.** <!-- id:c8e5 -->
- [x] [ROUTINE] **`tests/test_reconcile_stranded_2b4b.sh` asserts the liveness gate by GREPPING THE SOURCE, so it stayed green through the entire life of `id:b99f`.** Line 70 is `grep -q 'live-runs' "$SCRIPT"`, and its own comment concedes the shape: *"FAIL-SAFE: a branch whose run IS alive must not be reported as stranded. **Simulated by asserting the code consults heartbeat live-runs** rather than assuming every run is dead."* Consulting the registry and consulting it CORRECTLY are different claims, and only the first is tested — the string `live-runs` was present the whole time the whole-line `grep -qxF` match made the gate unreachable. **Proven, not argued (2026-08-13):** run against pre-fix `relay-reconcile.sh`, this test PASSES while the two new behavioural tests both FAIL. Same family as `id:3a50` (`test_provision_attempt_persists_315c.sh` mutation-tests its own `sim.js` instead of `relay-loop.js`) but milder — this one does exercise the real script for its other assertions. Fix: replace the source grep with the behavioural assertion now carried by `tests/test_reconcile_stranded_liveness_b99f.sh`, or delete the line as superseded by it — do not leave both, since a source-grep sitting next to a real test reads as extra coverage while providing none. **Worth a wider sweep:** these are two instances in one window of a test asserting a fix's SHAPE rather than its BEHAVIOUR, both of which would have stayed green through a full revert. Consider a lint for `grep -q '<literal>' "$SCRIPT"`-style assertions in `tests/`. **✅ FIXED 2026-08-13 (`d2f645d`) — the source grep is removed, pointing at `tests/test_reconcile_stranded_liveness_b99f.sh` which now carries the behavioural coverage. The file's remaining assertions were genuinely load-bearing: it still FAILS against a pre-`id:2b4b` `relay-reconcile.sh`.** <!-- id:05a2 -->
- [x] **URGENT — `orphan-scan.sh --shipped` shell-executed EVERY discovered test regardless of language, writing desktop SCREEN CAPTURES into repos. FIXED 2026-08-13.** `meeting/orphan-scan.sh:404` ran `timeout ${timeout_s}s bash "$test_rel"` for every `# roadmap:`-tagged test it found, so in a Python repo it shell-executed `tests/test_*.py` line by line — `import csv` resolves to `/usr/bin/import` (ImageMagick), which writes a multi-MB PostScript **screen capture of the desktop** into the repo root and blocks on a mouse click. `>/dev/null 2>&1` swallowed every trace (`id:4347`). **CONFIRMED live 2026-08-13 16:41–16:42 in `linguistic-universals`: 6 captures, 36 MB, and it blocked the relay pool via the dirty-tree guard.** Blast radius: 23 own repos carry non-`.sh` `# roadmap:` tests and `/todo-update` runs this scan after **every** prompt. **Fix**: dispatch on language — `bash` only for `.sh`/`.bash` or an sh shebang, `pytest` for `.py`/python shebang when available, and **SKIP anything unidentified** rather than guessing; skips are counted and surfaced on stderr naming each file, never silent. The run's combined output now goes to `$ORPHAN_SCAN_LOG` (default `~/.claude/logs/orphan-scan.log`) instead of `/dev/null`, so a runner fault is inspectable; only the exit status decides TICK-READY. **The report understated it — this was also a CORRECTNESS bug, not only a side-effect hazard**: reproduced pre-fix, the unidentifiable test was executed AND **falsely claimed TICK-READY**, because `import` exits 0 so the "test" read green. A false TICK-READY on a scan that runs after every prompt is a direct route to ticking un-done work. **Containment verified**: no capture was ever committed in `linguistic-universals` (no `.ps` blob in history, pack 19.56 MiB), and a content-typed sweep of `~/src` found no stray PostScript beyond two legitimate 2009 academic files in `diploma-thesis`. Regression test `tests/test_orphan_scan_language_dispatch_15f3.sh` reproduces the mechanism with a PATH shim standing in for ImageMagick and is confirmed non-vacuous — **4 of its 5 assertions fail against pre-fix code** (2 shim invocations, a stray file literally named `csv` in the repo root, no pytest dispatch, and the false TICK-READY), all pass after. The fix is live fleet-wide immediately via the `~/.claude/skills/meeting/` symlink. (from it-infra, investigation of `routed:a2bb`) <!-- routed:15f3 -->
